# Decisions
- chore(python): pin nanocode 4b6fa30e3 (2026-11-17, subproc lane). python-revision.json moves from fbfc5eaa5 to 4b6fa30e3, which adds 4b6fa30e3 (subprocess honors timeout= through the shell's timeout command and raises TimeoutExpired; sys.executable is /usr/bin/python3, so [sys.executable, '-c', ...] runs python3 in the shell) and nanocode master's commits since fbfc5eaa5. python/Cargo.lock gains djs-libm under nanocode-python-capi, which nanocode master now needs (the build stopped on --locked without it). Checked by building dist with NANOCODE_PYTHON_SOURCE at this commit's tree: bench/python/subprocess.ts 40/40, a timeout probe (list, shell=True and sys.executable forms) and CAPI=... bench/python/score.ts at packages 8/18, numpy 14/14, matplotlib 6/6, pillow 6/6, limits 4/4.
- feat(python): subprocess, os.system and os.popen run their command through the hosting shell (2026-11-17, subproc lane). Why: on the live Vercel benchmark (build-29a51dc), 4 of 4 runs of 'Clone wevm/viem and chart its largest source files with Python' failed a cell on subprocess.run(['git', 'ls-files'], capture_output=True, text=True) with 'this Python has no processes to run a command in'. Python's host_spawn only ran psh synchronously (Psh.runNow), with an empty command map, so the shell's TypeScript commands (git, python3 itself, anything a host adds) were missing, and a shell without psh had no spawn at all. What: pythonCommands takes a Host (Bash.ts builds it from the shell's own commands, Psh.run or the TypeScript runner, with the same cwd, env, limits and filesystem). A command psh can run on its own still runs at once. Any other command stops Python, rolls back what Python changed since its last command, runs the command asynchronously in the shell, and then runs the script again from the start: earlier commands' results and file effects are replayed in order, and the run reaches the new command with its answer ready. The replay is deterministic because every replay starts on a fresh interpreter instance whose host_random, host_time and host_now values are recorded and played back (a reused instance keeps its RNG and hash seed in memory). A script that still asks for a different command at the same point twice gets exit 126 with a message saying why, instead of looping. The undo log is src/bash/internal/commands/journal.ts: before a path's first change it snapshots the path's subtree, bounded by maxMemoryBytes (ENOMEM past that). On an async filesystem (Cloudflare, SqlFileSystem) the command reads through the layered cache and fetches missed paths before retrying the operation. git ls-files now honors pathspecs (src, '*.ts', 'src/*.md'). Cost: each host command reruns the script so far on a fresh instance. Measured 10 calls in 1.4 s and 100 calls in 9.1 s (git log per file, numpy imported), where the shared-instance replay took 4.5 s for 300. Checked: bench/python/subprocess.ts, 10 probes on sync and async filesystems, with and without psh (40/40): git ls-files, check_output with shell=True and a quoted list, CalledProcessError, os.system statuses (512 for exit 2), os.popen and close(), input=, cwd=, env=, Popen().communicate(), getstatusoutput, writes visible both ways, FileNotFoundError, and the viem chart with matplotlib. Iterating p.stdout, a poll() loop, stdin=PIPE and a two-Popen pipeline also work, because a Popen runs to completion first. CAPI=... bench/python/score.ts is unchanged (packages 7/18, numpy 14/14, matplotlib 6/6, pillow 6/6, limits 4/4). npm run check:types passes. Unsupported: streaming or interactive children (output arrives only when the command ends; writing to stdin after reading output), signals, real pids. Side effects outside the filesystem (network commands) happen once per command, but Python's own code before the command runs again on each replay.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode f85645b6a (2026-11-17, demo annoyances swarm). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 76a97ad63 to f85645b6a, which adds f85645b6a (Code Mode's image() also takes an image file's path and reads it through the host's image reader, deduped with the copy the cell shows on its own) and nanocode master's commits since. On the deployed Vercel demo, the viem chart chip failed a cell in 4 of 4 Claude-skin runs because Haiku wrote image("/workspace/<chart>.png"); with the fix, a local Haiku harness showed 0 failed image() cells over 17 runs. Validated through `node scripts/release.mjs` on gak-9.
- feat(python): python.wasm ships its warmed memory as `dist/python.data` instead of data segments, so a Cloudflare Worker with matplotlib fits under the 64 MiB limit (2026-11-17). Why: since matplotlib was frozen in (fbfc5ea), `wrangler deploy` failed with "Your Worker exceeded the uncompressed size limit of 63.9 MiB [code 10027]": python.wasm was 50,530,681 bytes, of which 30.5 MB was the data section (the frozen stdlib and site packages, mpl-data, and the baked heap) and 19.9 MB code. Workers cannot compile WebAssembly from fetched bytes, but plain data can come from Workers Static Assets, which do not count. `bake(bytes, { image: true })` (scripts/python.mjs) drops the data and data count sections and returns python.data: gzip of `NCPYMEM1`, the SHA-256 of the memory from the first to the last non-zero byte, its base and length, then those bytes; the module keeps the same 40 bytes in a `nanocode.python.image` custom section, so a python.data from another bake is refused (each bake differs in a few bytes of the heap). `RustPython.provide` checks and unpacks it once per module (DecompressionStream) and `instantiate` copies it into each new instance's memory, which is what the data segments did. `Python.Options.data` supplies it (bytes, a promise or a loader called once per isolate; a failed load retries on the next run); by default Node reads `nanocode-ts/python.data` beside python.wasm, and the Cloudflare app's default loader fetches `/nanocode-ts/python.data` from `env.ASSETS` (or `workspace.pythonData(env)`). Without it python3 exits 126 with `python3 needs nanocode-ts's python.data beside python.wasm`. It is gzip because the image is 35.4 MB and a static asset may hold 25 MiB. Measured (`node scripts/build.mjs`, binaryen 133, this pin): python.wasm 50,530,681 to 20,005,791 bytes, python.data 18,994,179 (35,443,681 unpacked); durable-cloudflare `wrangler deploy --dry-run` modules 71.4 to 42.2 MiB (python 20.0, nanocode_wasm 13.8, psh 6.6, interpreter 1.2, index.js 2.7). The module is already `-Oz` with no name or debug sections, so stripping saves nothing; dropping fonts would only shrink python.data. Checked: `CAPI=... bench/python/score.ts` unchanged (packages 7/18, numpy 14/14, plots matplotlib 6/6, pillow 6/6, contourpy 2/2, kiwisolver 1/1, limits 4/4); Node `python3 -c 'import matplotlib.pyplot as plt; plt.plot([1,2]); plt.savefig("a.png"); plt.show()'` writes a.png and Figure_1.png (18,216 bytes) in 1.5 s with the default loaders; a wrong or missing python.data gives the 126 message; durable-cloudflare's dry-run bundle under Miniflare with its assets directory runs the same plot through a scripted turn's `tools.Bash` (exit 0, a.png 18,216 bytes); `npm run check:types` passes.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode 76a97ad63 (2026-11-17, demo annoyances swarm). `sdk-revision.json` moves from fbfc5eaa5 to 76a97ad63, which adds 76a97ad63 (Code Mode shows a chart whose path only a nested call's output names, keeps shown-image hashes across cells, and the exec description says to run the script and savefig) and c928a0c69 (matplotlib extensions built with its Agg bug-fix defines). Real Haiku turns on a prompt that saves a chart under a runtime-computed name showed the image in 1 of 6 runs before and 6 of 6 after; the plain sine and viem chart prompts stayed at 5/5 with no duplicates. Validated through `node scripts/release.mjs` on gak-9.
- fix(wasm): cell `WebAssembly` takes WebAssembly text and the result shapes models write, and the `wasm` command takes WAT files (2026-11-17, demo annoyances swarm, WebAssembly chip lane). Why: on build-1384947, 48 live runs of the `Run a WebAssembly add function` chip across the six demo sites (Playwright bench with each run's streamed events saved) left 4 runs with a failed cell, each a pattern the runtime refused: `WebAssembly.instantiate(await bash.readFile("add.wasm"))` got the file as a 41-character string; `const { instance } = await WebAssembly.instantiate(await WebAssembly.compile(bytes))` read `exports` of undefined; `WebAssembly.instantiate(wasm, {})` passed the helper object; and a ChatGPT run, told nothing about text modules, decided it needed a WAT compiler and reached for paid compute. What: `WebAssembly.compile`, `instantiate`, `Module`, `validate` and the `wasm` helpers take WAT text (as a string or its UTF-8 bytes), assembled by the shell's `wat2wasm` through a new `assemble` host op, and a binary module read as text when no byte was lost; `instantiate` of a `Module` still resolves to the `Instance`, which also answers `instance` and `module`, and the `{ module, instance }` result also answers a non-enumerable `exports`; `CompileError`, `LinkError` and `RuntimeError` exist and a non-module throws `CompileError`, where `compile` used to accept anything; errors name what was passed (`got an object (Object) with keys run, invoke, instantiate`, the first 8 bytes in hex) and what is accepted; a binary that fails to compile adds that WAT is accepted in its place; `wasm.run` with non-array args points at `wasm.invoke`. The shell `wasm` command runs `.wat` files. The globals sentence now says `WebAssembly` is the standard API taking WAT or a path in place of bytes and gives the `wasm` helpers' signatures: the first wording, which said `wasm` and `WebAssembly` take WAT, made Haiku call `wasm(wat)` as a function (21 of 24 runs had a failed cell). Measured with `output/wasmchip.ts` (an in-process `AgentRuntime` with `node:sqlite`, one runtime per session) on `cargo build --locked --offline --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm -j 2` against nanocode 101176e2b as `../nanocodex`: 19 scripted cells replaying the captured patterns and their WAT variants, 8 of the first 16 failing before (the build-1384947 dist), all passing after. Real Haiku 5.5 on the chip prompt: before 12 runs, 13 cells, 1 failed (a hand-encoded section size), median 6.90 s, p90 8.45 s; after the reworded sentence but before the `exports` alias, 24 runs, 3 failed cells, all `(await WebAssembly.instantiate(wat)).exports`; after, 24 runs, 24 cells, 0 failed, every run writing WAT, medians 3.56 s and 3.42 s, p90 4.54 s and 4.24 s. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `npm run check` (only these files kept), `git diff --check`, `cargo clippy --offline --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings`. Files: src/wasm/prelude/extra.js, src/bash/internal/commands/wasm.ts, src/wasm/cells.rs.
- chore(python): python.wasm pins nanocode fbfc5ea (python-revision.json and sdk-revision.json), which freezes matplotlib 3.11.2, Pillow, contourpy and kiwisolver with their pure-Python dependencies and mpl-data, so `import matplotlib.pyplot` runs on RustPython: nanocode's `_missing` no longer reports those packages, and the Pyodide route is not taken for them (2026-11-17). python3's default matplotlib backend writes `Figure_<n>.png` to the working directory on `plt.show()` and says so on stderr, and Code Mode shows `Figure_1.png` to `Figure_4.png` after a call that runs Python, like any image file a call creates. python/Cargo.lock adds only `nanocode-compression` to nanocode-python's dependencies; the root Cargo.lock is unchanged. `node scripts/build.mjs` (binaryen 133): dist/python.wasm 50,528,263 bytes, 24,796,970 with `gzip -9` (was about 40.9 MB and 16.3 MB without matplotlib; fonts are about 3.9 MB of the gzipped growth). `bench/python/asyncfs.ts` gains sine, `plt.show()` and bar-chart probes: 20/20 pass, async and sync; matplotlib's first import takes about 1.1 to 1.4 s cold and 1.0 to 1.2 s warm, a later plot 150 to 240 ms. `CAPI=... bench/python/score.ts`: packages 7/18, numpy 14/14, plots matplotlib 6/6, pillow 6/6, contourpy 2/2, kiwisolver 1/1. `npm run check:types` passes.
- feat(machines): a host can answer an unpaid gateway KV call from the session's own store (2026-11-17, demo annoyances swarm, paid-fallback lane). Why: without a connected Tempo Wallet and without `TEMPO_SPONSOR_KEY`, the homepage KV chip ended in `tempo_wallet_required`, and the deployment should not depend on a sponsor wallet. `Wallets.create({ kv: true })` (Cloudflare and Vercel) makes `Payment.locked` take a `fallback` that runs after the sponsor (when one is set and it fails) and before `tempo_wallet_required`: `src/machines/Kv.ts` answers the Tempo gateway's `kv_set`, `kv_get`, `kv_list` and `kv_delete` MCP calls from the Wallet's `Store` (Cloudflare: the Wallet Durable Object's SQLite KV; Vercel: the session's wallet doc in DurableSessions), keyed by `app`, with versions, `ifVersion` compare-and-set, `ttl` and the gateway's published limits (64 KiB JSON values, 90-day TTL, list pages of 100). Calls naming another `owner` or asking for `public: true` fall through to `tempo_wallet_required`, since those only mean something on the shared gateway. The model sees the gateway's own `tools/list` (free), so no tool text changes and nothing goes upstream; a connected wallet still pays the real gateway exactly as before, since `Payment.locked` only runs without a key. Web search needs no fallback: nanocode's native `WebSearch` (Claude) and `web_search` (Codex) already run server-side on the deployment's model key, so the demos reword the Mercator chip instead of spending a wallet. Checks: `npm run check:types` passed (all three tsconfigs), `npx vp check` on the six files passed. Measured with real Claude Haiku 5.5 and the built `dist/` overlaid on build-241889c in each demo, no wallet and no sponsor: durable-cloudflare on Miniflare (`scripts/local-runtime.mjs`, provider routed to api.anthropic.com) ran `Store a note in KV and read it back` 3/3 completed (`mcp__gateway__kv_set`, `mcp__gateway__kv_get`, 4.2 to 20.0 s) and `Search the web for today's Tempo news` 3/3 completed (`WebSearch`, 13.9 to 16.0 s); durable-vercel under `next dev` (AI Gateway base URL pointed at a local Anthropic proxy) ran the same two chips 3/3 and 3/3 (3.6 to 6.2 s and 13.5 to 15.3 s); zero failed cells in all 12 runs.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode 101176e2b (2026-11-17, demo annoyances swarm). `sdk-revision.json` moves from a9e83b2cc to 101176e2b, which adds 101176e2b: a nested call's `workdir` joins the relative paths it names when Code Mode looks for image files the cell created. Codex runs a script through exec_command with workdir set, so a chart saved under a relative name now shows. A local Codex replay of a captured /chatgpt turn showed the image in 0 of 3 runs before and 4 of 4 after. Validated with `npm run build` and `npm run check:types` on gak-9 through `node scripts/release.mjs`.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode a9e83b2cc (2026-11-16, demo annoyances swarm). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 55b597835 to a9e83b2cc, which keeps the Python lane's commits and adds: bc4fbbbc9 names deferred nested tools' arguments in the exec description (models called Mercator's describe_service with serviceId); 721f9ee79 makes prelude WebAssembly exports synchronous through callSync (a naive add(2, 3) returned a Promise and models retried); 0f8dd4fc2 waits in place for a foreground command under the default yield instead of spending a model turn on wait; a9e83b2cc adds image files a cell creates or changes as that cell's image output. Validated with `npm run build` (NANOCODE_CC and CC/AR_wasm32_unknown_unknown on LLVM 22) and `npm run check:types` on a Linux hosted Hand; each lane's measurements are in nanocode's DECISIONS.md.
- feat(wasm): synchronous `WebAssembly` instance exports through the prelude's `callSync`, and `WebAssembly.Module.exports` and `imports` (2026-11-16, demo annoyances swarm, WebAssembly lane). Why: models call `instance.exports.add(2, 3)` without `await`, get a Promise and retry (6 of 8 Haiku 5.5 runs of the `Run a WebAssembly add function` prompt had a failed or Promise-valued cell, median 11.07 s), and some list exports with `WebAssembly.Module.exports(module)`, which was missing. What: when nanocode passes `callSync` (nanocode 721f9ee7, `__nanocodeSyncTool`), `callWasmSync` makes the `wasm` host call blocking, `new WebAssembly.Instance` instantiates at once and each function export returns its result directly; without it the async exports and the `ready` proxy stay as before, so the current pin is unchanged. `WebAssembly.Module.exports` and `imports` read the export and import sections of a module built from bytes. Measured with `output/wasmprobe.ts` (an in-process `AgentRuntime` with `node:sqlite`) on a local `cargo build --locked --offline --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm` against nanocode 721f9ee7 as `../nanocodex`: scripted naive call prints `5` and `number`, `new WebAssembly.Instance(new WebAssembly.Module(bytes)).exports.add(40, 2)` prints `42`, 200 sequential calls take about 300 ms, and `Module.exports`/`imports` list `add` and `env.f`/`env.mem`. Real Haiku 5.5 on the default prompt: before (current prelude and SDK) 8 runs, 28 cells, median 11.07 s; after, 8 interleaved runs, 9 cells, median 6.21 s, then 6 more runs with `Module.exports`, 7 cells, median 7.6 s, no Promise-valued cell; the one failing cell left is a model-written bad section size byte. Checks: `npm run check:types`.

- feat(machines): the deployment can pay a visitor's paid MCP tool calls under an allowance (2026-11-16, wallet-pays lane). Without a connected Tempo Wallet every paid MCP call (gateway KV, Mercator jobs) answered `tempo_wallet_required`, so a demo's homepage chips for KV and Mercator dead-ended for an anonymous visitor while the footer said the deployment pays. `Account.create`, `vercel/Wallets.create` and `cloudflare/Wallets.create` take an optional `sponsor: Payment.Sponsor` (Cloudflare: `(env) => Payment.Sponsor | undefined`, so the key stays a secret binding): `key` is the deployment's secp256k1 key, `account` the root account when `key` is an access key already authorized on Tempo (the key's own address otherwise), `deposit` the session-channel policy for the deployment's channels, and `allowance` `{ budget, calls, windowMs }`, all host policy with no defaults in nanocode-ts. Only when the visitor has no key and an MCP server answers payment required, Core prices the call from its challenges (`Payment.price`: the highest amount over all challenges, and only charge or per-request session challenges, so an unpriceable call is never paid), admits it against a per-visitor ledger (`sponsored` in the visitor's own store: calls in the current window, USD spent, window start), and replays the request through an mppx payer built from the sponsor key with the same session, charge, challenge ordering and channel exhaustion code as a visitor's wallet (`payerOf`, split out of `pay`; its channels are stored under the sponsor key's address in the visitor's store). A refused call or a failed payment (refunded from the ledger) answers `tempo_wallet_required` with the allowance, so the model still tells the visitor to connect their wallet. A visitor with a key never reaches this path. Scripts' paid fetches stay unsponsored. Validation (Node 24, hosted Hand): `npm run check:types`, `vp check --fix src/machines`; a smoke script against the live gateway and mercator.sh MCP servers with an unfunded random key: no sponsor answers `tempo_wallet_required` as before (kv_set 323 ms, create_job 184 ms); with a sponsor the KV call is admitted and tries to open a 0.01 MACH channel and the Mercator job a 0.002 USDC charge (quote 0.002), both failing only on balance, refunded to spent 0 and answered with the allowance text; with `calls: 1` the second call is refused locally in 87 ms with no payment attempt. Not validated: a funded sponsor completing a payment, since no funded key was available.

- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 8ec3b215e and take stall_nudge_ms (2026-11-16, K7 small-models lane). nanocode 8ec3b215e (https://github.com/nanocodexyz/nanocode/pull/1194) adds the opt-in `ClaudeBuilder::stall_nudge(every)`: each time a session's `exec`, `wait` and `Bash` calls have run for another `every` of wall time in all, that tool result carries a reminder with the minutes waited, the time left and to stop a job that will not finish instead of waiting on it again. The session request takes `stall_nudge_ms`, a positive whole number of milliseconds (400 otherwise); it joins the session key and the configuration like `yield_floor_ms`, and wasm.rs hands it to the builder only when set, so an unset field sends the same configuration as before. Why: both feal-linear-cryptanalysis timeouts of claude-haiku-5-5 in run 0f032a59 spent their last 30% waiting on a brute-force job. Validation: cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown against nanocode 8ec3b215e.

- feat(bench): add bench/sla/ci.ts, paired bootstrap verdicts for interleaved SLA runs (2026-11-16, B6b-12 lane). Benchmark lanes compared arms by eye from medians, and no script turned interleaved samples into a verdict. `node bench/sla/ci.ts --arm=nc (--vs=<arm> | --target=<value>) <samples.jsonl>...` reads rows `{round, arm, session, turn, metric, value, t?, build?, match?}` or the rows `bench/sla/run.ts` writes (`index` is the round, `lane` the arm, every numeric key a metric, `ready` only on turn 1, `wall` the order, an `error` row a failed round). For each metric and turn (or all turns with `--pool-turns`) it prints p50 and p99 per arm with linear (numpy default) quantiles, the ratio of medians, the ratio of p99s and the median of per-round median ratios, each with a 95% percentile bootstrap interval: 10,000 resamples (`--resamples`) of whole rounds with a fixed seed (`--seed`, mulberry32), where rounds that share a session are merged into one cluster and resampled together, and both arms of a round always travel together. The verdict follows the paired round ratio: WIN when its interval excludes 1 on the better side (`--better`, by default lower, and higher for metrics ending in `tps`), LOSS when it excludes 1 on the other side, and TIE otherwise. A cell is MISSING when it has fewer than 30 complete rounds (`--min-rounds`), a round lacks an arm, failed or has unequal sample counts per arm (`--drop-incomplete` drops such rounds and reports the count), an arm has more than one `build`, the arms have different `match` strings, or the time spans of two rounds overlap (by `t` or `wall`, else by line order). `--json=<path>` writes every cell. Checked on the hosted Hand (Node 24, unpinned and shared, load 180-316) against a separate numpy reimplementation that uses the same generator: points and interval ends match to 1e-9 on 9 synthetic sets (win 0.8x, tie, loss 1.25x, sessions over 2 rounds, rendered_tps, target 800, run.ts rows, each turn). An independent numpy generator gives intervals within 0.4%. An exact 0.8x set gives [0.8, 0.8]. Blocked, line-ordered, unbalanced, mixed-build, unmatched and N=29 sets give MISSING. On 200 null cells (true ratio 1, 30 rounds, 2 turns, 1,000 resamples), 6.0% of paired verdicts, 1.5% of ratio-of-medians intervals and 5.0% of ratio-of-p99 intervals were not TIE. The paired median interval ends fall on order statistics of the round ratios, so a new seed often leaves them in place.

- feat(bench): bench/sla/run.ts pairs arms on one prompt per round and tags samples for clustered CIs (2026-11-16, B6b-1 lane). The TTFT comparison against Claude Code had N=10 per lane, a lane-specific prompt and no per-sample round or build, so no paired CI could be computed. `--same-prompt` gives every arm in a round the same run id (`r<round>-t<turn>-<nonce>`), so the prompt text is identical across arms and unique per round; fixture log entries are filtered to those that arrived after this sample's Enter, so the arm that ran earlier in the round is not counted. `--model=<name>` passes the same model to `claude --model` and to `nc-claude` as `--harness claude -m <name>`. Every JSONL sample now carries `round`, `session`, `build` (the first line of the binary's `--version`) and `match` (model, prompt mode, mock or live, PTY size), which the paired bootstrap reads to mark unmatched arms MISSING. Lanes already alternate order every round (AB, BA). `--offset=<n>` starts the round index at n, so a long series can run as several short processes (each under the bench lock's ten minutes) without reusing round or session ids. The `codex` lane now waits for the composer text `Ask Codex to do anything`: Codex CLI 0.169.0 shows `model: loading` against the fixture's model list and never prints `bench/model`, so every sample failed as not ready (found by the B6b-2 lane). A functional run of `nc-claude` with `--same-prompt --model=claude-haiku-4-5 --samples=1 --turns=3` wrote all four fields; the timings themselves need a quiet host.

- feat: arm and fire the mapped root region before session restore with `root_region` (2026-11-16, demoregion1 lane). nanocode 92c9c22fa added `ClaudeClient::with_root_region`, `arm_root_region` and `ArmedRoot::fire`, but no host set a region. `AgentConfiguration.root_region` (Cloudflare `App.create({ rootRegion })`, Vercel `Harness.create({ rootRegion })`) now opts a Claude session in. `WorkerRegion` is the sink: `RootRegion::store` attaches the region to the next write transaction of the store or log, so the host writes it in the same SQL transaction as the records it describes (`nanocode_root_region (session_id, region BLOB)`), and a cleared region deletes the row. A region larger than `SqlHost.value_limit` is not written and counts in `root_regions_dropped`. `open` now reads the row with the `root_region` host operation before `log(scope)` and the session restore, arms it, and fires the root request at once. A checksum or decode failure clears the region and counts in `root_regions_invalid`, and the session restores as before. The SDK clears the region once the root request completes, so a restored session never sends the request twice. Durable Objects keep the row in their SQLite storage. Vercel journals it as a blob with the other statements. `output/region.ts` (node:sqlite host, mocked Claude endpoint, a session with 20 turns and an in-flight turn at eviction) checks per restore that exactly one model request is sent, the turn completes, and the row is gone. Trial (N=1 per lane, `--history 5`): first model request 286 ms without the region, 81 ms with it, 254 ms with a corrupted region (`invalid` 1, normal restore). Clippy needed `#[expect(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` on `codex_harness`, which already failed `-D warnings` on master.

- feat(vercel): return Server-Timing from the chat send route (2026-11-16, tsopt6 lane). Live probes after build-39e67dc (durable-vercel c695f46) showed no first-text gain from `warmDriveMs`. In 12 interleaved live turns, the warm and send requests reached the same instance every time. The model request began 75-300 ms after the send headers in 11 of 12, and the cached model first output took 616-1520 ms (13,012 of 13,016 input tokens cached). The send headers took 372-1869 ms. A text request now returns `server-timing` with `route` (the whole route before the response), `admit` (start of the route to the call of `sessions.send`), `submit` (the `sessions.send` call) and `warm` (`alive` if this instance still runs a warm drive for the session, else `none`). These values separate durable-harness submit latency from route work and show if the warm drive lives when the turn arrives. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `vp check src/vercel/App.ts`.

- perf(vercel): start the drive on the warm call with `warmDriveMs` (2026-11-16, tsopt5 and tsopt6 lanes). On Vercel, a warm session paid the drive start, the runtime open and the model connection after the first turn arrived: live N=4 warm turns had a header median of 918 ms and a first text median of 1800 ms, with model text about 835-930 ms after the headers. `App.create` takes an optional `warmDriveMs` host policy. If it is set, the warm POST (`warm: true`) starts `sessions.drive` for the new session under `waitUntil`. `Harness.create` takes `warmed(sessionId)`, which returns an abort signal for such a drive. A warmed drive opens the runtime, sends a HEAD to the AI Gateway origin to open its connection, and then waits until a `hook_received` hint shows a pending submission, the signal aborts after `warmDriveMs`, or the drive is stopped. If no submission arrived, the drive returns without the idle event. The drive holds the durable-harness lease the whole time, and a send that lands while it waits only publishes a hint, so an acked submission runs in the waiting drive or in the next drive after the lease ends, and never in both. durable-harness (read-only) does not change. Local check against the world-local shim at 100 ms per World call: each turn issues one model request with the warm drive on and off (mock gateway log). Checks: `npm run check:types`, `vp check` on the changed files.

- perf(mcp): replay failed initialize within mcp.failureMs (2026-11-16, tsopt1 lane). `Mcp.defer` cached only successful listings, so an MCP server whose `initialize` request threw on transport (`fetch failed`) was asked again on every turn, before the first provider request. In a durable-vercel run with two failing servers, this took about 740 ms of an 810 ms first event. `RuntimeOptions.mcp.failureMs` is a new optional host policy. If it is set, the isolate keeps the transport error of a failed `initialize` per listing key, and `initialize` throws that error again until `failureMs` passes. It does not keep a failure if the request signal was aborted. A successful origin `initialize` removes the failure. If `failureMs` is not set, the behavior does not change. Measured on the hosted Hand at 02d2a82 (Node 24.22.3, loadavg 9.1/6.3/5.4) with `node output/mcp-failure/run.ts 700 <none|60000> 5`, a mock send that throws after 700 ms, 5 interleaved runs of 5 turns each, one `defer` per turn: without `failureMs`, every turn took 700.0 to 725.7 ms (p50 701.8 ms). With `failureMs` 60000, the first turn took 700.7 to 705.5 ms and turns 2 to 5 took 0.03 to 0.75 ms (p50 0.07 ms, p95 0.57 ms). Check: `npm run check:types`.
- fix(scripts): read store sizes from nanocode_log in measure-cold (2026-11-16, tsopt1 lane). The store keeps every record in one `nanocode_log (scope, seq, body)` table, so the size query of `scripts/measure-cold.ts` failed with `no such table: log_chunks` before the first round. The script now reports `records_bytes` as the sum of `body` lengths, `records` as the row count and `head_bytes` as the largest row. Measured on the hosted Hand at af0f71b (SDK bae2d7fa6, Node 24.22.3, 5 cores, loadavg 4.2 to 4.5), `NANOCODE_WASM_PATH=output/base.wasm node scripts/measure-cold.ts --rounds 5`, p50/p95 in ms: Claude compile 56.5/72.8, cold open 106.1/113.2, cold dispatch 82.8/90.1, cold turn 132.6/144.1, warm turn 16.3/57.8, open on a compiled module 15.4/18.1. Codex compile 61.1/82.0, cold open 122.0/144.6, cold dispatch 46.4/121.7, cold turn 103.3/189.5, warm turn 20.5/36.5, open on a compiled module 29.8/49.5. The module is 13,984,478 bytes (4,963,896 with gzip -9): code 12,005,309 and data 1,938,440. Against the same unoptimized module, wasm-opt with `--converge` gives 14,014,209 bytes and with `--gufa -Oz` appended 13,951,747 (0.23 percent), so the flags stay. `node --no-liftoff` raises the cold open to about 2.3 s, so cold opens depend on lazy Liftoff compilation. Check: `npm run check:types`.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode 95291a918 (2026-11-16, rootfirst2 lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from bae2d7fa6 to 95291a918. 95291a918 removes the open calls of a forgotten cell by a prefix range on `Fold::calls` instead of a scan of every open call: a native 100K-child restart that stalled for more than 20 minutes in `Fold::paged` reaches its first root call in 5-20 s. This package exposes no new API. Checks: `cargo +1.97.1 metadata --locked`, `npm run check:types`.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode bae2d7fa6 (2026-11-16, standby1 lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from f192991d1 to bae2d7fa6. bae2d7fa6 adds `TurnLog::follow`, `Follower::advance`, `Follower::claim`, `OneLog::claim` and `nanocode::turnlog_claim`: a standby folds the turn log as it grows and takes over the writer with a claim row, which fences the old writer through its next append. `TurnLog::open` keeps its behavior and is now `follow` plus the same finish. The range also carries b22fcae70 (durability keeps loaded payloads) and cf26d238b (sim cell_calls). This package exposes no new API. Checks: `cargo +1.97.1 metadata --locked`, `npm run check:types`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode f192991d1 and take persistence_nudge (2026-11-16, Nanocode Opus failure lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from ccc66f323 to f192991d1. f192991d1 adds `ClaudeBuilder::persistence_nudge`: in a headless root session, a final message that admits unfinished or unverified work receives the codex persistence nudge once per turn, and the round continues. The admission list and nudge text move to the tool host unchanged, so codex behavior stays the same. The session request takes an optional `persistence_nudge` boolean in `finishLevers`. Sessions without it keep the same key and configuration. Measured in the sdk: mock Messages API scenarios show off byte-identical to the parent, and on sends one extra request only after an admission. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode ccc66f323 and take verification_requested (2026-11-16, Nanocode nanox lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from dc09e1baf to ccc66f323. ccc66f323 adds `CodexBuilder::verification_requested`, which appends a verification request block to the host instructions so the upstream rule against running tests unless the user asks to verify is answered. d5928d314 in the same range writes the cached system and tools JSON into the Claude request body once. `CodexOptions` takes an optional `verification_requested` boolean, and the Cloudflare session descriptor takes it as a codex switch. Sessions without it keep the same key and configuration. Measured in the sdk: with the option off all 8 system prompt and host instruction outputs for Luna and Sol match the parent byte for byte. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode dc09e1baf and take clearing_cache_floors (2026-11-16, Nanocode cache-write lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 59df7a1bb to dc09e1baf. 59df7a1bb makes the finish notice v2 (kept inputs, computed limits, default library calls) the headless default. dc09e1baf adds `ClaudeBuilder::clearing_cache_floors()`, which places cache breakpoints before the previous and the current tool result clearing boundaries, so a clearing no longer rewrites the cached prefix up to the previous boundary. The session request takes an optional `clearing_cache_floors` boolean in `finishLevers`, passed to the claude builder. Sessions without it keep the same key and configuration. Measured in the sdk: a mock Messages API replay of six 41 to 81 request turns models -6.21% pooled input cost and -13.07% cache-write tokens, with off byte-identical. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode b19220112 and take tool_result_clearing (2026-11-16, Nanocode input-token lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from d2253c609 to b19220112, which adds `ClaudeBuilder::tool_result_clearing(keep, at_least_tokens)`: history clearing keeps the most recent `keep` tool results whole and clears older ones in batches of at least `at_least_tokens`, in place of the fixed 12 and 20,000. The session request takes an optional `tool_result_clearing` object `{ at_least_tokens, keep }` of whole numbers (other keys or values return 400), passed to the claude builder. It joins the session key, so sessions with different values never share a session. Unset sends the same configuration as before. The nanocode entry holds the measurements: on 24 mock sessions shaped by nanoeval run 7dbf47d1, `{ keep: 2, at_least_tokens: 10000 }` lowers input cost by 14.6% at cache read 0.1x and write 1.25x with the server dropping thinking after a changed prefix, and by 4.0% without that drop. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`. `cargo clippy --no-deps --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` reports only the existing `too_many_arguments` on `codex_harness`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode d2253c609 and take finish_ambiguity (2026-11-16). `sdk-revision.json` moves from c55648a2c to d2253c609. 9951a4bf8 adds `ClaudeBuilder::finish_ambiguity(true)`, which turns the headless finish check on and, while less than half of the deadline has elapsed, adds one bullet (474 bytes) to the finish notice: settle each two-way reading of the task statement by its literal wording and examples, keep a value the task says to match verbatim, and else make the result hold under both readings or follow the examples. The session request takes an optional `finish_ambiguity` boolean in `finishLevers`, passed to the claude builder. Sessions without it keep the same key, configuration and request bytes. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode c55648a2c (2026-11-16). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 1b62f5c8b to c55648a2c, whose turn log keeps an in-memory key filter for each evicted index page and no longer loads a whole page to look up a new key. The 10K scale-bench parent holds 4.48 KB per child instead of 5.63 KB (median of 3 interleaved runs). No field or request of nanocode-ts changes. Checks: `cargo +1.97.1 metadata`, `npm run check:types`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 1b62f5c8b and take finish_check (2026-11-16). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 066620494 to 1b62f5c8b. 1adee5c97 makes a headless root session on a Claude Opus model (`claude-opus-` model ID prefix) skip the finish check by default and adds `ClaudeBuilder::finish_check(true)`, which runs it. 1b62f5c8b asks the finish notice for stated cases and checked claims. Every other Claude model keeps the check by default, and the Codex harness is unchanged. A set `finish_retries` always runs the check. `skip_finish_check` is still accepted, and on Opus it is now the default. The session request takes an optional boolean `finish_check` in `finishLevers`: the session key holds it only when true, it is passed to the WebAssembly configuration, and `claude_harness` sets it on the Claude builder. A session that leaves it out keeps the same session key and configuration as before. On Opus that session no longer gets the finish check. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, and `cargo clippy --locked --no-deps --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings`, which reports only the existing `too_many_arguments` on `codex_harness`.
- chore(wasm): pin nanocode 066620494 (2026-11-16). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 80fe87002 to 066620494, whose fresh-attempt finish notice under `finish_retries` also starts the fresh attempt when a run on an input the task did not show, or on one that must come out unchanged, fails. No field or request of nanocode-ts changes. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 80fe87002 and take finish_retries (2026-11-16). `sdk-revision.json` moves from a30a3890d to 80fe87002, which adds the opt-in `ClaudeBuilder::finish_retries`: when the finish check of a headless root turn finds that the task's own check fails, the model reports with `[fresh attempt]` and the turn restarts its conversation from the task prompt and that report, from the files as they are, up to `finish_retries` times within the same turn and its limits. The session request takes an optional `finish_retries`, a positive whole number validated like `finish_check_min_requests` (400 `finish_retries must be a positive whole number`), kept on the session descriptor, passed to the WebAssembly configuration and set on the Claude builder in `claude_harness`. When it is absent, every request is as before. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, and `cargo clippy --locked --no-deps --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings`, which reports only the existing `too_many_arguments` on `codex_harness`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 3c3000300 and take finish_check_min_requests (2026-11-15). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 42e37d2cf to 3c3000300, which adds the opt-in `ClaudeBuilder::finish_check_min_requests`: a headless root turn gets its finish check only once it has made that many model requests by its first final message, so a short turn ends there and a long one is still checked. The session request takes an optional `finish_check_min_requests`, a positive whole number validated like `late_finish_check_ms` and `yield_floor_ms` (400 `finish_check_min_requests must be a positive whole number`), which joins the session key and the configuration only when set and reaches `FinishLevers` through `#[serde(default)]`, so a session without it builds what it built. It composes with `late_finish_check_ms`. The pin also brings nanocode bd2b32c8a, which adds `ExecCommandResult::time_left_seconds`: the wasm workspace's shell sets it to `None` as nanocode's own shell does without a time limit, so its results read as before. Checks: `npm run build` (the built `nanocode_wasm.wasm` names the field), `npm run check:types`. Not run: a session with the field against a model.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 42e37d2cf and take skip_finish_check, late_finish_check_ms, result_effort, stop_at_target, yield_floor_ms and yield_share (2026-11-15, Nanocode 10x cost lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 92a50d3ff to 42e37d2cf, which adds six opt-in `ClaudeBuilder` options: `skip_finish_check` (6008dd10b, a headless turn ends at its first final message), `result_effort` (0f0e2cb2b, the effort of a round that brings only tool results), `stop_at_target` (91785a4bb, a target the task can check ends it once met), `yield_floor` (11f7f6e90, the shortest yield of `exec` and `wait`), `late_finish_check` and `yield_share` (42e37d2cf, the finish check past the last deadline reminder while that much time is left, and a cap on each yield at a percent of the time left). The session request takes them as optional fields: `skip_finish_check` and `stop_at_target` booleans in `finishLevers` (in the session key only when true), `result_effort` one of `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`, `late_finish_check_ms` and `yield_floor_ms` positive whole numbers of milliseconds, and `yield_share` a whole percent from 1 to 100, each answered 400 otherwise. The four valued fields join the session key and the configuration like `tool_result_tokens`, so sessions with different values never share a session. wasm.rs hands each to its builder method only when set, so an unset field sends the same configuration as before. Validation: npm run build against nanocode 42e37d2cf (dist/nanocode_wasm.wasm holds the six field names), npm run check:types.
- Any `@cf/` model of a codex session runs on the Workers AI chat gateway, never on a ChatGPT subscription (2026-11-15, Nanocode 10x cost lane). `workersAiModels` held only `glm-5.3`, so `@cf/deepseek-ai/deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017` opened a Responses session to OpenAI or ChatGPT, and on a host holding a ChatGPT login `served` marked it subscribed, which `Workers AI serves Codex API-key sessions, not ChatGPT subscriptions` refuses. `workersAiModel` now takes `glm-5.3` or any `@cf/` ID, and `served` never subscribes one. The host's egress gets the request at `api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/<WORKERS_AI_ACCOUNT_ID>/ai/v1/chat/completions`, as glm-5.3's did. The pinned nanocode already holds de88656e0, so its chat transport sends thinking none for a custom model. The same change was released alone as build-0faae3c from branch pin/w7m-workers-ai (nanocode 0500769ef: 6fb4539ea plus de88656e0), so a run can take it without the nanocode commits since 6fb4539ea. Validation: npm run check:types.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 92a50d3ff and take persist_finish (2026-11-15, Nanocode structural cost lane). The session request takes an optional boolean `persist_finish`, a finish lever like `brief_finish` (so it joins the session key only when true), passed to `ClaudeBuilder::persist_finish` and, for Codex sessions, to the Codex builder's `persist_finish`. Unset or false sends the same configuration as before. The nanocode entry holds the loss-ledger evidence: the most common shape of a nanocode loss to the reference agent on the same task and attempt is a final message that names an unmet requirement and stops with time left. Validation: `npm run build`, `npm run check:types`; the built WASM carries the new checklist line.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 6fb4539ea and take escalate and tool_result_tokens (2026-11-15, Nanocode structural cost lane). The session request takes an optional `escalate` object `{ model, context_tokens?, tool_errors? }` (a non-empty model and at least one positive whole trigger, else 400; both triggers make `EscalationTrigger::Either`), passed to `ClaudeBuilder::escalate`, and an optional positive whole `tool_result_tokens`, passed to `ClaudeBuilder::tool_result_tokens`. Both join the session key, so sessions with different values never share a session. Unset sends the same configuration as before. The nanocode entries hold the measurements behind each: Sonnet 5.5's rates in nanocode's pricing table are 0.5x Opus 5.5's on every component, and results above about 2,000 tokens cost $0.093 an attempt at a 6.25 write and 0.50 read per million tokens in nanoeval run 7dbf47d1's Opus medium arm. Validation: `npm run build` (needs `clang` on PATH for the Python SDK's C API; clang-22 linked as clang on the hosted Hand), `npm run check:types`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 5fbdf8ca8 and take `lean_cells` (2026-11-15, Nanocode cost-per-pass lane, output-token lane OT). `lean_cells` is an optional boolean of the session request, validated and passed on only when true like the other `finishLevers`, and wasm.rs hands it to `ClaudeBuilder::lean_cells`, which appends a reminder for short code cells, bounded output reads and no re-read of unchanged files to each prompt of a headless root session. Unset sends the same configuration as before. Validation: npm run build against nanocode 5fbdf8ca8 (dist/nanocode_wasm.wasm contains the notice text), npm run check:types.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode f417d3594 and take `brief_final` (2026-11-15, Nanocode cost-per-pass lane, output-token lane OT). `brief_final` is an optional boolean of the session request, validated and passed on only when true like `brief_finish`, `fold_finish` and `low_effort_rounds` (`finishLevers`), and wasm.rs hands it to `ClaudeBuilder::brief_final`, which appends a three-line limit on the final message to each prompt of a headless root session. Unset sends the same configuration as before. Validation: npm run build against nanocode f417d3594 (dist/nanocode_wasm.wasm contains the notice text), npm run check:types.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 6e52ee2a3, which adds a bullet to `FINISH_CHECKLIST`: if the task states a speed, size or score goal, the result is compared with at least one structurally different approach under repeated measurements and the best one is kept (2026-11-15, Nanocode cost-per-pass lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 13f7ae847 to 6e52ee2a3. Why: in nanoeval runs b7d70ffb and 6294482a, two failed rows of one optimization task beat only the slow given baseline and stopped at 1.10x and 1.12x of the reference time (limit 1.05x). The finish check and the `fold_finish` checklist carry the new bullet. No TypeScript changes. Checks: `npm ci`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:types`.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode 13f7ae847 and take `brief_finish`, `fold_finish` and `low_effort_rounds` (2026-11-15, Nanocode cost-per-pass lane, wave 9). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 2bb4f90e3 to 13f7ae847, which adds `ClaudeBuilder::low_effort_rounds` and `ClaudeBuilder::fold_finish`; `ClaudeBuilder::brief_finish` came with 2bb4f90e3. Each is an optional boolean of the session request, validated in `src/cloudflare/App.ts` (`finishLevers`, 400 `<name> must be a boolean`), stored on the `SessionDescriptor`, added to the session's opening key and to the configuration only when true, read by `src/wasm.rs` as `#[serde(default)]` booleans and passed to the Claude harness's configure closure. Unset or false sends the same configuration as before, and the builder defaults are false, so requests are unchanged. Why: nanoeval A/B arms for the finish-check tail and the Code Mode wait rounds that the cost decomposition of runs 784946b4 and b7d70ffb measured (nanocode DECISIONS.md, 13f7ae847). Checked: `npm run check:types` passes, and `cargo check --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` passes against nanocode 13f7ae847.
- feat: an unset `refusal_fallback` means `return_once`: `#[default]` moves from `RefusalFallback::Session` to `RefusalFallback::ReturnOnce` in `src/wasm.rs`, which matches the nanocode default from 26c5f99e7 (2026-11-15). Why: Terminal-Bench run 43fc0b7d solved 28/36 with both scopes, and `return_once` cost 9.7% less. Checked: `npm run check:types` passes.
- feat(wasm): pass a session's `deadline` to the Codex harness as well as the Claude harness (`CodexHarness::deadline`, nanocode 9f34c3852), so Codex sessions get the 50/75/90% time reminders, `yield_time_ms` capped to the time left and one headless finish check (2026-11-15). Why: Terminal-Bench run 908873c9 (gpt-6-luna high) scored Nano Codex 44/102 against the stock Codex CLI's 50/103, with turns that ended at 160 to 223 s with an admission of failure. A session without `deadline` is unchanged. Checked: `npm run check:types` passes, and `node scripts/build.mjs` builds the WebAssembly module (`wasm` profile) at nanocode 9452a7af3.
- feat(wasm): pin nanocode b48e3d5c6, bound a hand request's recorded deadline by a direct call's run time, and take `refusal_fallback` (2026-11-15, wave 7c, run 3d8f01a9). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 783b0d402 to b48e3d5c6, which adds 1e3f95b1e and 30e4b80b2 (finish check and deadline reminder wording), 84acc1a6e (the refusal fallback model stays for the session, or `RefusalFallbackScope::ReturnOnce`), b6e103105 (the time left on every tool round after the first reminder), c0bf03f85 (a build-time Code Mode bootstrap snapshot) and b48e3d5c6: a direct Claude Bash on a hand is bounded by its own `timeout` (`run_time_ms` on the Call) instead of the 30 s `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` alone, and a headless root Claude session on a hand that advertises `job_notices` holds its turn for each background job notice before the finish check. `requestedYield` returns a Call's `run_time_ms`, so the deadline `recordHandRequests` stores for a recovered direct Bash is `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` plus that run time, the same bound the live operation has. A turn body's `refusal_fallback` (`'session'`, the default and Claude Code 2.1.325's behavior, or `'return_once'`) is validated, kept in the session descriptor, appended to the opening key only when set (so sessions without it keep their key), and passed to `ClaudeBuilder::refusal_fallback_scope` through the harness's `configure`. Measured with output/wave7c/run.ts (AgentRuntime with this module built from b48e3d5c6, `nanocode hand` built from b48e3d5c6, a scripted Messages endpoint, the remote policy's `timeout_ms` 30000, one run each): `sleep 40` with `timeout: 300000` completed after 40.4 s; a background `sleep 6` then an end_turn gave the task notification as request 3 at 6.1 s and the finish check as request 4, direct, through an exec cell, and with `refusal_fallback: 'return_once'`; a turn deadline of 20 s with a `sleep 11` produced "Time limit: 8 s of this task's 20 s remain." in the next request, so a deadline in the configuration reaches the Claude harness. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `npm run check:types`, `cargo fmt --check` and `vp check` show no new findings in the changed lines. `cargo clippy --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` still stops at `codex_harness` (9 arguments), which this change does not touch; `claude_harness` takes its eighth argument under `#[expect(clippy::too_many_arguments)]`. Not run: the App's turn endpoint under workerd and a deployed Worker; `node scripts/build.mjs` built the module and stopped at the Python interpreter, whose C build needs a clang with the wasm32 target.

- feat(wasm): a session opens with the hands already attached to its scope, and the Claude harness takes `headless` (2026-11-15, wave 7b). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 498e20459 to 783b0d402, which adds 8131a9e31 (a headless Claude root turn checks its work once before it ends), a06bce803 (a Code Mode yield lists returned and running nested calls), c7c42d5e5 (a foreground Bash names the processes it leaves running), 783b0d402 (Claude's Bash gives the output of a running nested call) and f1cc5300c. The Claude harness never called `ClaudeHarness::headless`, so `headless: true` reached only Codex sessions; `open` now passes `config.headless` to both. A turn whose deadline differs from the one its session opened with opens the session again, since the deadline is in the opening key and `ClaudeHarness::deadline` is fixed when the session is built. The hands were attached after `TurnInbox::open`, and a resumed Codex session computes its request prefix, the declared tools included, when it opens, so every request of a reopened session declared no hand tools in `exec`: nanoeval's `branches` lane found this. `AgentConfiguration` now takes `attached_hands` (each `{ connection, hand }`) and `prompt_hand`; `open` attaches those hands and selects the prompt hand for the session before the agent opens, `init` returns the connections it attached, and `AgentRuntime.attach` skips them. The Cloudflare App passes every hand of the scope and the turn's hand. A reopen still applies a changed deadline. Checked with output/wave7b/reopen.ts on gak-9 (macOS arm64): a Node host of the unoptimized `wasm` profile build, a native nanocode hand (783b0d402, debug) and scripted model endpoints, which runs one turn, disposes the runtime and opens the same session on the same store for a second turn. Codex with the hands attached after open (as before): turn 1 declares `exec_command`, turn 2 does not; with `attached_hands`: both do. Claude with the hands attached after open declares no `Bash` in either turn, and with `attached_hands` declares it in turn 2; a fresh Claude session builds its tool catalog when it opens, so a hand that attaches later is not in it. Commands: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `cargo build --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`. `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` stops at `codex_harness` (9 arguments), and `cargo fmt --check` and `vp check` report files this change does not touch, as on d56a177.
- feat(wasm): `claude_orchestration` (`code_mode` or `direct`, default `code_mode`) selects how a Claude session calls its tools, and `sdk-revision.json` pins nanocode 498e20459 (2026-11-15, Terminal-Bench 2.1 claude-direct lane). With `direct`, `claude_harness` sets `ClaudeHarness::direct_tools_for`, and nanocode 379ffb760 then offers the hand's Claude tools to the model directly instead of `exec` and `wait`. The hand tool selection for `claude` becomes `include: nanocode::claude::HAND_TOOLS` (unless the host sets its own `hand_tools.claude`), so the model does not get apply_patch, exec_command, write_stdin or view_image. The `hands` tool and `PLACEMENT_INSTRUCTIONS` are left out of a direct Claude session, because both describe Code Mode scripts. Codex sessions and `code_mode` Claude sessions are unchanged. The Cloudflare `App` accepts `claude_orchestration` on a turn, rejects other values with 400, binds it to the session like `codex_orchestration` (409 on a change), and passes it to the module. nanocode 498e20459 also returns a hand's background job notices with direct calls. Why: direct tools beat Code Mode by 6.4 points on the Codex pair, and the Claude arm of nanoeval could only run Code Mode. In the 39 nano attempts of run 1fbd4a11 kept in ~/nc-live/miss89 on dev-georgios, Code Mode took 700 exec and 80 wait calls for 1,449 nested calls, 11 failed cells, 79 yields without output (3,439 s of cell time and 405 s of model calls that only sent the next wait) and 25 finished cells that printed none of their nested output. Checked with output/claude-direct/run.ts on gak-9: this module built from source against nanocode 498e20459, a debug `nanocode hand` of the same tree, and a scripted Messages endpoint. With `claude_orchestration: "direct"` the first request offers Bash, Edit, Read, TaskStop, WebSearch and Write with no Code Mode block and no placement text. Bash, Write, Read, Edit, a background Bash and a foreground Bash all run on the hand, and the foreground Bash returns the background job's `<task-notification>`. Without the option the request offers exec and wait as before. Commands: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `node scripts/build.mjs`. `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` reports only the existing argument count of `codex_harness`. Files: src/wasm.rs, src/wasm/remote.rs, src/Contracts.ts, src/cloudflare/App.ts, sdk-revision.json.
- Pin nanocode 6e55490e2 (2026-11-15, cwd-prompt lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 9cc200cfe to 6e55490e2, which adds f39f35df1 (a Code Mode tool call's promise is settled by the host) and 6e55490e2: a Claude session whose cells run on a hand renders its `# Environment` section from that hand at its first request (` - Primary working directory`, ` - Is a git repository`, ` - Platform`, ` - Shell`, ` - OS Version`, Claude Code 2.1.318's bullet form). nanoeval opens Claude sessions with `workspace: false` and `hands: 'always'`, so `wasm.rs` gives the Claude harness an `Environment` that holds only the placement instructions, and the section had no working directory (Terminal-Bench 2.1 run 1fbd4a11, dna-assembly a00397: the model wrote /root/primers.fasta while its cwd was /app). The SDK now asks the hand the session is placed on, so nothing in `wasm.rs` or `App.ts` changes. `Contracts.HandEnvironment` gains the optional `git_repository`, the field nanocode's `HandEnvironment` now carries, so a TypeScript hand can report it; a hand that leaves it out gets no git line. Checked with `cargo check --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` against ../nanocodex at 6e55490e2 (Cargo.lock unchanged) and `npm run check:types`. The first request of a remote-hand session was checked in nanocode's output/cwd-prompt (nanocode DECISIONS, 6e55490e2). Not run: a nanoeval rerun on a build of this pin.
- feat: a turn takes `deadline: { at_ms, limit_ms }` (the Unix time in milliseconds at which the host stops the turn, and the length of the limit), and the session passes it to the Claude harness as `ClaudeHarness::deadline`. `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 9708674f4 to 9cc200cfe, which adds only that deadline (2026-11-15, Terminal-Bench lane deadline). The value is host policy: the App checks that both fields are positive safe integers, keeps it in the turn's `SessionDescriptor` so a restarted object reopens the session with it, and adds it to the session's opening key and to `AgentConfiguration.deadline`. The Codex harness ignores it. Without the field the configuration, the opening key and the session are as before. With it, the nanocode Claude agent adds a time-limit system message at 50%, 75% and 90% of the limit and caps a Bash `timeout` or an exec or wait `yield_time_ms` at the time remaining with a notice (see nanocode DECISIONS for the measurements behind the fractions). Why: nanoeval cancels a Terminal-Bench attempt at its agent timeout, and in run 1fbd4a11 the agent did not know that time. The model text comes from the SDK. nanoeval sends only the numbers. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` and `npm run check:types` pass. `cargo clippy --no-deps --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` reports only the existing `too_many_arguments` on `codex_harness`, as on 3dab5a6. `cargo fmt --check` and `vp fmt --check` report the same differences as on 3dab5a6.
- feat(codex): a session configured with `headless: true` (`Contracts.AgentConfiguration.headless`, or the Cloudflare `App` option `headless`) builds its Codex harness with `CodexHarness::headless(true)`, and `sdk-revision.json` pins nanocode 30221fc5f, which continues a headless Codex turn once with a `<persistence>` nudge when its final message admits incomplete or unverified work (2026-11-15, Codex persistence-guard lane). Default off, so browser and chat hosts are unchanged; a benchmark host such as nanoeval's Worker opts in. Why: 12 of 25 failed nanox attempts in Terminal-Bench 2.1 run 0d6c29c9 ended on such a message, and replaying those final requests with the nudge on Workers AI kimi-k2.7-code and deepseek-v4.1-flash, 54 of 60 decided replies called a tool instead of stopping (nanocode DECISIONS.md has the measurement). Checked: `cargo check --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` against nanocode 30221fc5f and `npm run check:types` on the hosted Hand.
- Pin nanocode 455d4e724 (2026-11-15, wave 6). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode b7ec802b6 to 455d4e724, which adds: the Claude client bounds the response head by `MESSAGES_HEAD_TIMEOUT` (600 s) instead of the 300 s stream idle timeout, and the Responses HTTPS path no longer bounds the head (88dbfed15); `Tools::default()` sets `background_jobs: true` and Codex host instructions gain a plain-decimal numbers line for built-in models (9cf69a208); a static `nanocode-front` binary for the CLI on Linux x86_64 (3e574d49a, CLI only); and DECISIONS-only commits. The brain here still passes `with_background_jobs(jobs)` with `jobs` true only for `codex_orchestration: "jobs"`, and `remote::ToolSelections` still hides the job tools on the hand otherwise, so the SDK default change does not change which tools a nanocode-ts session exposes. `Cargo.lock` is unchanged (`cargo metadata` writes no edge). Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm` with ../nanocodex at 455d4e724 (`NANOCODE_CC=clang-22`).
- Pin nanocode b7ec802b6 (2026-11-15, nanocode 1.0 djs track: run_hot redesign against QuickJS). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 13e49f790 to b7ec802b6, which adds five djs changes: for-of over a plain generator and its `yield` switch frames inside the tier-0 loop (ee29f9c5e), iterator results built in the cached shape (c26f047e1), Array.prototype.forEach/map/filter/some/every/find/findIndex/reduce enter their loop at the call with a new `EachNative` continuation and step it inside run_hot with no prelude frame (5c6f08709), run_hot checks fuel only at its safepoints instead of before every instruction (11717466a), and run_hot keeps only bp, lp and the code of the frame in locals (b7ec802b6). Natively on djs-embed (`perf stat -e instructions:u`, best of 3, nanocode DECISIONS.md has the commands) from 767ba009c to b7ec802b6: for-of over a generator 3,259M -> 2,549M (-22%), `g.next()` 5,434M -> 4,654M (-14%), find 1,084M -> 769M (-29%), forEach 1,338M -> 1,010M (-25%), a plain for loop 849M -> 767M (-10%), reduce 1,023M -> 697M (-32%), map 1,407M -> 1,098M (-22%); forEach, some, map, filter, reduce and `new Error` now take fewer instructions than QuickJS. Under V8 the fuel and frame-local changes measured -10% on the loop and -5 to -9% on find, gen and forEach (wasm32 build in node, before the rebase). Fuel totals are unchanged; a run that runs out of fuel now stops at the next safepoint instead of the next instruction, still deterministically. The pin also brings the SDK commits between the two revisions, and `Cargo.lock` gains their `nanocode-sys` -> `djs-libm` edge. Checks: test262 subset 2,535 passing with an identical pass set; `cargo clippy --locked --lib --no-deps -p djs-core -- -D warnings` natively and for wasm32 in nanocode; here `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm` with ../nanocodex at b7ec802b6.
- `psh-revision.json` pins nanocode 02b304a43 (2026-11-15, psh 10x-bash lane). The psh commits since 92f4e0d9c: loops (while, until, for ((…)), for name in words) are compiled once to flat code with integer variable slots, with native arithmetic, assignments, integer tests, true/false/:, break/continue and echo (a951e636d, 02b304a43); array subscripts reached inside arithmetic are expanded as bash does, behind a parse-time gate (302f38a87); in-process utilities search PATH once (3443e2042); seq writes integers in place (040439201). psh-spec in-process on Linux: oils 2493 to 2495 of 2511, brush 3131 of 3171, agent 727 of 727 and utils 998 of 1001, no new failures. Native psh, CPU ms (bash 5.2 in parentheses): `while [ $i -lt 100000 ]; do i=$((i+1)); done` 101.3 to 5.5 (207.3), `while ((i<100000)); do ((i++)); done` 26.9 to 5.8 (79.1), `for ((i=0;i<20000;i++)); do echo $i; done | grep -c 1` 10.9 to 4.2 (54.3), `seq 20000 | grep -c 1` 3.9 to 2.7 (10.6). `node scripts/psh.mjs` builds `psh_wasm_core.wasm` at 7,198,630 bytes with no new paths; `npm run check:types` passes.
- fix(wasm): the hand tool selection hides job_start, job_wait and job_kill from every session but a Codex session with `codex_orchestration: "jobs"` (2026-11-15). nanocode 16f93adbc advertises the job tools on every hand and hides them only through `CodeModeToolPolicy::hidden_host_tool`, which filters the exec description the brain writes; a cell placed on a hand lists and calls the hand's own catalog, narrowed only by `remote::ToolSelections`. So with nanocode-cli-92f4e0d as the hand and build-ef48569 as the brain, a plain `codex` session's exec description had no job tools while `ALL_TOOLS` in its cells was `[apply_patch, exec_command, view_image, write_stdin, job_start, job_wait, job_kill, hands]` (nanoeval's scripted gateway lane, three of three codex cells). `ToolSelections::new` now takes whether the session is a jobs session and adds `nanocode::process::JOB_TOOLS` to the exclusions of `claude` always and of `codex` unless it is. A hand that advertises no job tools still gets no selection (every definition passes), so older hands are unaffected. Checked with nanoeval main plus its new codex-jobs assertions, this tree packed locally (`node scripts/build.mjs`, `npm run prepare`, `npm run check:types`, `npm pack`) against nanocode-cli-92f4e0d: the gateway lane (codex `ALL_TOOLS` holds no job tool), the direct lane with a `codex`, a `direct` and a `jobs` agent (only the jobs session's exec description and `ALL_TOOLS` hold the three job tools, and only its two requests mention them) and the branches lane pass; each failed before on build-ef48569. Files: src/wasm.rs, src/wasm/remote.rs.
- fix(build): `scripts/psh.mjs` extracts `crates/patches/djs/Cargo.toml` and `crates/patches/djs/crates/djs-libm` beside the psh workspace (2026-11-15, release of a2d41bd). psh at 92f4e0d9c depends on `djs-libm` by path (`crates/patches/psh/Cargo.toml`: `djs-libm = { path = "../djs/crates/djs-libm" }`), which inherits edition, version and publish from the djs workspace root, and the archive list held neither, so a build from a fresh clone (no `target/psh-source/<revision>` and no `NANOCODE_PSH_SOURCE`) stopped in `scripts/release.mjs` with `failed to read .../crates/patches/djs/crates/djs-libm/Cargo.toml`. The two paths are what `cargo metadata` of the psh workspace at 92f4e0d9c lists outside it beyond the existing ones; with only them added, `cargo metadata --offline` in an extract of exactly the listed paths passes. Also found while releasing on the hosted Hand: `nanocode-python-capi`'s build script runs `clang` unless `NANOCODE_CC` names a compiler, and a machine with only versioned LLVM (clang-22) needs `NANOCODE_CC=clang-22` for `node scripts/release.mjs`. Files: scripts/psh.mjs.
- `psh-revision.json` pins nanocode 92f4e0d9c (2026-11-14, psh conformance). The one psh commit since 93ae28965 that this pin is for, 92f4e0d9c, makes the standalone shell catch a signal only while a trap, an EXIT trap, interactivity or a foreground wait needs it, lets an interactive shell survive SIGTERM, and keeps an interactive shell running after `set -u` meets an unset parameter; the commits between (hexdump, base64, strftime and math moved onto shared nanocode crates) come along. psh-spec in-process on Linux, with psh on PATH as bash was in the recording: brush 3129 to 3131 of 3171, oils 2492 to 2493 of 2511, agent 727 of 727 and utils 998 of 1001 unchanged, no new failures. `psh_wasm_core.wasm` is 7,077,467 bytes. `scripts/psh.mjs` needs no new paths. A native while-loop of 100,000 iterations runs in 127.6 ms against 126.6 ms before (best of 7, within noise).
The record below describes the SDK, the hosts, the adapters, and measured behavior. Where it conflicts with the conventions above, the conventions apply to new and changed code. Entries written before the namespace API of 2026-10-28 use its old names: `createNanocode` is `App.create` (`nanocode-ts/cloudflare` and `nanocode-ts/vercel`), `aiGateway(ai, gateway, serviceTier, firstByteMs)` is `Gateway.create(ai, { id, serviceTier, firstByteMs })`, `nanocodeHarness` is `Harness.create`, `connectHand`, `prepareHand`, `compileModule` and `handKeeper` are `Hand.connect`, `Hand.prepare`, `Hand.compile` and `Hand.keeper`, `deferMcp` is `Mcp.defer`, `relayHands` is `Bridge.relay`, `openSession` is `Session.open`, `threadEngine` is `Thread.engine` (`src/internal/Thread.ts`), `localEngine` is `AgentRuntime.localEngine`, `new EventHub` is `EventHub.create`, `Timings` is `Timing.create`, `HostFailure` and `RemoteRefusal` are `Contracts.HostFailureError` and `Contracts.RemoteRefusalError`, `DurableObjectSql` is `Sql.create`, `relay`, `relayToken` and `refuseSocket` are `Relay.route`, `Relay.token` and `Relay.refuse`, `cloudflare(...)` and `vercel(...)` are each adapter's `Transport.create`, `openReader` is `Reader.open`, `SqlJournal` and `LocalSql` are `Journal.create` and `LocalSql.create`, `gatewayDispatch` is the Vercel `Gateway.dispatch`, `dialHand`, `attachedHands` and `handRoute` are `Hands.dial`, `Hands.attached` and `Hands.route`, `new Bash`, `new MemoryFileSystem`, `SqlFileSystem(sql, scope)` and `mounted` are `Bash.create`, `MemoryFileSystem.create`, `SqlFileSystem.create` and `Mount.create`, `BASH_COMMANDS` and `DEFAULT_LIMITS` are `Bash.commandNames` and `Bash.defaultLimits`, `preloadPython` is `Python.preload`, `fileResponse`, `uploadFile` and `writeFile` are `Files.response`, `Files.upload` and `Files.write` (`src/internal/Files.ts`), `containerWorkspace` and `vercelSandbox` are `Container.create` and `VercelSandbox.create`, `createMachines`, `createWallets` and `walletClass` are `Machines.create`, `Wallets.create` and `Wallets.subclass`, `createConfig`, `modelItems` and `thinkingItems` are `Config.create`, `Pickers.models` and `Pickers.thinking`, and an uppercase constant is a camelCase member of its module (`MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES` is `Files.maxUploadBytes`).

Durable Values. These values outlive a deploy or cross a wire, so a change to them breaks stored state or peers: `Remote.handAccepted` is `{"type":"ack","ids":[]}` in that key order, as the wire format fixes it; the `nanocode_cell_journal_batches` table (`scope`, `session_id`, `cell_id`, `seq`, `body`, where `body` is one or more of the SDK's `CellJournalUpdate` JSON lines joined by newlines and `seq` is the first line's) and the `nanocode_cell_journals` table it replaced (the same columns with one update per row, still read and cleared), the `nanocode_cell_tools` table (`scope`, `digest`, `body`, where `body` is the JSON array of a cell header's tool catalog and `digest` the first 32 hex characters of its BLAKE3) and the marker `[{"$nanocode_tools": <digest>}]` that stands for that catalog in a journal line's `header.tools` in the session's SQL store and the Cloudflare session's `hand_requests` table (`hand`, `runtime`, `id`, `scope`, `session_id`, `call_id`, `deadline`, `replied`, `reply`, where `reply` is the hand's reply frame text); the Vercel harness's settlement keys (`nanocode.settled:<batches>`) and the `settled: true` field and step `placed` IDs of its `nanocode.sql` batches, the `{ base91 }` form of those batch inputs (basE91 over the printable ASCII characters 0x21 to 0x7E other than the double quote, `<` and the backslash, applied to the brotli pack) and the bare-string writer output of `nanocode.sql` steps, its attachment markers (`<nanocode-attachments>`, which Workflow replays parse out of stored prompts), its subagent command marker (`Harness.subagentText`, which stored steer submissions begin with, followed by the command's JSON), the `nanocode.wallet` document kind at version 1 in `nanocode-ts/machines/vercel`, and the `Symbol.for('nanocode-ts.vercel.hands')` and `Symbol.for('nanocode-ts//vercel//module')` globals, which let every copy of the package in one process share its hands and its compiled module. `src/vercel/Wasm.ts` reads `../nanocode_wasm.wasm` relative to itself, so the module stays one folder below the package root. In `src/bash/Bash.ts` later command tables shadow earlier ones, so the order of their spreads decides which handler a name runs. `nanocode-ts/cloudflare` and `nanocode-ts/machines/cloudflare` also export their Durable Object classes flat, because Wrangler binds Durable Object classes by export name. `@vercel/oidc` is an optional peer, so the Vercel gateway imports it only when no API key is set. The WebAssembly fingerprint hashes `src/wasm` and `scripts/build.mjs` and `scripts/wasm.mjs` byte for byte, so `vite.config.ts` keeps the formatter and linter off them.

nanocode-ts and the hosts built on it (nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare, nanocodexyz/durable-vercel, nanocodexyz/nanocode-site) send models only upstream nanocode's native prompts and tool text: no system or developer instructions of their own, no workspace or sandbox instructions, no tool description overrides (Georgios, 2026-10-25). What models need goes into nanocodexyz/nanocode (its WASM `exec` defaults defer `mcp__*` tools, brief the rarely used subagent tools and note QuickJS, nanocodexyz/nanocode#1085). `instructions` stays an option for other hosts. Hands mode's placement text (`PLACEMENT_INSTRUCTIONS`, which the SDK asks brains without a local evaluator to add) goes into `environment.project_instructions`, after the workspace's AGENTS.md, not into `instructions`, because the harnesses' instructions replace their system prompt (Codex's `system_prompt`, Claude's `system`): until 2026-11-03 every session in hands mode, which tempoxyz/gateway's agents always are, sent that one paragraph as its whole system prompt, 592 characters on Claude instead of Claude Code's 5,351, and 496 on Codex instead of its 18,274. `node bench/probe.ts <wasm> claude|codex` opens a hands session with a model that answers 400 and prints the first request's system prompt and where the placement text landed; with this change it lands in Claude's system after the upstream blocks, and in Codex's first user message as `# AGENTS.md instructions for /workspace`, beside `<environment_context>`. Mentions below of `LOCAL_INSTRUCTIONS`, `BASH_CODE_MODE_INSTRUCTIONS`, `BASH_REMOTE_PYTHON_INSTRUCTIONS`, the sandbox instructions, `briefTools`, Codex's file-only workspace default and `deferMcp`'s default deferral describe what was removed then: `deferMcp` now defers only with `deferred: true`, and Codex gets all of its native workspace tools. With every host instruction removed, a local GLM-5.3 sweep through the Cloudflare demo Worker passed 12 of 12; the chart prompt needed 17 model calls, since nothing tells models to embed a saved image. Second pass (Georgios, 2026-10-25): nanocode-ts defines no tool text that duplicates or rewrites an upstream tool. `deferMcp` only caches MCP `initialize` and `tools/list` (its `describe_tools`/`call_tool` rewriting, `deferred` and `core` are gone; the SDK defers `mcp__*` tools natively), `Bash` has no `tool()`/`bashTool`/`BASH_TOOL_DESCRIPTION` (no host used them), and the machines cloud tools drop `list_files`, `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file` and `delete_file` (upstream Read/Edit/Write and `exec_command` cover the session workspace), leaving `http`, `list_secrets` and `wallet` with one factual line each. On WASM, `AgentRuntime.open` reads the workspace's own `AGENTS.override.md` or `AGENTS.md` through the workspace host and passes it as `environment.project_instructions`, as native nanocode loads a workspace's AGENTS.md (nanocodexyz/nanocode's WASM context source only takes it from the host); nanocodexyz/nanocode-site mounts its docs index there. Codex sessions on WASM get every native Codex tool except native-only host routing (`hands`, `runners`): `view_image` reads workspace images through `HostFiles::read_bytes` (the `cell_read` op), and `image_gen__imagegen` posts to `<codex endpoint>/images/generations|edits` through the session's model transport and saves to `generated_images/` through `write_bytes` (`cell_write`), unless the host defines its own `image_gen__imagegen` (nanocodexyz/nanocode#1091). The Cloudflare gateway runs them as the AI Gateway catalog model `openai/<model>` (`gpt-image-2`) and returns OpenAI's `{data: [{b64_json}]}` from the image URL the catalog answers, with edit images as data-URL strings in `images`: AI Gateway added no stored OpenAI key to `openai` provider `images/generations` (401 Missing bearer, while `responses` passed on the same gateway, 2026-10-26). `bench/targets/rust.ts` reads `nc`'s OpenTelemetry traces from a temporary `--home`, which carry Codex token counts, `code_mode.cell` spans and per-session `agent.depth` for subagents.

`bench/` is the optimization and eval harness, in TypeScript with raw-text reports. `node bench/run.ts --targets=cloudflare,vercel,rust --models=haiku,sol,glm [--probes=a,b] [--conc=1] [--label=x]` runs the probes in `bench/probes.ts` (cells, bash, file edits, git clone, fetch, parallel calls, python, WebAssembly, notify, yield and wait, store and load, ALL_TOOLS, web search, subagents, charts) against the live Cloudflare demo (`CF_BASE`, `CF_TOKEN`), the Vercel demo (`VERCEL_BASE`) and native Rust (`nc exec` on a machine with Codex and Claude logins; `nc` has no Workers AI route, so no GLM), grades each answer, and writes `output/bench/<label>.jsonl` and a `.txt` table of pass rate, wall time, model calls, first-call and per-turn input tokens, cached share, and wall time split into model, cells, cold runtime start and host overhead, plus a pass grid. `node bench/overhead.ts` measures Code Mode's own cost in this module in Node with a mock model that answers at once, and `node bench/overhead-native.ts` the same cells in the SDK's native `code_mode` example (`CODE_MODE_BIN`, a release build). `node bench/hands.ts --hand=local|ts|native|docker [--provider=claude|codex] [--cells=a,b] [--n=5]` runs 28 cells, from an empty cell to 500 sequential callbacks, 6 MiB of `text()`, 100,000 `console.log` lines, 10,000 timers, a 10^7 loop and a 200 KB source, through this module's brain in Node (in-memory SQLite, a scripted model that answers at once, a fresh session per cell;
