Checked with `npm run check:types` and `output/wasm-limits.ts` (assembled with the shell's `wat2wasm`): recursive `fib 60` with a 1000 ms deadline exits 124 after about 1,005 ms with and without an unrelated loop, the reserved export and `(memory 16385)` (with or without code) exit 126, `memory.grow 16384` from one page returns -1, `fd_allocate` and `fd_filestat_set_size` of 3 GiB return 22, and 4,096 and 100 bytes still work. The function-entry check costs the most on call-bound code: `output/wasm-bench.ts`, `wasm --invoke fib fib.wasm 32` (7 million calls) in Node 24 on a Linux x86_64 VM, took a median 33 to 36 ms against 15 to 17 ms before (three interleaved runs of 7).
`/wallet` says what the key has paid on the site, not what the site's server paid with it (2026-11-10): a host whose `Keyring` keeps the key in the tab and pays from there, as the home page of nanocode.xyz now does with `Payments.create`, reports its own ledger through `status`, and the sentence named a server that host does not have. `npm run check:types` passed.
A Vercel host can keep each run's SQLite between drives and requests (2026-11-11). `Journal.create` rebuilt an in-memory SQLite from every committed SQL batch on every drive, and the files route, the subagents route and `Reader.open` did the same for every request: Brotli decompression of each batch (about 40% of the CPU), `JSON.parse`, and every statement run again. `Journal.replicas({ bytes })` returns a pool of replicas, one per run: the database, the number of batches it holds and the SHA-256 of each batch's packed bytes. `Journal.open(state, replicas)` takes the run's replica, compares each digest with the log's batch at that position and applies only the batches after them. A replica with a digest that differs is closed and the log is replayed from the start. A replica that is ahead of the view it is opened for stays in the pool and that view is replayed cold. `Journal.create(ctx, { replicas })` returns its replica to the pool when it closes with no failure and no write waiting to commit, so the pool never holds a database that is ahead of the log. Otherwise it closes it, as before. A transaction on a closed journal is now refused with `the SQL journal is closed` (before, the closed database threw). `Journal.read(state, replicas)` is the read path: its `close()` returns the replica only if `total_changes()` and the schema version did not move, so a reader that created a table never leaks it into a drive. The pool keeps at most `bytes` of SQLite pages (`page_count * page_size`), evicts the run kept longest ago first, keeps the longer of two replicas of one run and keeps none whose log holds a batch from before `packed` existed, which has no bytes to digest. Those logs replay as before. `App.create({ replicaBytes })` builds one pool for its harness, its files route and its subagents route. `Harness.create({ replicas })` and `Reader.open(state, { replicas })` take a pool. Without the option nothing is kept and every open replays the log, as before: how much memory an instance may spend on warm databases is host policy, and on 2026-10-24 three sessions that cloned vercel/ai at once ran a shared Function out of memory. `Stats.replayed_batches` and `replayed_transactions` count what this open applied. No stored format changes.

Measured with `FILES=12000 FILE_BYTES=400000 node output/harness/journal.ts` (a hand-written `DriveContext` over a map of effects, 12,000 files of node_modules and src written through `SqlFileSystem.writeMany`, 300 log transactions, 44 batches, 74.8 MiB of files, 14.2 MiB packed), three interleaved runs without and with `REPLICA_BYTES=268435456`: the replay inside `Journal.create` took 269 to 432 ms of CPU and 6.6 to 9.0 ms with a replica (the digests of 14 MiB). Its wall time was 0.9 to 2.6 s against 7 to 223 ms at load 200 to 280. A files request (open, read one file, close) took 0.35 to 2.5 s of wall against 12 to 204 ms. A drive after one new batch applied that batch alone (6.5 to 44 ms against 0.4 to 2.2 s). The base tree and this tree without the option measured alike. `node output/harness/journalcheck.ts` checks 21 properties and all pass: four warm drives replay nothing and the replica then equals a cold replay row for row, a journal closed with an uncommitted write keeps no replica and the next drive does not see the write, a transaction after close is refused, a reader that changed nothing returns its replica and one that created a table does not, a log whose second batch was written by another journal is replayed from the start and its replica dropped, a view three batches long is replayed cold while the longer replica stays, a budget of 0 keeps nothing, a budget of 2.5 replicas keeps the two newest of three, and a log of unpacked batches replays and keeps nothing. Measured: 'FILES=12000 FILE_BYTES=400000 node output/harness/journal.ts' in /tmp/swarm-ts/base (before), in the worktree (after, option unset) and with REPLICA_BYTES=268435456, three interleaved rounds of the three. drive_replay_cpu_ms (5 opens per run): before 431.7,356.1,306.0,295.6,350.0 / 408.2,300.2,358.3,304.3,323.6 / 421.9,346.8,332.3,305.3,349.2. After without the option 432.6,376.7,327.4,283.0,350.5 / 398.8,360.5,293.1,268.7,281.1 / 415.0,354.1,320.8,376.6,401.6. With replicas 8.0,6.7,6.7,6.8,6.7 / 8.0,8.8,6.6,6.7,6.8 / 9.0,6.8,6.8,7.0,6.8. drive wall: 0.89-3.2 s -> 7.3-222.6 ms. file_request_ms: 354.7-2667 (before), 398.5-2481.8 (after, no option), 11.6-204.1 (replicas). drive_after_one_new_batch_ms: 383-2398 -> 6.5, 13.2, 43.9. log_rows 301 in every run. Profile of the cold path (ROUNDS=12, node --cpu-prof, scripts/cpuprofile-top.ts): node:zlib processChunkSync 20.0 s of 47.6 s outside the harness's own file reads, garbage collection 8.6 s, LocalSql statement runs 7.4 s, Journal.unpack 4.6 s (JSON.parse 1.2 s, blob slices 1.9 s). Checked: npm run check:types: pass. npm run build:ts: pass (no Rust or wasm input changed, so the module was not rebuilt for this commit. The later full npm run build on the branch head passes). node output/harness/journalcheck.ts: 21 of 21. npx vp check: formatting issues only in bench/probe.ts, scripts/prepare.mjs and scripts/release.mjs, which this branch does not touch. git diff --check: clean.
`EventHub.stream` serializes an event once for all streams (2026-11-11). Each stream wrote each event as `JSON.stringify({ ...event, streamed_at })`, which was 26% of the CPU of a hub with eight streams (`node --cpu-prof output/harness/events.ts`, line 333 of `EventHub.ts`), `recent()` filtered the whole tail for each stream on each wake (7.5%), and `notify()` copied the listener set (2%). The hub now keeps, per event object, the text `id: <seq>\ndata: <json without its last brace>,"streamed_at":` and each stream appends its time and `}\n\n`. The bytes are the same as before for an event without a `streamed_at` of its own. An event that has one is written the old way. The map is weak and is replaced on every publish, so it holds the text of the events sent since the last publish and no more. `recent()` finds the first event after the cursor by binary search over the tail, whose sequence numbers ascend, and filters by request only from there. `notify()` walks the set, since resolving a listener changes nothing synchronously. Measured with `SUBSCRIBERS=<n> node output/harness/events.ts` (2,000 publishes of four events of 200 to 800 bytes with a live delta each, every stream read to its end, a tail of 1,000 events, CPU of the whole run, three interleaved runs, the second and third round of each): one stream 168 to 245 ms to 164 to 206 ms, eight streams 468 to 546 to 264 to 334, 32 streams 1,556 to 1,699 to 622 to 683. `node output/harness/eventscheck.ts` prints the byte count and SHA-256 of five streams (all events, with live chunks, one turn's events with live chunks, a stream that starts 40 events back, a stream behind the tail that reads log pages) with `Date.now` fixed, and prints the same before and after. `node --expose-gc scripts/measure-events.ts` still passes (a tail of 1 MiB events retains 3,145,896 bytes of heap). Measured and left out: keeping each event's frame as encoded bytes and assembling a stream's chunk from them made one stream slower (155 to 201 ms against 160 to 186 ms, the first round 293 to 327 against 224 to 263) for no more gain at eight. The Cloudflare session's WebSocket watchers (`published` and `deliver` in `src/cloudflare/App.ts`) still serialize each event once per socket: there is no local harness for the Durable Object, so that path is unchanged. Measured: 'SUBSCRIBERS=n node output/harness/events.ts' in /tmp/swarm-ts/base (before) and the worktree (after), three interleaved rounds, cpu_ms of rounds 1,2,3 of each process. n=1 before [264,175,168] [248,218,245] [275,191,206], after [257,171,195] [306,181,164] [239,178,206]. n=8 before [644,522,535] [535,545,525] [596,546,468], after [426,334,292] [350,289,264] [332,322,296]. n=32 before [1832,1580,1556] [1653,1699,1620] [1608,1558,1620], after [879,683,622] [762,626,680] [728,637,631]. Bytes sent are identical (7,829,265 / 62,634,120 / 250,536,480). eventscheck.ts: the five 'name bytes sha256' lines are identical between the two trees. Checked: npm run check:types: pass. npm run build:ts: pass. node --expose-gc scripts/measure-events.ts: ok. node output/harness/eventscheck.ts: identical output before and after. On the branch head: npm run build and npm run check:types pass, git status is clean, the WebAssembly fingerprint is 6dcf49fc1d3e2bcf0723.
2026-11-11 (nanocode-ts): **git's working-file cache is not trusted for a file as young as its entry.** `repo.local.stat[path]` was `[size, mtime, id]` and the id was trusted while size and mtime matched, so an edit of the same length in the millisecond the entry was recorded was invisible. The TypeScript shell was slow enough to hide it; psh over the synchronous filesystem commits and edits within one millisecond. An entry now has a fourth value, when it was taken, and is trusted only when the file's time is older than that. Durable value: `[size, mtime, id, seen?]`; an entry without the fourth value (written by an older build) is hashed once and rewritten, and older builds ignore the extra value. Measured: output/psh/differential.ts, case top-shapes/33 ('c' becomes 'X' in u.txt, then git diff --unified=0 HEAD -- u.txt): empty output on the synchronous path before, the diff after; cases where the synchronous and promise paths differ: 23 to 2 (both print the commit's clock time). Checked: npm run check:types (tsc for the three tsconfigs) passes; the git group of the agent cases gives the same 20 of 84 passes as before under both engines.
2026-11-11 (nanocode-ts): **`Bash.create({ psh })`: the interpreter and the standard utilities come from psh; stage one of replacing the TypeScript shell.** `psh` is the compiled module of nanocode's `psh-wasm` (a `WebAssembly.Module` or a promise of one, as `wasm.interpreter` is); without it the TypeScript interpreter runs as before, so no host changes until it passes the module. `Bash`, `create.Options`, `ExecOptions`, `Result`, `Event` and `FileSystem` keep their shapes. `src/bash/internal/Psh.ts` is the glue: one instance per module shared by every `Bash` of the isolate (a filesystem call is routed to the run being stepped), a run is `psh_shell_new`, `psh_shell_start`, then `psh_shell_step` until done, awaiting the module's wake when it is blocked and `Fs.breathe()` between steps; a trap (a panic, a stack overflow) fails the runs on that instance with `Psh.EngineError` and the next run instantiates again. Limits: `maxCommands` is psh's fuel (commands and loop iterations, so a loop is charged twice per iteration where the interpreter charged once), `maxOutputLength` its output bytes, `maxMemoryBytes` its file bytes, `maxFileOperations` its operations, `maxCallDepth` its nesting (the module allows 768). `fallback` is psh's strict mode: a command that is not found ends the script with 127, and curl or wget without `fetch`, `file`, and a host command's unsupported-option error end it the same way, with the texts Pyramid matches ('command not found', '(this simulated shell has no', 'network access is not available'). A timeout or abort cancels the run and is reported with 124 as before; the limit lines the glue writes begin '/bin/bash: ' without the interpreter's 'line N: '. Inventory of the 119 TypeScript commands under psh (output/psh/inventory.ts): 51 are psh utilities (awk base64 basename cat chmod cmp comm cp cut date diff dirname du egrep env fgrep find grep head jq ln ls md5sum mkdir mktemp mv nl paste printenv readlink realpath rg rm rmdir sed seq sha1sum sha256sum sha512sum sleep sort stat tail tee timeout touch tr uniq wc which xargs), echo, kill and printf are builtins, time a keyword, and all 50 interpreter builtins exist in psh; 64 stay host commands that psh calls with their `CommandContext` over its pipes (stdin on demand, stdout and stderr as a `Pipe` or, when it is the shell's captured output, a spill): the network (curl wget gh), Python (python python3 pip pip3, Pyodide or the remote service as today), WebAssembly programs (wasm wasmtime wat2wasm and `./x.wasm`), git, archives (tar zip unzip gzip gunzip zcat), make, patch, yq, file, and the small ones (base32 bc clear column csplit dd df envsubst expand expr factor fmt fold free hexdump hostname iconv id install join less more mawk nawk nohup nproc numfmt od ps rev shuf split sponge strings tac tree truncate uname unexpand uuidgen whoami xxd yes). A command a host passes in `commands` wins over a psh utility of the same name. Measured importance (psh's corpus of 295,401 agent commands, research/corpus/results.json): psh's utilities and builtins already cover the frequent ones (cd, head, grep, rg, sed, tail, cat, echo, ls, find, sleep, printf, wc, sort, rm, cut, cp, awk, mkdir, mktemp, jq); of the 64 host commands only git (70,242 uses, 23.8%), python3 (31,961), gh (8,128), curl (4,060), make (2,680), ps (989), python (872), nohup (580), tar (552), file (426), id (331), strings (266), gzip (219), xxd (142), wasmtime (82), uname (78), column (76), unzip (67) and od (53) are among the 150 most used names; the other 45 are not, so the next command worth moving into Rust is git's local half, and nothing is dropped yet because the TypeScript table still serves `machine` mode. `machine` mode (the read-only answers Pyramid's proof asks for) stays on the TypeScript interpreter. Hosts must ship the module: the build step and the package export are not in this change (scripts/ is not src/bash). Measured: nanocode-ts worktree, `output/psh/run-bench2.sh OUT ROUNDS ITERS ONLY 'NAME|CHECKOUT|WASM'...` (each engine in its own Node 22.23 process, order alternating per round; workloads = output/psh-bench/workloads.json plus loop2000, stat2000, cat2000, write2000, hostcmd200 over output/psh-bench/data in a MemoryFileSystem; user CPU ms, medians; the machine ran at load 190-280 the whole day, so wall time is not reported), 3 rounds of 5, psh at opt-level s over a promise-returning MemoryFileSystem against the TypeScript shell: faster: sortpipe 33.5 against 208.7, cutsort n/a here (fuel, fixed in 1c833d5d8), jq 135.4 against 244.2, subst 18.0 against 23.7, loop2000 8.9 against 14.3, write2000 33.7 against 35.0, find 12.5 against 12.9; slower: loop 131.8 against 91.7, strbuild 38.1 against 10.5, awk 487.5 against 241.9, sed 675.0 against 441.1, grepr 71.1 against 59.6, rg 67.9 against 39.5, catall 72.2 against 45.9, stat2000 32.1 against 24.2, cat2000 36.6 against 25.7, hostcmd200 22.1 against 10.8, echo 0.4 against 0.1. Differential corpus (output/psh/differential.ts: psh's 727 agent-shaped cases, each in a fresh MemoryFileSystem on both engines, judged against bash 5.3's recorded stdout and status): psh 628 pass, TypeScript 544; identical stdout and status between the two engines in 616. Per group psh/TypeScript: cd-chains 16/16, control 25/25, files 50/46, git 20/20 of 84 (the TypeScript git under both), globs-status 20/20, heredocs 17/16, nested-shells 21/10, pipes 24/24, procs 59/54, redirects 19/19, scripts 53/50, search 72/71, text 47/47, tools 65/18, top-shapes 37/25, vars-quotes 22/22, view 61/61. Cases the TypeScript shell passes and psh does not: 1 (nested-shells/19: `bash -c 'kill -0 $$'`, psh's nested shell has no process id). API parity (output/psh/api.ts, 33 cases: fallback, timeouts, abort, limits, stdin, env, cwd, custom commands with run/exec, a custom command over a utility, binary and UTF-8 data, background jobs, git, tar, zip): 26 identical; 7 differ only in the explained ways (no 'line N: ' in the glue's limit lines; `while true; do :; done` with timeoutMs 300 ends 124 after 0.3 s under psh, the interpreter ran 9.7-17.3 s to 'too many commands'). SqlFileSystem over node:sqlite (output/psh/sql.ts, 2,000 files): same output, the same number of SQL transactions (grep -rn 2,044 against 2,043, find 43 against 43) and CPU within noise of the TypeScript shell (grepr 90.8 against 92.0 ms, grep -rl 55.3 against 90.1, rg 80.1 against 75.5). Heap after the bench: 16-21 MB against 72-217 MB. Checked: npm run check:types passes (tsc --noEmit for tsconfig.json, tsconfig.vercel.json, tsconfig.cloudflare.json); vp check on the changed files: no warnings (the repository's other files were reformatted by `vp check --fix` and put back); git diff --check clean.
2026-11-11 (nanocode-ts): **`FileSystem.sync`, an optional synchronous view, and `MemoryFileSystem` has one.** Every `FileSystem` method returns a promise, so each psh file operation left the engine's step, waited a microtask turn and stepped again, and rg, grep, fd and jq ran more than once to collect answers. `FileSystem.sync?: SyncFileSystem` (stat, read, write, list, mkdir, remove, rename, and optional chmod, utimes, symlink, readlink, lstat as plain functions) is additive; a `MemoryFileSystem` without a lower layer holds everything in memory, so its operations are now plain functions, its promise methods call them after the lower layer's fault and load, and `sync` is set only when there is no lower layer. Wrappers (Mount, a host's own) that do not forward `sync` keep the promise path. SqlFileSystem stays on promises: its SQL host is asynchronous and its lazy sources fetch. Measured: nanocode-ts worktree, `output/psh/run-bench2.sh OUT ROUNDS ITERS ONLY 'NAME|CHECKOUT|WASM'...` (each engine in its own Node 22.23 process, order alternating per round; workloads = output/psh-bench/workloads.json plus loop2000, stat2000, cat2000, write2000, hostcmd200 over output/psh-bench/data in a MemoryFileSystem; user CPU ms, medians; the machine ran at load 190-280 the whole day, so wall time is not reported), 3 rounds of 5, same module, promise path against synchronous path [TypeScript shell]: stat2000 32.1 to 22.2 [24.2], cat2000 36.6 to 23.3 [25.7], write2000 33.7 to 23.3 [35.0], grepr 71.1 to 54.5 [59.6], rg 67.9 to 43.3 [39.5], find 12.5 to 9.7 [12.9], catall 72.2 to 67.2 [45.9]; echo 0.4/0.3, loop2000 8.9/8.0 and hostcmd200 22.1/21.9 within noise. Per operation in the boundary (stat2000 is 4,000 operations): about 5.8 us on the promise path, 3.6 us on the synchronous path, 3.0 us after 9dedb0d, on a machine at load 250. Checked: npm run check:types passes; the 727 agent cases run on ts, psh (sync) and psh over the same filesystem without `sync`: the two psh paths agree in all but two cases, which print the clock.
2026-11-11 (nanocode-ts): **The psh glue answers a synchronous filesystem inside `host_call`, and `maxMemoryBytes` is the module's memory budget.** With `FileSystem.sync` an answer still cost two more exports, a copied payload, a new `Uint8Array` over the module's memory and an `FsError` with its stack for every missing file. The glue returns the answer from `host_call` (psh-wasm f914216af: a length or a negative errno, `LATER` for a promise), decodes paths from the module's memory without copying (only written data is copied, since the filesystem keeps it), holds one view of the memory until it grows, writes a stat through one shared 24-byte buffer and encodes requests into one growing buffer. `limits.maxMemoryBytes` is passed to `psh_memory_limit`: a script past it ends 126 with 'the script needs more than N MB, this host's memory budget' (category `resource_limit`) and the next run loads a fresh instance, where the interpreter threw 'Invalid string length'. A step's budget is 2,000 safepoints: at 2 to 5 us per shell-level safepoint a step stays an order below `Fs.breathe`'s 40 ms. Measured: nanocode-ts worktree, `output/psh/run-bench2.sh OUT ROUNDS ITERS ONLY 'NAME|CHECKOUT|WASM'...` (each engine in its own Node 22.23 process, order alternating per round; workloads = output/psh-bench/workloads.json plus loop2000, stat2000, cat2000, write2000, hostcmd200 over output/psh-bench/data in a MemoryFileSystem; user CPU ms, medians; the machine ran at load 190-280 the whole day, so wall time is not reported), 3 rounds of 5, b8642c1 with module 4b1224709 against this glue with module f914216af (both opt-level s): stat2000 28.6 to 20.3, cat2000 25.9 to 20.5, write2000 30.0 to 24.1, grepr 63.3 to 50.5, rg 48.2 to 38.3, find 11.4 to 7.5, catall 76.9 to 60.9, hostcmd200 25.3 to 21.3, echo 0.3 to 0.2. Final state, module at opt-level 3 (bca29f26f) against the TypeScript shell, 2 rounds of 5: faster: jq 90.7 against 247.0, cutsort 210.9 against 568.0, sortpipe 34.5 against 164.4, find 8.5 against 12.2, stat2000 19.9 against 29.4, write2000 20.1 against 32.5, rg 35.2 against 38.7, grepr 48.4 against 53.9, subst 7.6 against 8.7, loop2000 3.7 against 7.3; slower: loop 92.5 against 77.4, awk 353.4 against 228.1, sed 526.3 against 417.4, catall 70.1 against 47.1, strbuild 67.2 against 6.9, hostcmd200 20.9 against 12.3, cat2000 21.4 against 18.2, echo 0.3-0.5 against 0.1. CPU profile of the slower ones (node --cpu-prof, output/psh/prof.ts, module with names): catall is 31% `psh_utils::wc::Counter::feed`; awk, sed, loop and strbuild are inside psh-utils and psh-engine; hostcmd200 is four JS-to-module round trips per command (two stdin reads, one write, the exit). Checked: npm run check:types passes; vp check on Bash.ts and Psh.ts: no warnings; differential 628 of 727, api.ts 26 of 33 identical with the same 7 explained, memory.ts and sql.ts as recorded.
2026-11-11 (nanocode-ts): **A psh run's timers end with it.** psh's `sleep` is a `setTimeout` on the JavaScript side; a run that was cancelled or timed out left its 'sleep 5' pending, which held Node's event loop until it fired (and answered an operation nobody waited for). Timers are kept per run and cleared when its shell is freed. Measured: output/psh/api.ts 'timeout sleep' ('echo a; sleep 5; echo b' with timeoutMs 200, both engines in one process), three interleaved runs, process wall time: 6.61, 6.11, 6.30 s before; 1.98, 1.35, 1.06 s after. Checked: npm run check:types passes; vp check on Psh.ts: no warnings; the case's output is unchanged.
Integration recheck of the seven changes above on master (2026-11-11): `npm run check:types` passes; `journalcheck.ts` 21 of 21; `eventscheck.ts` prints the same five digests as the branch; `api.ts` against nanocode's merged `psh_wasm.wasm` (7,095,021 bytes, nanocode 3a10fd09c) gives 26 identical and 7 differing only in the 'line N: ' prefix, the extra per-file line of the file-operation limit and the timeout that now ends a `while true` loop; `differential.ts` over the 727 agent cases gives psh 628, TypeScript 544, identical stdout and status in 615. The SDK pin stays where it was: the move to the djs build, the own-thread Vercel harness and djs-core at opt-level 3 are on branch `swarm/perf` and are not on master.
The psh glue keeps one shell per configuration and renews it for each exec, and a host command's reads and last write take one crossing each (2026-11-11). `Bash` caches the encoded configuration keyed on the environment entries (which include PWD) and hands `Psh.run` a kept shell that a finished run gives back; a `FinalizationRegistry` frees it with the `Bash`. The environment of a host command is decoded only if the command reads it, the abort controller is made only if asked for, output goes through `Output.spill` so a broken pipe is seen, and the final write is sent without waiting. Four interleaved rounds of 7, user CPU ms, old glue and module to new: exec500 40.39 to 22.80, hostexec200 28.39 to 24.31, hostcmd200 21.38 to 17.77, echo 0.26 to 0.14, view 0.23 to 0.11. Needs a module with `psh_shell_renew` and `READ_END` (nanocode 21ff23209 and 062d2bef4).
`Bash.create({ machine, psh })` runs machine mode on psh (2026-11-11). The glue passes `machine.commands` and the variables it has answers for (the environment's names and IFS, OPTERR, OPTIND, OSTYPE, PS4, PWD; not BASH, BASH_VERSION or BASH_EXECUTION_STRING, which the TypeScript mode invented), sets strict mode, and turns an unanswered run into exit 127 with `/bin/bash: NAME: (this simulated shell has no answer for this machine's NAME)`. A `MachineError` from the file system (HostFileSystem's refused writes and outside paths) or from a host command ends the script the same way instead of surfacing as an exception or a plain failure, and a host command whose stdin has no answer gets `Input.machine()`. ABI 5. Of 319 scripts run in both engines over a file system that follows pyramid-core's Hand host: 144 same answer, 1 same with different stderr, 104 both refuse, 22 both refuse naming something else, 33 psh refuses where TypeScript answers, 11 different answers, 4 TypeScript refuses where psh answers.
dist/psh.wasm is built from its own pin and exported as `nanocode-ts/psh.wasm` (2026-11-11). `psh-revision.json` names the nanocode revision; `scripts/psh.mjs` takes `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `crates/core/shell` and `crates/patches/psh` at that revision out of the SDK checkout with `git archive` (or uses `NANOCODE_PSH_SOURCE`) and builds psh-wasm with `--locked`; `scripts/build.mjs` runs wasm-opt -O1 on it and writes dist/psh.wasm; `scripts/wasm.mjs` lists it among the outputs and its inputs in the fingerprint. `sdk-revision.json` does not move: the SDK and psh move independently. The Cloudflare app and the Vercel harness take `workspace.psh`, and `Wasm.psh()` loads the packaged module on Vercel. wasm-opt -O1 takes the module from 7,168,910 to 6,529,675 bytes (gzip 2,419,510 to 2,337,952, brotli 1,688,092 to 1,656,129) and is neutral to slightly faster: awk 204 to 198, sed 169 to 164, sedregex 905 to 846, loop 28.8 to 27.7, jq 87.4 to 88.0 ms.
The psh pin is nanocode master 5e15e341f (2026-11-11), the last of the machine-mode and regex commits as they landed and before the bundled tools: a module built from a later revision carries git, curl and wget as psh utilities, which would run before this package's own git (a GitHub snapshot with clone and commit) and report the network as unavailable, so the pin moves again only together with a `without` list or a Fetch import. Integration recheck on master with the module built from that pin by `node scripts/psh.mjs` (7,169,300 bytes): `api.ts` 26 identical and the 7 explained differences; `differential.ts` psh 628, TypeScript 544 of 727; `machine.ts` over 319 scripts 144 same answer, 104 both refuse, 22 both refuse naming something else, 33 psh refuses where TypeScript answers (20 with the paths outside the workspace answered absent), 11 different answers, 4 TypeScript refuses where psh answers; `npm run check:types` passes. The whole `scripts/build.mjs` was not run and nothing was published or deployed.
Code Mode cells run on djs, the SDK's pure-Rust JavaScript engine, instead of QuickJS (2026-11-12, the owner's decision after the Workers measurement). The five commits from `66d2ce7` to `5a16eb7` land together: the module builds against nanocode 18358c944 with no WASI SDK or rquickjs, `cell_memory_bytes` caps a cell's heap (the Cloudflare App defaults to half of `RUNTIME_MEMORY_BUDGET_BYTES`), the Vercel harness runs the module on the function's own thread, djs-core compiles at opt-level 3, and the module keeps prepared cell snapshots per `WebAssembly.Module` through three host imports (`snapshot_length`, `snapshot_read`, `snapshot_write`), so a session's first cell loads the snapshot from bytes instead of compiling the bootstrap and the prelude again. djs had measured slower on Workers only for a session's first cell, for that reason. Local workerd (`output/harness/workerd`, Miniflare 5.20261110.1-beta, three interleaved rounds of 40 sessions, CPU per session) against master's QuickJS module: an open and a turn with one cell 10.0 to 10.3 ms against 17.0 to 17.2 ms, two cells 13.5 to 13.8 against 21.0 to 21.3; per turn (two rounds) text('ok') 3.67 against 5.27 ms, 16 tool calls 5.8 against 7.1 to 7.2, 20,000 objects through JSON 28.75 against 40.0, the strings cell 14.25 against 16.25 to 16.5. Module 12,786,121 to 13,510,488 bytes. `npm run check:types` and `node scripts/build.mjs` pass; 72 cells printed the same output on both engines except strict mode, stack frames and DOMException (66d2ce7).
Python on the Node worker reads the shell's files on demand (2026-11-12). Each `python3` in Node's default worker mode scanned the working directory and /tmp with one `stat` per file and sent the stamps to the worker before the script started, so a run cost time in proportion to the workspace even when the script read no file. The worker now mounts each root (the working directory, /tmp and the script's directory) as a MEMFS whose directories list their entries and whose files read their bytes through a synchronous request to the main thread the first time Python looks them up or opens them: the worker posts `fs` and waits on a shared flag, the main thread answers from `ctx.fs` over a `MessageChannel`, and the worker takes the answer with `receiveMessageOnPort`. Changed paths come back through the tracking delegate as before and are written back the same way, and the mounts are removed after each run, so every run sees the file system as it is then. A root of `/` or one that holds a Pyodide path keeps the scan and mirror. Measured: `output/pybench/run.ts` (`Bash.create` over a `MemoryFileSystem` with N files of 8,000 bytes in 50 directories, worker mode, Node 24.22.1, Linux hosted Hand), three interleaved rounds against a worktree at the previous commit, five warm runs each, milliseconds: `python3 -c 'print(1)'` with 0 files 1.5-2.3 against 1.4-2.5, with 2,000 files 26-47 to 1.5-3.3, with 10,000 files 123-204 to 1.4-4.3; a `json.load` of a 1,000-row file with 2,000 files 27-39 to 2.7-3.9, with 10,000 files 113-177 to 2.7-6.8; the first run (Pyodide start) 1,569-2,043 against 1,594-1,722. Checked: `npm run check:types` passes; vp fmt and vp lint on both files: no warnings; `output/pybench/edge.ts` (12 scripts: imports from the working directory, scripts in subdirectories, stdin, /tmp, a working directory of `/`, listing, appending, truncating, removing, renaming, recursive glob, a missing file) prints the same output before and after.
The Node Python worker starts Pyodide from a memory snapshot kept beside its package cache (2026-11-12). `loadPyodide` took about 1.5 s of every process's first `python3`. The worker now loads `<cacheDir>/pyodide-<version>.snapshot` with `_loadSnapshot` when the file exists. Otherwise it starts with `_makeSnapshot`, writes `makeMemorySnapshot()` (31,457,408 bytes for 314.0.7) to a partial file and renames it into place, and keeps the same instance. A snapshot that fails to load is a cache miss: the worker starts normally and writes it again. Measured: `output/pybench/run.ts` with `ONLY=print1 N=2` (worker mode, Node 24.22.1, Linux hosted Hand), five interleaved processes against a worktree at the previous commit, first `python3 -c 'print(1)'` in each process: 1,576-1,654 ms to 352-394 ms; the process that writes the snapshot 1,672 against 1,698 ms; warm runs 2.6-5.5 ms on both. Of the rest, the snapshot restore is about 200 ms (`output/pybench/snap3.mjs`). Checked: `npm run check:types` passes; vp fmt and vp lint: no warnings; `output/pybench/edge.ts` prints the same output; numpy imports and `pip install six` then `import six` work on a restored instance.
Python on Pyodide's own thread reads the shell's files on demand when the file system has a synchronous view (2026-11-12). Browsers, Deno and \`worker: false\` run Pyodide on the calling thread, where each \`python3\` copied the working directory and /tmp into MEMFS and compared every file afterwards. \`src/bash/internal/commands/pylazy.ts\` mounts the same lazily filled MEMFS as the Node worker, answered directly from \`FileSystem.sync\`, and records changed paths with the tracking delegate. The shell's operation budget now forwards \`sync\` with the same charge per operation (\`budgetedSync\` in \`Bash.ts\`). A file system without \`sync\` (SqlFileSystem, a wrapper, a umask other than 022) and \`pip\` keep the copy. Two fixes apply to both lazy paths: Emscripten calls \`onMakeDirectory\` before \`mkdir\` checks for an existing directory, so \`mkdirTree(cwd)\` or \`os.makedirs(path, exist_ok=True)\` marked the whole existing tree as changed and read and wrote back every file below it, and a directory that Python removed or renamed away was left behind because \`remove\` refuses a directory with entries (the shared \`apply\` now removes its entries first). Measured: \`output/pybench/run.ts\` (\`worker: false\`, a \`MemoryFileSystem\` with N files of 8,000 bytes, Node 24.22.1, Linux hosted Hand), two interleaved rounds against a worktree at the previous commit, warm runs, milliseconds: \`python3 -c 'print(1)'\` with 2,000 files 115-138 to 1.4-2.1, with 10,000 files 503-567 to 1.3-4.6; a \`json.load\` script with 2,000 files 114-201 to 2.7-4.0, with 10,000 files 507-565 to 2.6-5.9. Worker mode, \`output/pybench/md.ts\` (\`os.makedirs('src', exist_ok=True)\` over 2,000 files), two rounds: 109-169 to 2.0-5.5. Checked: \`npm run check:types\` passes; vp fmt and vp lint: no warnings; \`output/pybench/edge.ts\` prints the same output on the calling thread, and in worker mode differs only where a renamed directory is now removed.

The package builds against nanocode 43e077ff3 and is released as `build-dad8ad0` (2026-11-12). That SDK commit leaves a command's background processes running when the command ends, starts psh through the dynamic loader that started nanocode, and lets a hand that is stopped read on what its commands left running; all three are native code, so for this package the pin brings the Code Mode and Claude changes that reached master before it (the exec arguments, what a nested tool resolves to, a hand catalog's output schemas, the hands list). The release was made as the steps of `scripts/release.mjs` on two machines, because the one that holds the release credential could not reach crates.io or PyPI that day: psh-wasm needs `deser-json`, which its cargo cache did not hold, and `scripts/pyodide.mjs` installs packages through micropip. `node scripts/build.mjs`, `node scripts/publish-wasm.mjs`, `npm run prepare`, `npm run check:types` and `npm pack` ran on a Linux x86_64 machine with both, from fresh clones of dad8ad0 and of the SDK at 43e077ff3 (fingerprint 95ff426077cc404e1828: `nanocode_wasm.wasm` 12,983,885 bytes, `psh.wasm` 6,529,254, `interpreter.wasm` 1,213,360); the outputs and the package were carried to the other machine and compared by SHA-256, then `node scripts/publish-wasm.mjs --upload DIR` and `gh release create` with the script's title and notes ran there. The package is 46.3 MB: 5f8a7ad's Pyodide snapshot is 11 parts of 87.9 MiB beside 26.6 MiB of precompiled modules, where a package without `dist/pyodide` is 8.6 MB. Checked with nanoeval at 073df34, this package as its harness and the hand built from the same SDK commit: its types, unit tests and deploy dry run pass, and twelve of its end-to-end lanes pass under local workerd with a scripted model (directory and microVM slots: tasks, failure kinds, a judge, an orchestrator restart, a runner restart, a scripted gateway, slot networks, closed networks). a13e45f held `sdk-revision.json` with the markers of a merge for about a minute before dad8ad0 replaced it; a build of a13e45f stops at that file. Not checked: a deployed Worker, a model, the Vercel, Node and browser hosts, and the bench.

nanocode_wasm.wasm's -O3 pass inlines a function with one caller only up to 100 in binaryen's size measure (`--one-caller-inline-max-function-size`, unlimited by default) (2026-11-12). Unlimited, -O3 merged chains of single-caller functions into very large ones, and workerd's resident memory after the first turn was about 50 MB higher than with -O1 (the 240 to 300 MB above); with the cap it is back at -O1's. Against SDK 43e077ff3 in Miniflare's workerd on the hosted Hand: resident memory after one turn 214/211 to 161/161 MB and after the bench 321/303/325 to 249/244/245 MB; instructions per turn (`perf stat -e instructions:u` on workerd, three interleaved rounds) for no cell 21.67M to 20.84M, trivial 26.73M to 26.62M, a worker tool 29.17M to 29.41M, sixteen Code Mode tool calls 44.10M to 44.95M, compute 481.1M to 484.1M and strings 217.0M to 217.9M. Most of -O3's gain over -O1 stays: on SDK 130e7f0dc, -O1, -O3 and the cap took 22.0M, 21.7M and 21.0M for no cell, 46.1M, 43.8M and 44.7M for the tool calls and 221.2M, 216.7M and 217.6M for strings. The module is 13,089,003 bytes instead of 12,981,720 and 4,533,964 instead of 4,780,100 with gzip -9. Caps of 400 and 1,500 kept 10 to 25 MB of the extra memory without lower instruction counts.

The package builds against nanocode d4e8af2a3 (2026-11-12). Its module could not be built against the pin before it: 202ec8924 had left nanocode-mcp-tools without its catalog types on WASM (10 errors in a `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` here against d82943831), which d4e8af2a3 puts back. Besides that the SDK changed four things since d82943831, of which two reach this package. exec's refusal of a source that is a JSON wrapper of its input now tells the model how to write that JSON string (each double quote as `\"`, each backslash as `\\`) instead of advising `String.raw`, which a model behind a JSON function cannot follow. And a `wait` call without `yield_time_ms` takes the runtime's default, which a native host can now set (`ToolsBuilder::cell_yield`, `nanocode hand --cell-yield-ms`); this package sets none, so its cells yield after the protocol's 10 s as before. The other two are native code: psh forks for a background job that is not one program, so such a job outlives its command as under bash, and a hand that ends on SIGINT or because its brain ended it leaves a reader for what its commands left running. `Cargo.lock` is unchanged: these SDK commits add no dependency.

`build-061892c` is that package (2026-11-12): WASM fingerprint 8fdb066aa52289fb27f1 (`nanocode_wasm.wasm` 13,091,664 bytes, `psh.wasm` 6,529,273, `interpreter.wasm` 1,213,360), 45,945,695 bytes with `dist/pyodide`. It was made as the steps of `scripts/release.mjs` on two machines, as `build-dad8ad0` was and for the same reason: the machine that holds the release credential reached neither crates.io nor PyPI. `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, `node scripts/build.mjs`, `node scripts/publish-wasm.mjs`, `npm run prepare`, `npm run check:types` and `npm pack --ignore-scripts` ran on a Linux x86_64 machine with both, from fresh clones of 061892c and of the SDK at d4e8af2a3; the package was carried to the other machine and compared by SHA-256, and `node scripts/publish-wasm.mjs --upload PACKAGE.tgz` and `gh release create` with the script's title and notes ran there. Checked with nanoeval main c9af639 and two pins, this package as its harness and the hand built from the same SDK commit (`nanocode-cli-d4e8af2`): every run step of its CI from a fresh clone, 66 of 66, which is its types, its unit tests, the deploy dry run and 58 end-to-end lanes under local workerd with a scripted model on directory, microVM and namespace slots; and on microVM slots four fixture tasks whose solutions a scripted Claude-harness session ran through the hand's Bash tool, then their verifiers. Not checked: a deployed Worker, a model, the Vercel, Node and browser hosts, and the bench.
The psh pin moves to nanocode master 278e7b85a, and the package ships psh-wasm-core (2026-11-12). The module is now ABI 6 (`psh_shell_fetch` and the `K_FETCH` request, which this glue does not use, are the only additions), and since 5e15e341f it carries 37 more utilities that used to be this package's host commands (base32 column dd envsubst expand expr factor fold gunzip gzip hexdump hostname id install join mawk nawk nproc od patch rev shuf split sponge strings tac tar tree truncate uname unexpand unzip uuidgen whoami xxd yes zcat zip) plus nanocode-shell's curl and wget. `scripts/psh.mjs` builds `psh-wasm-core`, the same sources without git, so git stays this package's (the GitHub snapshot with clone); `Bash` puts curl and wget in front of the host's own commands in `without`, so they stay this package's over `options.fetch` (strict mode without `fetch` ends them as before); the other 35 now run in psh, and a host command of the same name still wins. psh_wasm_core.wasm is 6,718,135 bytes against 7,112,222 for 5e15e341f's psh_wasm.wasm (before wasm-opt). Measured with harnesses in output/ (`Bash.create({ psh, fallback: true })` over a MemoryFileSystem at /work, Node 24.22.1, Linux hosted Hand, each module with its own glue): psh's 727 agent cases judged against bash 5.2 run in an empty /work, 606 pass with either module and none regress (the cases that differ between the two print commit hashes and dates from the clock, or run Python, which that machine could not load); 150 scripts of the moved utilities against GNU coreutils 9.4, vim's xxd and util-linux, 8 more match with the new module than with the TypeScript commands (patch -p0 and -p1, a failing hunk, --dry-run, zip's output, join with -, base32 -w 0, a pipeline from /dev/urandom through fold) and none regress; the API cases (timeouts, abort, fuel, output, memory and file-operation limits, stdin, env, host commands over psh utilities, curl and wget with and without fetch, git, strict mode) and machine mode with and without the moved names in `machine.commands` answer as before. Running them found three psh bugs, fixed in nanocode before the pin: `od -N`, `hexdump -n` and `xxd -l` read their whole input before cutting it, so `od -N 4 -An -tx1 /dev/zero` (which the TypeScript od answered) ran into the memory budget, and fold read its whole input (6fbbdf39d); and tr filled its byte table through SipHash maps on every run, which made `x=$(echo $i | tr 1 2)` 50% slower than 5e15e341f's module (278e7b85a). CPU of the psh-bench workloads through the glue, median of three interleaved rounds (TypeScript shell / 5e15e341f / 6fbbdf39d, ms): loop 74.3 / 21.7 / 16.4, strbuild 6.5 / 4.2 / 2.6, sortpipe 112.0 / 15.4 / 9.3, awk 171.7 / 144.3 / 54.8, sed 293.9 / 92.6 / 41.6, cutsort 396.1 / 115.9 / 42.7, grepr 39.7 / 29.3 / 23.4, rg 39.9 / 22.8 / 18.5, catall 40.0 / 16.2 / 10.7, jq 185.1 / 58.8 / 50.6, sortn 100.4 / 33.7 / 31.9, func 36.9 / 13.4 / 11.9, the small ones (echo, view, smallpipe, find, grepc, wcl, catwc) equal or faster, every output identical between the two modules; with 278e7b85a, 10,000 rounds of `x=$(echo $i | tr 1 2)` take 81 to 84 ms against 82 to 88 for 5e15e341f and `seq 1 1000000 | tr 1-5 a-e` 18 to 20 against 119 to 123. Differences left, all psh's: `uname -a` prints `unknown unknown` for the kernel release and version, where the TypeScript uname invented `6.0.0 #1 SMP` (the glue has no field for them); `tar --version`, `gzip --version`, `split --help` and `zip -v` are refused or fail where GNU prints; `command -v tar` answers `tar` and `type tar` 'a psh utility', as for the utilities psh already had; and on a fast machine `while true; do :; done` with a 300 ms timeout reaches the default fuel of 1,000,000 commands first and ends 126, not 124 (gak-9, Apple M1 Max). Checked: `node scripts/psh.mjs` builds the pin from the SDK checkout with `--locked` (gak-9, 1 min 12 s), and that module gives the same output as the Linux build on the 150 tool scripts; `npm run check:types` passes. `scripts/build.mjs` (wasm-opt and dist/) was not run, and nothing was published or deployed.
The psh pin moves to nanocode 4b1fb5fbd (2026-11-12), which brings eleven psh changes measured against upstream psh's spec suites with bash 5.3 goldens (oils 2359 to 2413 of 2511, brush 2843 to 2941 of 3171 plus the 51 coproc cases, agent 727 of 727, utils 1001 of 1001): coproc, extdebug and the DEBUG/RETURN trap details, declare's function attributes and subscript handling, locale-aware quoting in printf %q, declare -p, set and ${x@Q}, unset of a caller's local and of array forms, cd -L/-P and CDPATH, builtins answering --help, `${!a[@]}` with an operator as indirection, and multi-line syntax errors prefixed on every line. The module's ABI goes from 6 to 7 because HostInfo gained the kernel release and version: the glue writes `release` and `version` after `hosttype` (Identity gains both fields), and Bash.ts passes `6.0.0` and `#1 SMP`, the values the TypeScript uname used to invent, so `uname -a` prints `Linux localhost 6.0.0 #1 SMP x86_64 GNU/Linux` again instead of `unknown unknown`. Checked: `node scripts/psh.mjs` builds the pin with `--locked` (gak-9, 1 min 13 s); through the glue with that module, uname -a/-r/-v, a coproc round trip, `declare -fx` with `declare -F` and `export -f`, `printf %q` of an invalid byte, a quoted declaration subscript, `cd --help`, `type cat`, a RETURN trap under set -T and a `[[` syntax error give bash's output; `npm run check:types` and `npx vp check` on the changed files pass. `scripts/build.mjs` (wasm-opt and dist/) was not run, and nothing was published or deployed.

The WASM host commits through the SDK's `Ledger` (2026-11-12), with the SDK pin at nanocode 19e65d550. `src/wasm.rs` keeps one `Ledger<WorkerStore>` per instance (`ledger()`, replaced when broken, which also drops the session logs built on it); the session store, branches, session logs and cell journals all write through it, so a turn's state, events and journal writes share one transaction per durable point. A cell journal save without tool calls and `forget` are deferred; a save with calls stays a transaction, so a call is recorded before it runs. The journal load skips the legacy `nanocode_cell_journals` delete once that table is empty. `HostOptions.log_flush_ms` (`configure`'s `flush_ms`) sets how long committed-later events may wait; the Cloudflare app passes `logFlushMs` (default 1000), and without it every batch commits as before. `progress()` reads both counters in one statement, falling back to two reads if that fails. Measured on workerd with durable-cloudflare 0a0daa9 (`node output/sqlturn/run.mjs text 10`), against 958499b: 21.0 -> 11.0 transactions, 13 -> 11 statements outside transactions, 43.2 -> 32.2 statements and 23.4 -> 19.4 rows written per one-tool-call turn; a plain reply 13.0 -> 9.0 transactions. `npm run drill` in durable-cloudflare passes with the same counts on both builds; `npm run measure` fails at its eviction step with "it still has active references" on both builds, so it was not used.

The WASM host writes directly through `WorkerStore` again, with the SDK pin at nanocode 052f73db2, which removes `Ledger` (2026-11-12). The entry above counted `transactionSync` calls, which are savepoints on workerd: counting event-loop commits, a one-tool-call turn made 5.0 before the ledger and 5.0 with it, and its deferred tool result let a crash during the next model call report a completed charge as unknown (nanocode DECISIONS has the crash matrix). `HostOptions.log_flush_ms` and the Cloudflare `logFlushMs` option go with it. Kept: the cell journal load skips the legacy `nanocode_cell_journals` delete once that table is empty, and `progress()` reads both counters in one statement. Measured with durable-cloudflare 0a0daa9 (`node output/sqlturn/run.mjs text|tool|reply 10`) against 958499b: commits 5.0, 5.0 and 4.0 per turn and rows written 23.4, 24.4 and 14.2 unchanged, statements 43.2 -> 39.2, 44.2 -> 40.2 and 29.1 -> 27.1. Crashing the `charge` turn before each of its 22 commits, with the last commit or the last two lost (`output/crash/run.mjs`, 43 runs), gives the same charges, model calls, statuses and unknown-outcome reports as 958499b at every point, and `npm run drill` passes.

python3 runs on nanocode-python, RustPython built for wasm32 and shipped as `dist/python.wasm` (export `nanocode-ts/python.wasm`), with its interpreter already started and its common stdlib already imported in the module's memory (2026-11-13). `python/` is a cdylib over nanocode-python and nanocode-platform at the nanocode commit `python-revision.json` pins (407831762a); `scripts/python.mjs` takes those crates from `../nanocodex` at that commit with `git archive` (or `NANOCODE_PYTHON_SOURCE`), builds it with `cargo build --locked --release`, and `scripts/build.mjs` runs `wasm-opt -O2` and bakes it: it instantiates the module with an empty filesystem, runs one script that imports about 50 stdlib modules (json, re, os, pathlib, collections, datetime, csv, dataclasses, typing, argparse, hashlib, decimal, ast, inspect and the rest in `warm`), and writes the memory it ends with back as the module's data segments, with the memory's minimum raised to match. Modules that seed from the host (random, tempfile, uuid, secrets) are checked to stay unimported, since a baked seed would repeat in every instance; the stack pointer is the only mutable global, which the bake checks. The host imports are a filesystem call with errno results, the clocks, randomness, a stop flag and a panic message; the module keeps one interpreter per instance. Sizes: 20.0 MB built, 18.1 MB after `-O2`, 35.2 MB baked (11.9 MB gzip, 8.7 MB brotli), of which 23.8 MB is the data section. Pre-importing less is smaller and slower: only the interpreter's own start is 20.4 MB and runs the typical script below in 106 ms, a core set 23.6 MB and 54 ms, the full set 7 ms. `wasm-opt` levels did not change the first run. Measured in Node 24 (`node output/pyrs/t2.mjs`, 5 runs): compile 33-49 ms, instantiate 14-22 ms, first run 35-50 ms (V8 compiling about 850 functions on first call), a second instance's first run 1.4 ms; unbaked, the first run was about 90 ms and the first `import json` 58 ms.

`python3` runs a script on RustPython first, and Pyodide (worker, main thread or the remote) only when the script imports a package RustPython lacks, such as numpy, or for `pip` (2026-11-13). `src/bash/internal/commands/rustpython.ts` instantiates `Python.Options.module` (by default `nanocode-ts/python.wasm` compiled once per process; `false` turns it off), keeps one instance per module, and maps a refused limit to 124 (time) or 126 and a cancel to 130; a trap drops the instance. When a script needs modules that are not in the stdlib and a fallback exists, its stdin is replayed into Pyodide; otherwise the missing modules are reported and the exit is 127. `Python.preload` warms RustPython and loads Pyodide only for named packages. A filesystem without a synchronous view is read on demand: a miss stops the run, the host fetches that path (its stat, listing and children's stats, or contents), discards the run's writes and runs it again, and the writes of the last run are applied after it ends. Scripts run on the calling thread, bounded by the time limit. Measured in Node 24 (`node output/pybench/cold.ts`, 5 alternating fresh processes each): first `python3 -c 'print(1)'` in a process 351-418 ms with Pyodide's worker and its cached snapshot (1,565-1,661 ms on the main thread) -> 124-144 ms, and 11-14 ms after `Python.preload`; later runs 1.6-2.6 -> 0.6-0.7 ms; a typical script (json, re, os, collections, pathlib, `json.load` of 1,000 rows) first 7-10 -> 18-30 ms and later 3.5-7 -> 3.7-8 ms. `node output/pybench/edge.ts` (25 scripts: packages, argv, stdin, file writes, renames, /tmp, glob, exits, exceptions, recursion, `while True`, environment, numpy through Pyodide in 1.1-1.2 s) gives the same output over a synchronous filesystem and with `ASYNC=1`. Known differences from Pyodide: `sys.version` is 3.14.0 alpha, `subprocess` exits 127, `maxMemoryBytes` does not bound the instance's memory, and string hashing uses the same seed in every instance.

The Cloudflare app takes `workspace.pythonModule` (a compiled `python.wasm`, or a promise of one) and passes it to the workspace's `python3`, alongside the remote Pyodide when `workspace.python` gives one (2026-11-13). A Worker cannot compile WebAssembly at run time, so the module comes from the Worker's bundle (`import pythonModule from 'nanocode-ts/python.wasm'`, a CompiledWasm module). Measured in workerd 2026-07-30 under Miniflare with Bash in a Worker (`node output/workerd/drive.mjs`, fresh isolate per cold run, 5 runs): the first `python3 -c 'print(1)'` request 85-113 ms including `Bash.create` and HTTP, later ones 3.9-6.5 ms; the typical script first 23-37 ms, later 9-14 ms. Loading the Worker script takes about 230 ms more with the 35 MB module (78-85 -> 305-324 ms). The Pyodide path this replaces measured 0.87-1.9 s CPU and 3.5-4.0 s wall for a cold `print(1)` in production (entry of 2026-10-27). The 64 MiB Worker limit leaves room for nanocode_wasm (13.5 MB), psh (6.5 MB) and this module (35.2 MB) in one Worker; `App` was type-checked with it, not run in a deployed Worker.

The WebAssembly host keeps one `OneLog` per scope (nanocode `nanocode-turnlog`, `SqlLog` over `WorkerStore`) as its only durable store: turns, events, the checkpoint (`OneLogStore`), cell journals and the effect gate (2026-11-12). `SessionLog`, the `RowStore` checkpoint and src/wasm/journals.rs are removed, and so are the Cloudflare App's per-turn tripwire, background marker and alarm-delete writes. The one alarm rides in the admission tick. Breaks: Durable Objects created before this change start with empty sessions, because `log_turns`, `log_chunks`, `state_rows`, `state_pages` and `nanocode_cell_journal*` are no longer read (the older `jobs` table still migrates through `OneLog::import`). `HostOptions.log_logical_limit` is replaced by `log_restarts`, because the log has no logical event limit and takes its row limit from the SQL host's value limit. Branch export and import need an open session. Measured with durable-cloudflare 0a0daa9 and this package packed (`node output/sqlturn/run.mjs text|tool|reply 10`): 2.0 rows, 3.0 statements and 3.0 event-loop commits per turn for every kind, against 23.4/24.4/14.2 rows, 39.2/40.2/27.1 statements and 5.0/5.0/4.0 commits at 464864c (tool/reply/text). Bound parameters were 14.5/9.0/12.3 KB against 26.9/29.9/10.9 KB. The third commit is the `subagent_directories` cache upsert. In the crash matrix (`output/crash/run.mjs`), losing the commit in flight gives 0 double charges, 1 lost unacknowledged admission, 1 unknown outcome and 2 extra model calls, against 0/1/5/2 before. `npm run drill` passes (charge 2.5 s, waiting 8.2 s, deploys 5.2 s). `npm run build`, `npm run check:types` and `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` pass.

On 2026-11-12 the onelog branch moved to SDK 98cacfd39 (live-only telemetry, event segments, boundary rows, collection at open, snapshot ratio). The bridge passes `snapshot_ratio: 2` to the turn log policy, so a cold open reads at most about twice the state at about 30% more write bytes than ratio 4 in the simulator sweep. `App.remember` keeps the last written subagent directory body for each scope and skips the upsert when the body is unchanged, which removes the third statement of a turn. The durable-cloudflare sqlturn harness measured 2 statements, 2 rows written and 0 rows read per turn in text, tool and reply modes (0.8, 0.9 and 0.5 KB of parameters), and the crash matrix returned the same outcomes as the previous bundle.

On 2026-11-13 master took the onelog branch (3a4c9ac, ceaf569) as one commit, merged with the RustPython work, and moved to SDK 999400a81. That SDK retires finished turns past a caller retention window. `create({ retainedTurns })` passes `log_retained_turns` to `AgentRuntime.instantiate`, and the `configure` command sets the turn log policy's `retain_finished`. If the option is absent, every finished turn stays, as before. A retired request ID is unknown to turn reads, and a new submission with that ID starts a new turn. Its events stay in the history. The window belongs to the deployment because it trades request-ID dedupe and status reads against cold reads. In the SDK simulator, the largest cold read of a scope over 300 turns falls from 59,742 to 12,916 bytes with a window of 8. The durable-cloudflare sqlturn harness measured 2 statements, 2 rows written and 0 rows read per turn in text, tool and reply modes (0.8, 0.9 and 0.5 KB of parameters), and also over 30 text turns with `retainedTurns: 4`. `npm run drill` passes. The crash matrix returns the same outcomes as the onelog bundle, both with and without the window. `npm run build`, `npm run check:types` and `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` pass.

On 2026-11-13 the SDK pin moved to cfd8999c7. Its `OneLogJournals::save` commits a row when a Code Mode cell starts. On build-f1b2093, a Vercel drive killed while a cell waited lost the model step that started the cell, and recovery asked the model for that step again: nanocodexyz/durable-vercel's `npm run drill` failed its four `waiting` runs. A local pack of this commit passes them all. In durable-cloudflare, text and tool turns in `output/sqlturn` write 3 rows instead of 2, and `npm run drill` passes. `npm run build`, `npm run check:types` and `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` pass.

python.wasm moves to nanocode f336d78f3, bounds a run's memory, and on Cloudflare bounds its steps (2026-11-13). Tried in a deployed Worker first (`nanocode-python-probe` on workers.dev, `Bash.create({ python: { module } })` over the build-f1b2093 module, 48 MiB `maxMemoryBytes`, 10 s `timeoutMs`): `print(1)` answered in 1,947 ms on a fresh isolate and 48-90 ms after (client-side, with the round trip); `s = "a " * 30000000; s.split()` trapped (`unreachable`, the instance restarts); `json.loads` of 5,000,000 floats and `while True: pass` ended in "Worker exceeded resource limits" (503), the loop after 37 s, because a Worker's clock does not move while a script runs and the time limit never fired; and the next `python3` panicked with "RefCell already borrowed", since the runtime stopped the instance inside `python_run` and the glue kept it. Changes: the module installs nanocode-python's `Metered` allocator and the request carries `memory`, which `executeRust` sets to `maxMemoryBytes` (a refusal is 126, "it held more memory than this shell allows", and the instance lives on); the request's `steps` comes from `Python.Options.stepsPerMs` times `timeoutMs` (unbounded without it), and the Cloudflare app passes 42,000 steps per ms, the low end of 42,500-50,600 measured in that Worker (`while True: pass` with a step cap of 1e8 and 2e8, five interleaved requests each: 2,197-2,396 and 4,036-4,709 ms against 29-111 ms for 1,000 steps), so a run there stops by its time limit; and an instance found still inside a run when the next begins is dropped and instantiated again. nanocode f336d78f3 keeps no per-instruction location table for code read from frozen modules (decoded from the line table on first use) and counts allocations through one function: dist/python.wasm is 28,256,680 bytes against 35,155,888 (data section 16.7 against 23.6 MB, code 11.55 against 11.48 MB; 11.3 MB gzip, 8.1 MB brotli), and with nanocode_wasm, psh and interpreter a Worker's modules are 48.5 MB against 55.4 MB of the 64 MiB limit. Fourteen failing scripts (json, re, pathlib, strptime, namedtuple, argparse, csv, dataclasses, decimal, generators, `traceback.print_exc`, `format_stack`, `f_lineno`) print the same tracebacks on both modules. Baking fewer stdlib modules was measured and not taken: the core set without typing, dataclasses, decimal, fractions, inspect, ast, argparse, difflib, traceback and the rest is 23.6 MB and `import typing, dataclasses` then costs 151-201 ms on first use (1 ms baked), while `print(1)` and the json/re/pathlib script are unchanged (55-65 and 12-20 ms); in that Worker the module's size did not show in Cloudflare's startup time (5-6 ms with the 35 MB module). Measured in Node 24 (`node output/pyrs/limits.ts`, Linux, `Bash.create` over a MemoryFileSystem, 48 MiB, `stepsPerMs` 42,000): `print(1)` 62 ms first, a 60 MB string and the 5,000,000 floats refused in 4 and 138 ms, an endless `list.append` refused at 909 ms, then `'a b'.split()` answers 2 in 2 ms on the same instance, 200,000 strings within budget 118 ms, `while True: pass` with `timeoutMs` 2000 refused for steps at 909 ms, `sum(range(1000000))` 88 ms; `node output/pyrs/time.mjs MODULE SCRIPT...` (the module alone, three interleaved fresh processes against the build-f1b2093 module): compile 28-37 ms, instantiate 8-12 ms, first `print(1)` 55-65 ms on both. Checked: `node scripts/build.mjs` (6 min 9 s on dev-georgios) and `npm run check:types` pass.

python.wasm moves to nanocode 91a6295a7, is optimized for size, starts subprocesses through psh, and on Cloudflare bounds a run's steps by a CPU slice that the session's Durable Object survives (2026-11-13). On the demo (nanocode-cloudflare-durable at 5cbecd4 with build-81eada9) `while True: pass` reset the session's Durable Object, "exceeded its CPU time limit" at 32.5 s of CPU, because the step budget came from the Bash `timeoutMs` (up to 120 s); with `cpu_ms` raised to 300,000 the same loop ran 93 s and the object reset again, "storage operation exceeded timeout", so a long synchronous run cannot stay in the session object at any CPU limit. `Python.Options.cpuMs` now caps the steps at `min(timeoutMs, cpuMs) × stepsPerMs`, and the Cloudflare app passes `workspace.pythonCpuMs` or half of a Worker's default 30,000 ms CPU limit, so the loop is refused for steps (126) and the session lives on. Handing a run refused for steps to the remote Pyodide object was built (c2318c0) and taken out again: that object cannot stop a running program, so on the demo the loop held it until its own 300,000 ms CPU limit reset it, and the account's next `import numpy` waited and then failed with "Durable Object exceeded its CPU time limit and was reset". `wasm-opt -Oz` instead of `-O2`: dist/python.wasm 27,412,288 bytes against 28,256,680 (code 10.70 against 11.55 MB, 11.2 MB gzip, 8.2 MB brotli), with `print(1)` on a fresh instance at 41-52 against 51-62 ms and a 2,000,000-iteration loop at 585-623 against 562-598 ms (three interleaved rounds of `output/pyrs/time.mjs`, Node 24, dev-georgios), within noise. Subprocess: the module's `Files::spawns` answers what the request says and `spawn` calls a new import, `host_spawn`, with argv, environment, cwd, stdin and whether stderr merges; `executeRust` answers it with `Psh.runNow`, which drives a shell of the psh instance the Bash tool already loaded to its end synchronously over the same `SyncFileSystem` (the layered cache too, so a child sees the program's writes and its own writes are deferred and applied with them), with the session's limits and `Psh.identity` (the identity Bash.ts gave psh, now shared), no host commands, and `curl` and `wget` left out. A missing program is ENOENT (`FileNotFoundError`), a child that would wait (`sleep`, a host command) is EAGAIN, a cancelled one EINTR, and one that passes the shell's limits exits 126 with "it ran more than this shell allows". Embedding nanocode-shell in python.wasm instead, as the native host does, was built and not taken: 34.2 MB, code 16.65 MB, for a second copy of psh. Measured with `output/run.ts` (Bash over a MemoryFileSystem with the psh module, Node 24): `subprocess.run(['echo','hi'], capture_output=True)`, `check_output('ls; grep -c hello a.txt', shell=True)`, `wc -l`, `sh -c 'echo err >&2; exit 3'` (stderr and status 3), `cat` with `input=`, `os.popen`, a pipeline `seq 3 | sort -r | head -2` and a child that writes a file Python then reads behave as on Linux; `['nosuchcmd']` raises FileNotFoundError; 100 `subprocess.run(['true'])` take 27-37 ms; with `sync` hidden the same cases and `statistics.mean` (nanocode 91a6295a7) answer. `node output/pyrs/limits.ts` matches the entry above (print(1) 61 ms, the three memory refusals, `while True` refused for steps at 721 ms). Checked: `node scripts/build.mjs` and `npm run check:types` pass.

The Cloudflare app's Python CPU slice is 5,000 ms by default (`workspace.pythonCpuMs`), not half of a Worker's 30,000 ms (2026-11-13). Measured on the demo (nanocode-cloudflare-durable with build-6e2c6c2, `while True: pass` through the agent's Bash): at 15,000 ms the session's Durable Object was reset with "Durable Object storage operation exceeded timeout" (13:30 UTC) and the turn lost its Bash result; at 10,000 ms the run was refused for steps (126) after 8,609 ms of CPU and 16,288 ms of wall time and the object lived on; at 5,000 ms it was refused and the turn answered in 8.5 s. A storage write the session made before the script cannot settle while the script holds the isolate, and its timeout, not the CPU limit, is what a long slice meets first; 5,000 ms keeps half of the slice that was measured to survive. A host that runs Python where nothing waits on storage can raise it.

`bench/python/score.ts` is the scoreboard of the nanocode-python hill climb (2026-11-13, Georgios: run the top compiled Python packages on our interpreter with no Pyodide fallback). For each of 18 packages (charset-normalizer, contourpy, cryptography, kiwisolver, lxml, markupsafe, matplotlib, msgpack, numpy, orjson, pandas, pillow, pydantic-core, pyyaml, regex, scikit-learn, scipy, ujson; manylinux cp313 wheels in `$CAPI/x`, default ~/rpw/capi), it copies the wheel's Python files into a MemoryFileSystem and imports each compiled module by name in one python3 run, counting only imports whose module is not a `.py` file, since charset-normalizer and lxml ship a pure-Python copy beside some compiled modules and the first count took those as 3 successes. It also reports the C API symbols implemented of the 538 the 18 packages' extensions import (`freq.txt`), the module's bytes, the registry crates in python/Cargo.lock beyond the async stack and serde, and four limit checks (print, a 60 MB split refused for memory, `while True` refused for steps, a json traceback). Baseline on master (dist/python.wasm of build-6e2c6c2's inputs): 0 of 274 compiled modules, 0 of 18 packages, 0 of 538 symbols, 27,406,971 bytes, 37 external crates (bytes, displaydoc, hashbrown, indexmap, itoa, libc, memchr, smallvec, wasi, windows-sys, the ICU set and its zerovec, yoke, tinystr and writeable crates, rustls-pki-types, zeroize and the rest), limits 4 of 4. Run: `node bench/python/score.ts [module]`.

The nanocode pin moves to 5f299dbce, and `bench/sla` measures nanocode's latency against a local streaming mock and live providers (2026-11-13, nanocode 1.0 latency SLA, Georgios). The pin brings nanocode-http's connection reuse (a dropped response whose message ended returns its connection to the pool, where every model request had opened a new one), preconnect at session start and when typing starts, and `NANOCODE_ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`; nanocode's SLA.md holds the bounds and the full measurements. `bench/sla/fixture.ts` serves the Anthropic Messages, OpenAI Responses and chat APIs; a prompt containing `SLA[run=,turn=,tokens=,rate=,ttft=,tool=]` picks the reply (tokens, a paced rate in tok/s or 0 for unpaced, a delay before the first token, a tool call first), and `/_log?run=` returns per request the arrival, headers, first and last token and end times in ns, the connection's ID, whether it was new and its age. `bench/sla/run.ts` drives each lane in a terminal under `script -qfec` (answering terminal queries), types the prompt and waits for the turn's first and last marks, which use letters no other turn uses so a diffing renderer cannot hide them; lanes are `nc`, `nc-claude`, `claude`, `codex`, another nanocode build as `nc@NAME` (`NC_BIN_NAME`), `live-*` on the real providers with the real home, and `rejected-*` against real endpoints with a bogus key; modes are `tui`, `exec` and `resume` (kill -9 mid-stream, then `--continue`). It writes `<label>.jsonl` and a p50 / p99 table to `REPORT.md` in `--out` (default `output/bench/sla`). `bench/sla/ts.ts` runs the AgentRuntime on Node against the same mock. Measured on dev-georgios with this pin's build, 3 sessions of 3 turns: overhead (run to request at the mock plus first event) turn 1 4.89 / 67.18 ms p50 / p99, turn 2 3.80 / 6.25, turn 3 3.37 / 3.90; turn 1's p99 is the first runtime of a process (wasm compile and undici's first connection, about 27 ms each). Checks: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json`, `tsc -p tsconfig.cloudflare.build.json` and `npm run check:types` pass; `scripts/pyodide.mjs` was not run, since the host cannot reach PyPI.

`maxConcurrentSubagents` (Cloudflare `App.create`, `max_concurrent_subagents` in `AgentConfiguration` for any host) bounds how many of a session's subagents run at once, and spawns past it queue instead of starting (2026-11-13, Georgios, through the Nanocode 1.0 durability track: 100K subagents in one session and a fast restart of a crashed swarm on every store). It calls the SDK's `SubagentControl::set_max_concurrency` and `set_queued_spawns(true)`, as native hosts already could; without it the SDK default (`DEFAULT_MAX_SUBAGENTS`, unbounded) starts every spawned child at once inside one Durable Object. Measured with nanocode-cloudflare-durable's local workerd (`scripts/measure-swarm.mjs`, a root whose one Code Mode cell spawns N children in batches of 50, each child calling `submit_result` against a mock Messages API, SDK c14de6c2d): 3,000 children unbounded peaked at 2,618 MB workerd RSS with all of them in flight, and a restart of the object listed them in 4.2 s with its first model call 4.6 s after the restart; with 64 at a time, a restart after 1,024 had started (1,014 finished) took 0.47 s to serve, 2.1 s to list 3,000 children and 2.5 s to its first model call, re-ran inference for 12 children (the ones in flight) and finished all 3,000 with 1.32 rows and 3.5 KB of bound SQL values written per child, 9.3 KB of SQLite per child. The bound does not make 100K children fit one object: WebAssembly memory still grows about 250 KB per finished child whether or not finished children stay resident (`maxResidentSubagents: 0` grew the same, 378 MB after 1,731 of 2,000), so a Durable Object's 128 MB holds a few hundred finished children and wasm32's 4 GiB about 15,000; that retention is the next thing to find.

The nanocode pin moves to ef2a8f5e3, and bench/sla gains live Claude lanes with an API key and runs behind an egress proxy (2026-11-13, nanocode 1.0 latency SLA, Georgios: measure live Claude against Claude Code, make the first turn as fast as later ones, and optimize Workers). The pin brings nanocode 61dfe4e8e, whose session event pump no longer holds a reply's first delta for `event_coalesce_ms`: in durable-cloudflare's local workerd measurement (its `scripts/measure-sla.mjs`, `EVENT_COALESCE_MS` 100) the provider's first token reached a `?live=1` client 96 to 101 ms after it was sent on warm turns before and 1.2 to 2.6 ms after, the first delta 111 to 118 ms after the POST before and 10 to 20 ms after; and ef2a8f5e3, which warms the first request's tool runtime and system text when a native session preconnects. `bench/sla/run.ts --key` gives the `rejected-*` lanes `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` from the environment instead of a bogus key and waits for the reply's mark instead of a 401 (Claude Code's approved key list takes the key's last 20 characters); every lane passes on `HTTPS_PROXY`, `HTTP_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, their lowercase forms, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` and `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY` when set, without which no lane reached a provider from a hosted Hand; `SLA_TRACE=<file>` appends each turn's Enter and request-arrival times (monotonic ns) for matching against a profile, and the fixture's `DUMP` writes whole request bodies. Live on the hosted Hand (claude-haiku-4-5, its egress proxy, 8 sessions of 3 turns, Enter to first token drawn, p50): nanocode at 5f299dbce 477, 439 and 446 ms, nanocode without 5f299dbce's connection changes 875, 1,071 and 848, Claude Code 2.1.318 1,844, 813 and 799. Checks: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json`, `tsc -p tsconfig.cloudflare.build.json` and `npm run check:types` pass at this pin; `scripts/pyodide.mjs` was not run, since the host cannot reach PyPI.
- Pin nanocode f4398902f, where Code Mode's local toolset shares one copy of its nested tool metadata between sessions with the same tools instead of a copy per cell that every finished subagent kept (2026-11-13, nanocode 1.0 durability, Georgios: 100K subagents in one Durable Object under 128 MB). Measured with durable-cloudflare's `scripts/measure-swarm.mjs` in local workerd (3,000 children spawned in batches of 50 by one root cell, each calling submit_result against a mock Claude, `maxConcurrentSubagents: 64`), this pin's release wasm against the same tree with the previous `local.rs`, interleaved twice: wasm linear memory at the end 494 and 799 MB before, 224 and 233 MB after; peak workerd RSS 1,650 and 1,667 MB before, 1,252 and 1,271 MB after; settled in 60.9 and 63.8 s before, 59.4 and 58.5 s after; SQL bytes per child 9,308 and 9,190 before, 9,179 and 9,183 after. A 1,000-child run killed at 500 finished children resumes and converges: ready 0.45 s after the restart, listing 1,000 children 1.30 s, first model call 1.71 s. It does not reach the goal: about 75 KB of linear memory per finished child remains, most of it the OneLog `Fold`, which holds every content-addressed state value of the Durable Object in memory (about 19 KB per child in a fold report of an instrumented build). Checks: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `npm run build:ts`, `tsc -p tsconfig.cloudflare.build.json` and `npm run check:types` pass at this pin.

The event hub's tail holds stored events only, and an event stream never asks for a page it has sent (2026-11-13). Since the WebAssembly host moved to `OneLog`, the SDK hands the events its log does not store (`model.call.completed`, `model.attempt.failed`, `model.attempt.retrying`, `model.call.failed`, `model.connection.*`, `model.warmup.*`, `api.event`) to `published` with `seq` 0, inside the batch of stored events they belong to: `LogSink::persist` passes what `OneLog::record` returns to `InboxHost::publish`. `EventHub.publish` appended such a batch to the tail when its first event followed the highest stored seq, and `recent` searches the tail by seq and answers `next_cursor` from its last event. A batch that ends in an event numbered 0 after stored ones is what a model stream that fails after streaming leaves: the flushed `reasoning.summary.delta` and `assistant.delta`, then `model.attempt.retrying`. With the tail [.., 16394, 16395, 16396, 0], `recent(16394)` answers [16395, 16396, 0] with `next_cursor` 16394, so `stream` sent the same three frames on every pull and its reader got a chunk on every read. A reader in the same isolate that reads until it sees an event it waits for (a host that holds a turn by its `result`) then never returns to the event loop: no timer, alarm or request runs, the retry of the model call 0.5 s later is never sent, and memory grows until the isolate is stopped. Seen in a host on `wrangler dev` twice in one day, each time after the provider ended a response stream early: workerd at 100-110% of a core, 1.7 and 2.3 GB resident, no answer on any route. Reproduced with a scripted model that ends its second response after one thinking delta and a few text deltas (4.5 KB): about 20,000 pulls a second, resident memory 185 MB to 1 GB in 44 s and 2.4 GB in 140 s, the same for a response of 484 KB; with the same batch the hub of build-061892c loops as well, but its log numbers every event, so nothing arrives with 0. Not every such batch loops: with one stored event before the 0 and the same tail the search ends past it and `recent` answers nothing, which hides that event from stream readers until the tail is replaced. `publish` now keeps events with a seq above 0 for the tail, the highest seq and the streams it settles; an event numbered 0 still reaches the host's `published` and wakes listeners, as before, and was never sent to a WebSocket watcher (`deliver` drops a seq at or below the cursor). A stream reader no longer receives an event numbered 0: before, it received one as a frame with `id: 0` when the batch joined the tail and the search reached it. `stream` moves its cursor past every event of a page it has sent and never back. Checked at build-81eada9: `tsc` with the options of tsconfig.build.json on src/EventHub.ts gives that build's dist/EventHub.js byte for byte before the change and the same declarations after it; with the changed file in place of the package's, the host that hung retries the model call 0.6 s after the early end and finishes its turn, for the 4.5 KB and the 484 KB response. Checked on this commit (Node 24.22, Linux): `npm run check:types` and `npm run build:ts` pass, and `node output/eventhub/harness.ts FILE` drives `EventHub.create` with the batches [1, 2], [3, 0, 4], [5], a batch of [0] alone first and later, [n+1, n+2, 0] with a reader from n, a byte budget, a late reader that pages through a log, and a `live` reader. On the changed file and on its dist/EventHub.js 24 of 24 checks pass. On the file before the change 11 of 24 pass: `recent(0)` answers [4, 5] after the first three batches, a batch of [0] alone empties the tail, and the reader from n pulled 44,726 chunks in 0.6 s while a 50 ms timer never fired (the harness stops it there). Not run: `node scripts/build.mjs` (the change has no Rust), a deployed Worker, a WebSocket watcher.


`sdk-revision.json` moves to nanocode 3954a7a46 (2026-11-13), whose tools answer a catalog revision so a Responses session prepared at preconnect is reused by the first turn when the workspace, config, model and catalog have not moved (bench/sla first turn 2.59 to 2.04 ms p50 against 5df4a7bac, 16 sessions, request bodies byte-identical). `scripts/tsbuild` on dev-georgios: metadata, WebAssembly, `tsc` and type declarations build. The release before this pin, build-1a597dd, is deployed as durable-cloudflare 7c43bb3 (version b1bdc8aa); from gak-9 its Claude warm turns reach response headers in 196 to 270 ms and the first delta in 738 to 1,574 ms, of which the provider's own start is 566 to 774 ms, and a cold Durable Object's first delta takes 2.8 to 4.2 s, mostly its first provider request (1.6 to 1.8 s against 0.6 to 0.8 s warm). Not run: a deployed Worker on this pin; its release needs this commit on main.
- `App.create({ remoteSubagents: true })` runs each subagent in its own Durable Object behind the spawn_agent, wait_agent and list_agents tools, so the parent session keeps a SQL row per child instead of the child's registry entry, session, turn log state and Code Mode journals (2026-11-13, nanocode 1.0 durability, Georgios: 100K subagents in one Durable Object under 128 MB).
