With the option the session runs without nanocode's subagent registry (`subagents: false`) and offers host tools of the same names. spawn_agent inserts a row into `remote_agents` and posts the task as the first turn of the session `<parent>/agent-<id>` through `SESSIONS`, with `remote_parent` naming the parent; that child session gets the same tools plus submit_result, which stores its output. When the child's turn finishes it stores the notice in its metadata and posts it to the parent's `/internal/remote_done`, retrying from its alarm until the parent answers; the parent records the status and output, wakes wait_agent, and when no turn is running submits one turn for every child finished since the last, `subagent-completions-<last id>`, that names the agent IDs as ranges and the ones that did not complete, with the results left to list_agents and wait_agent. Measured in local workerd with durable-cloudflare's `scripts/measure-swarm.mjs` (a root cell spawns N children in batches of 50, each calls submit_result against a mock Claude; with `RELEASE=1` the root runs one more cell, so the spawn cell's journal is released as it would be in a longer session), at this tree on nanocode f4398902f: the parent's live wasm heap (a counting allocator in a build not committed) was 2.1 MB with 20 children, 2.87 MB with 250, 4.42 MB with 500 and 3.67 and 4.67 MB with 1,000, where the same swarm in one session grows 38 KB per child (48.9 to 139.2 MB from 512 to 2,880 finished children); its linear memory was 6.5, 9, 10 and 13 MB, against 224 and 233 MB for 3,000 children in one session. The first model call of a single child came 127, 146, 140, 142 and 182 ms after the spawn cell started, against 139, 134, 160, 167 and 180 ms in one session. Bursts start slower locally, where one workerd thread starts every child object: 20 children 549 to 667 ms against 213 to 264, 1,000 children 58 to 70 s to the first child call against 0.6 to 1.0 s, and a kill at 150 of 300 children took 6.96 and 7.7 s to list the children and 7.4 and 8.2 s to the next model call after the restart, against 0.64 and 1.2 s, while the parent itself was ready in 486 and 598 ms against 633; every child completed in each run. 1,500 child objects ended the local workerd on a 14 GB Hand, so 100K is an extrapolation from these sizes, not a run. Each child keeps its own SQLite database: 176 to 250 KB of local disk per child against 12 to 56 KB in one session, with 1.34 SQL rows written per child in the parent. Not offered: send_agent_message, interrupting or closing a child, budgets and lineage; harness, model, thinking and reasoning_mode are accepted and ignored, the output contract reaches the child as text, and finished child objects are kept. Node has no Durable Objects, so the option is Cloudflare's alone. Weighed and not landed: moving the turn log's content-addressed state values from the fold into a SQL table read on demand (nanocode branch `durability-log-values`) halved one session's live heap per child (18 against 38 KB) and its linear memory (128 against 224 and 233 MB at 3,000 children), but raised SQL bytes per child from 9.2 to 44 KB and rows from 1.06 to 24.7, slowed the 3,000-child swarm from 59 to 72.5 s, and left the parent of remote children where it was (5.01 against 5.02 MB at 1,000). Checks: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `npm run build:ts` and `npm run check:types` pass at nanocode f4398902f, and `tsc -p tsconfig.cloudflare.json --noEmit` passes on master at nanocode 3954a7a46.

A WebSocket watcher is behind by the first stored event of a batch (2026-11-13). `published` in src/cloudflare/App.ts skipped a watcher whose cursor was below `events[0].seq - 1` and started a catch-up read for it. The SDK passes the events its log does not store with `seq` 0 inside the batch they belong to, in the order of emission, so a batch can start with one. On nanocode 3954a7a46 the Claude backend emits `model.call.completed` in `request_response`, and after it `assistant.message` and `tool.call`. `LogSink::route` flushes only held reasoning text before `model.call.completed`, so a response without reasoning text gives a persisted segment that starts with that event. `OneLog::recorded` numbers it 0 and the events after it from the log, and `InboxHost::publish` passes the batch to `published` unchanged. For a batch [0, s, s+1] the test was `cursor < -1`, which is never true. A watcher below s - 1 then received s and s+1, its cursor moved to s+1, and `deliver` dropped the events before s when the catch-up page arrived, so the socket never received them. A watcher is in that state between `watch` and the answer of the page read that `flushWatchers` starts for it, and after a page read that failed. `published` now takes the first event of the batch with a `seq` above 0, and a watcher whose cursor is below that `seq` minus 1 is behind. A batch of events numbered 0 alone marks no watcher behind and moves no cursor, as before. The other readers of a `seq` in src/ were read again and need no change: `EventHub.publish`, `recent` and `stream` (the entry on the event hub's tail above), `deliver`, `lived`, `flushWatchers`, and the read loops of `events` and `subagentEvents`, which read stored events only. One reader is left as it is: `Transport.ts` in src/cloudflare and `Cloud.ts` in src/machines set the cursor of a session to the `id` of every frame, so a frame with `id: 0`, which only a server before that entry sends, moves the cursor back to 0 until the next stored frame. Checked on this commit (Node 24.22, Linux): `npm run check:types` passes, and `node output/watcher-gap/harness.ts FILE` compiles `published`, `deliver`, `deliverLive`, `liveCursor`, `notifyWatchers`, `flushAgain` and `flushWatchers` as FILE has them into a class with a stub socket, a stub log, a stub `ctx.waitUntil` and the real `EventHub`. With a watcher at cursor 10, a log up to 14 and the batch [0, 15, 16], the socket received 15 and 16 on the file before the change, with the page read in flight and with none, and 11 to 16 in order on the changed file. The batches [0, 0], [11, 0, 12] and [0, 11, 12] for a watcher at 10, and [15, 0, 16] for a watcher at 10 behind a log up to 14, give the same frames, cursors and page reads on both files. 18 of 25 checks pass before the change and 25 of 25 after it. Not run: a Durable Object, a WebSocket, the WebAssembly module, a deployed Worker, and `node scripts/build.mjs` (the change has no Rust).


`sdk-revision.json` moves to nanocode 6638af866 (2026-11-13): the Claude Messages stream reads its response to the end after `message_stop`, so a native session's next turn reuses the connection instead of opening a new one (live claude-haiku-5-5 through the hosted Hand's egress proxy, interleaved debug builds, 3 rounds of 3 sessions: turn 2 p50 845 to 667 ms, turn 3 1,022 to 690), and a Tools catalog revision now covers an embedded Code Mode host and answers on WebAssembly, so a Worker session's first Responses turn can reuse the runtime and request factory its preconnect built. bench/sla's live prompt carries the run's id, so live sessions no longer send byte-identical turns that read other sessions' prompt-cache entries. Checked: `cargo check --locked --lib` of nanocode-tool-host and nanocode-backend-claude-client for the host and `wasm32-unknown-unknown`; this repository's full build runs with its release.

- A turn's result timing on Cloudflare lists the model requests the runtime started during the turn, `model_requests`, each with its provider (null for an MCP or other non-model request), the endpoint's origin and the existing timing fields (2026-11-13, Georgios: cut the Worker's cold first delta). `started_ms` there counts from the turn's admission. A new session's first turn sends 7 requests against 1 for later turns, and its `host.http_start` sum (1.6 to 6.8 s on build-e919e54, deployed as durable-cloudflare version a9209e2f) does not show which of them the first token waits on. Checked: `npm run check:types`.

- A new Cloudflare session's first turn waits on its MCP servers before its model request starts, and `/warm` removes that wait (2026-11-13, measured with `model_requests` on build-4ed9bef, durable-cloudflare version a7616016, from gak-9, a "Say hi" turn). Without `/warm` the first turn sends each of the demo's two MCP servers (its gateway and mercator.sh) `initialize`, then `notifications/initialized` and `tools/list`. The model request starts only when they finish, because opening the runtime awaits `mcp::tools` and the first request needs the tool definitions. It started 427 to 1,814 ms after admission for Claude (3 sessions) and 611 to 1,108 ms for Codex (2), against 21 to 245 ms on second turns. The cold model request then took 1,189 to 2,383 ms to headers for Claude and 638 to 4,465 for Codex, against 950 to 4,601 and 689 to 722 on the second turns of the same sessions. So the provider path was slow and variable that hour on later turns too, and `MODEL_PRIME_IDLE_MS` already primes it. With `POST /warm` 2 s before the first turn, as the web client sends it when someone starts typing, the model request started 86 to 103 ms after admission for Claude (4 sessions) and 45 to 100 ms for Codex (3). Its headers took 817 to 1,216 and 616 to 1,211 ms, the same as later turns (714 to 2,033 and 534 to 1,377). The probe in SLA.md sends no `/warm`, so its cold numbers are an API client's that never warms. Such a client still waits for the MCP handshakes. Removing that wait means starting the first request from a stored tool list before the handshakes end, so a server that changed its tools would answer one request with stale definitions.

- A request `Mcp.defer` answers itself carries `x-nanocode-mcp-source` (`memory`, `store <ms>`, or `origin, store <ms>` for an `initialize` sent to the server after a store read that found nothing), and AgentRuntime copies it into that request's timing `diagnostic` (2026-11-13). A new session's first turn on the deployed demo waited 230 to 1,253 ms for `initialize` although `defer` serves stored listings, and `model_requests` did not show whether the store missed, was slow, or the server is stateful (no header). Checked: `npm run check:types`.
`cellYieldMs` (Cloudflare `App.create`, Vercel `Harness.create`, `cell_yield_ms` in `AgentConfiguration` for any host) sets how long a Code Mode cell runs before it yields when its call names no `yield_time_ms`, and `sdk-revision.json` moves to nanocode 439011f97 (2026-11-13, Georgios: make a longer default cell yield work end to end). The WebAssembly host passes the value to `ToolsBuilder::cell_yield`. Since 439011f97 a brain with a cell yield writes it into every Execute and Wait that names none, in the `// @exec:` pragma and in the arguments of wait, so a hand released before that commit yields then. The SDK's deadline for the operation and the deadline that `recordHandRequests` stores both read the yield from the frame, so both are `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` plus the yield. The exec description, the Claude exec schema, and the description and schema of wait state the number. The reason is a host whose cells build for minutes: with the protocol's 10 s the model spends one request for each `wait`. A hand started with `nanocode hand --cell-yield-ms 120000` did not help such a host, because the brain named no yield and stopped the operation after `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` ("remote operation deadline elapsed" after 30 s). Without the option nothing is set and a cell yields after 10 s. One difference comes with the pin and not with the option: a remote client with an operation timeout now names 10000 in an Execute or Wait that names none. The frame then carries the pragma, both deadlines for that call are 10 s later, and the `--cell-yield-ms` of a hand no longer applies to the cells of a Worker. The pin brings two nanocode commits, de7f3d76f (documentation) and 439011f97. Measured in a host on `wrangler dev` with `REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS` 30000, a scripted Messages endpoint and the hand released as nanocode-cli-3f97ab0. The cell is `await tools.Bash({ command: "echo started; /bin/sleep 45; ..." })`. With `cellYieldMs: 120000` the exec returns "Script completed" after 45.0 s in one call, and the task takes 2 model requests. The endpoint reads "default 120000 ms" in the exec description and "Defaults to 120000 ms" in the exec schema and in the description and schema of wait. Without the option the exec returns "Script running" after 11.0 s, four waits follow, and the task takes 6 model requests. With the hand started with `--cell-yield-ms 120000` and no option, the cell also yields after 11.0 s and no call fails. Checks: `node scripts/build.mjs` (WASM build ddf130637a2713aa9c6b), `cargo clippy --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings`, `npm run check:types`, `npm run build:ts`, and `npm run check`, which changes none of the touched files. Not run: the option through the Vercel harness, the option with local cells, and a deployed Worker.

- A Cloudflare session admitting a turn with no resident runtime starts a read of its MCP store (`nanocode-mcp:prime`) beside the model prime (2026-11-13). On the deployed demo every new session's MCP `initialize` was answered from the store, `store 409` to `store 1,569` ms, so the wait is the session's first call to the shared `McpListings` object, not the MCP servers. The read starts that call while the runtime opens (about 0.5 s). Checked: `npm run check:types`.

- On the deployed demo (build-2672c04, 2026-11-13, from gak-9) the store read at admission shortened the `McpListings` read to 152 to 523 ms, from 409 to 1,569. A host whose `mcp.store` is Workers KV reads in 3 to 65 ms: nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare moved the demo to KV (its DECISIONS.md), and a new session's first model request starts 146 to 534 ms after admission. The read at admission stays, since `McpStore` is any host's store.

- `remoteSubagents` runs each subagent in its own Durable Object behind the parent's own nanocode registry, replacing bb19ba0's host-side registry (2026-11-13, Georgios: route child execution to separate Durable Objects with full parity). The parent's runtime takes `children` (a `ChildHost`), so `spawn_agent`, `wait_agent`, `list_agents`, `send_agent_message` (urgent steers and deferred turns), `interrupt_agent`, `close_agent`, budgets, lineage, usage and completion notices are the SDK's, as for in-object subagents. A child object opens with `subagents: false`, runs turns posted to `/internal/child_turn`, forwards its non-delta events and every delegation tool call (a `wire` tool) to the parent, and keeps its last finished turn as a `child_notices` entry, posted to the parent until it answers `delivered` and dropped when a later turn starts. After a parent restart a resumed child's turn asks `/internal/child_pending` for its unfinished or last finished request and runs under that request ID, and the parent's log commits durable journal writes at once (`OneLog::settle_durably`), so a finished child is not run again: 40 children killed 8 s after all finished make no model call after the restart. Pins nanocode 1ec87b2d0. Measured on local workerd with the mock Claude of durable-cloudflare scripts/measure-swarm.mjs (hosted Hand, one run each, remote against in-object): parity transcripts per actor match except `wait_agent` showing an output in-object had delivered; 200 children all done 17.4 s against 6.1 s, parent WebAssembly memory 17.8 MB against 37.6 MB, parent SQL rows per agent 1.34 against 2.24, child first call p50 7.5 s against 2.0 s (one workerd hosts all 201 objects); 300 children killed at 150: 300 first child calls against 305, restart ready 0.51 s, list 3.6 s against 1.3 s. Each child object has its own workspace, unlike in-object children, which share the parent's. Checked: `npm run check:types`, `cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `node scripts/build.mjs`.

- A remote child forwards only its logged events (`seq` above 0) to the parent, so its telemetry (`api.event`, model call, attempt, connection and warmup events, which the parent's log does not store either) stays in the child object (2026-11-13). The parent publishes what it would have stored for an in-object child. On the Tempo account's `nanocode-swarm` Worker (Claude Haiku 5.5, low effort, durable-cloudflare scripts/swarm-live.mjs) 1,000 remote children completed in 199 s, the parent's WebAssembly memory peaked at 74 MB, `GET /subagents` answered in 0.5 to 1.7 s, and the children's model cost was $0.46 (20.9 M input tokens, 19.1 M of them cached). A root cell that polled `wait_agent` with 256 IDs per call held 156 to 223 MB and reset the parent object ("exceeded its memory limit") at 1,000 children, and on local workerd the same cell held 208 MB with 500 in-object children too, so the polled results, not the remote children, filled it; 8 IDs per call held 60 to 74 MB.

- The SDK pin moves to nanocodexyz/nanocode 7a39104, where `OneLogJournals::save` commits a Code Mode cell's first save only when the cell arms a timer before another row commits, instead of at every cell start as cfd8999c7 did (2026-11-14). Rows per turn on durable-cloudflare's local workerd (`output/turns.mjs`, mean of 5 turns) with a local pack of this tree against build-2672c04: reply 2 and 2, a `text("ok")` cell 2 against 3, a timer cell 3 and 3, a `tools.charge` cell 3 against 4. `Journal.committed` in durable-vercel shows the same transactions per turn (2, 2, 3, 3 against 2, 3, 3, 4 on build-90e28af) and 3.4 KB of journal for a cell turn against 4.4 KB. Checked: durable-vercel `npm run drill` (8 of 8 runs) and durable-cloudflare `npm run drill` (3 of 3 scenarios) with that pack, `npm run check:types`. The `npm run build` of the pack ran on an Apple Silicon Mac.

- The one log writes a snapshot once 64 rows follow the last one (`Policy.snapshot_rows: Some(64)` in `log` in src/wasm.rs), on the SDK that adds the row rule, the reverse snapshot search in the load query and turn pages that start at the turn's first event (2026-11-14, Georgios: bound the rows a cold open reads). Without it, a durable-cloudflare session read every row of its log twice at a cold open, because the byte rule writes no snapshot when the log is the conversation: 812 to 828 rows from the log statement after 203 turns. On local workerd (durable-cloudflare `output/turns.mjs`, `TURNS=1`, one run per length, 3 cold opens each, rows of the cold turn plus one warm turn), the cold open reads 100/108/116 rows after 8 turns (unchanged), 124/132/140 after 46 turns against 252/260/268, and 102/110/118 after 203 turns against 880/888/896. The value is host policy taken from that run and a run with 32: 32 reads 90/98/106 rows after 203 turns but stores 1,734,602 bytes of snapshots beside 281,806 bytes of rows, and 64 stores 912,163 beside 275,515. Snapshots stay in the log, so stored bytes grow with the square of the session length over the limit. Warm turns are unchanged (reply 2 rows, cell 2, timer 3, charge 3, 0 read). Checked: durable-cloudflare `npm run drill` (3 of 3 scenarios, no failures) with a dry-run bundle of build-0624213 whose nanocode_wasm.wasm is this tree built with `cargo build --profile wasm` and `wasm-opt -O3` as scripts/build.mjs runs them. No `npm run build` or pack ran: the TypeScript and the other modules are the release's.
- `/metrics` reports `requests` (requests the session object served) and `child_requests` (requests it sent to remote child objects), so a harness can score the requests per child turn and the peak request rate that one parent object meets against the platform's single-object request limit (2026-11-13, Georgios: the 10K-agent hill-climb metrics). durable-cloudflare scripts/hillclimb.mjs reads them. At 200 children and a 200 call/s mock provider, a remote parent served 7.1 requests and sent 1.0 per child, and an in-object parent served 0.06. Checked: `npm run check:types`.

- A session with a hand commits its log before an `Execute` or `Wait` request leaves for the hand, and before the first model request after a hand replied (2026-11-14). `WorkerLink::send` (src/wasm/remote.rs) calls `OneLog::effect("hand", call IDs)` for a batch that holds such a request, and it returns an unsent error when the commit fails. `WorkerLink::receive` marks its scope when a reply arrives, and `remote::settle` commits the marked scopes from `WorkerTransport`'s `Sender::send` (model requests only) and `Socket::send`. `remote::host` takes the session's scope and the link keeps it. Cause: since f1b2093 the checkpoint is a `OneLog` record that stays in the writer's buffer until a row commits. The SDK commits a row before a direct External tool call (`EffectGate`) and for a local cell (`OneLogJournals`). It commits none when `execute_code` sends a cell to a remote Code Mode host. A turn whose cells ran on a hand wrote one row at admission and one at its end. `App.durable` ran `storage.sync()` before a durable send, and the turn had written nothing to sync. A Worker that died in such a turn recovered from the admission row and ran the turn again from its prompt. It asked every answered model request again, and every cell ran again under a new call ID that `hand_requests` could not match. The row before a request leaves holds the checkpoint with the model step that issued the call, so a recovered turn waits for the hand that still runs the cell. The row before a model request holds the cell's result. That second row is needed because a recorded reply in `hand_requests` does not recover a finished cell: the reply names its nested calls as `delivered`, the updates that carried them are gone, and `ReceivedCalls::execution` refuses it. Measured on dev-georgios with nanoeval's release2/replay-work/harness/run.mjs (local workerd of wrangler 4.151.1, hand nanocode-cli-3f97ab0, a scripted Messages endpoint that answers after 1 s, one run per lane). One turn runs six cells of one `Bash` call each. Cell 4 sleeps 30 s and yields after 11 s, and the turn needs 8 model requests. The builds are build-061892c, build-2672c04, master 8ff3d5f (its WebAssembly module in a pack of 3362a76) and this change on 19c535d. A SIGKILL of workerd 5 s into cell 4 cost 8, 12, 12 and 8 requests. Cells 1 to 4 ran twice on build-2672c04 and on master, and no cell ran twice on build-061892c or with this change. The log held 1 row at the kill on build-2672c04 and on master, and 9 rows with this change. A SIGTERM at the same point gave the same requests and cells on every build. A SIGKILL 15 s into cell 4, during the model's `wait`, cost 8, 13, 13 and 8 requests. A SIGKILL during model request 3 cost 9, 11, 11 and 9 requests. On build-061892c and with this change the one extra request repeats request 3 byte for byte, and cells 1 and 2 ran twice only on build-2672c04 and on master. A SIGKILL as the answer to request 3 ended, with request 4 already sent, cost 9 requests on build-061892c and with this change, and request 4 is the one repeated. With the hand killed too and a new hand attached, the model reads "Tool execution interrupted by a host crash; outcome unknown" for cell 4 on build-061892c and with this change, and cell 4 does not run again. Cost: one row for each `Execute` and `Wait`, and one row before each model request that follows a reply. The turn without a kill writes 17 rows and 23.3 KB against 2 rows and 18.9 KB: admission, 7 `Execute` (a hand's first cell is sent twice), 1 `Wait`, 7 model requests and the end. Variants measured and not taken: with the first commit alone the turn writes 10 rows, and a kill during model request 3 or 4 makes the model read "outcome unknown" for the cell that had answered. `OneLog::settle_durably` for a session with hands writes 12 rows and gives the same "outcome unknown", because the write that carries the result is `Settle::Ordered` and stays buffered. Not done: the SDK could commit these rows itself, with `EffectGate` around `execute_code` for a host that keeps no cell journal. A deployed Worker was not run. Checked: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings`, `npm run build`, `npm run check:types`, and nanoeval aa573cb's directory, restart, forgotten, failures, judge, resume and lost lanes on directory slots with a pack of this commit.

- A Cloudflare session object's response carries `server-timing: session;dur=<ms>`, its own time from `fetch` to the response (headers, for a streamed turn) (2026-11-14). On the demo a new session's first turn took 1,244 ms p50 to response headers against 209 ms warm, and its `admission_ms` (74 ms p50) left the rest unaccounted. With the Worker's own `server-timing` (nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare) the client can split edge, Worker, routing to the object and the object. Checked: `npm run check:types`.

- The public app appends its `public_pre_session` and `session_response` timings to the session response's `server-timing` header instead of replacing it, so the session's own `session;dur` reaches the client (2026-11-14). With `set`, the deployed demo answered only the app and Worker entries, and the time from Worker dispatch to the session's response could not be split into object start and session work.

- bench/sla waits for the Codex composer prompt (`›`) instead of the placeholder text `describe a task`, and the mock `codex` lane trusts its work directory in the config it writes (2026-11-14). Codex CLI 0.145 and 0.166 show a random placeholder and ask whether to trust a new directory, so both Codex lanes produced 0 samples. The live lane uses the real `~/.codex`, which needs the work directory trusted and `check_for_update_on_startup = false`. With the fix, the mock lane readies in 1,754 ms and the live lane in 1,069 ms (1 sample each, dev-georgios).
The psh pin moves to nanocode e6b278b54 (2026-11-13), which adds POSIX mode: `set -o posix` and `--posix` turn it on instead of stopping the script, special builtins come before functions, assignments in front of them stay, and their usage, assignment and redirection errors end the shell as in bash (psh-spec: oils 2413 to 2418 of 2511, brush 2941 to 2967 of 3171; 34 of the 35 POSIX-mode cases pass, the CHLD trap interrupting `wait` does not). Since 4b1fb5fbd, psh-utils and psh-pattern depend on `nanocode-sys` (crates/core/sys), so `scripts/psh.mjs` exports that directory with the others; without it the pinned source does not build (cargo cannot read crates/core/sys/Cargo.toml). The module's ABI stays 7. Checked: `node scripts/psh.mjs` builds the pin with `--locked` (gak-9, 1 min 7 s); through the glue with that module, `set -o posix` shows in SHELLOPTS, `x=1 :` keeps x, `export 1x=2` ends a POSIX subshell with status 1, `set -o bogus ||` goes on, `alias a` prints `a='b'`, `type -t eval` says builtin and `uname -r` still prints 6.0.0; `npm run check:types` and `npx vp check` on the changed files pass. `scripts/build.mjs` (wasm-opt and dist/) was not run, and nothing was published or deployed.
- A session binds its payer at its first accepted turn: a turn that names none binds `plan` when the account's plan served it and `deployment` otherwise (2026-11-14). Before, a session without a bound payer asked the account's `PlanVault` for its subscription on every turn, before the turn was admitted, so each warm turn waited on a call to another Durable Object before its model request. The browser client already binds a payer to each conversation and sends it on every turn, so only clients that name no payer (the CLI transport, `p99.mjs`) waited. A session whose plan signs out now answers 403 on its next turn instead of moving to the deployment's key. Measured on two test Workers in the demo account with the same bundle (`nanocode-warmtest-a` without the change, `-b` with it, `wrangler deploy --name` from gak-9, Claude Haiku 5.5, no payer, interleaved, 10 sessions of 3 turns each, p50/p99 of warm turns): `admission_ms` 46/68 ms before and 0/0 after (an instrumented build put all of it in `planned`), POST to response headers 246/318 and 143/235 ms. The first delta, 857/976 and 834/1,416 ms, follows the provider's own time (645/828 and 651/1,218 ms). Checked: `npm run check:types`.

- The wasm profile builds nanocode-compression and nanocode-digest at opt-level 3, as djs-core already was, while the rest stays at "s" (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 nanocode-ts per-turn CPU). Deflate (the turn log's records stream and snapshots) and BLAKE3 (content-addressed durability records) run on every commit, and at "s" their inner loops (`longest_match`, `insert_string`, `compress_block`) were left uninlined and unrolled. output/prof.ts (200 turns of each kind, wasm after wasm-opt -O3, `perf stat -e instructions:u`, three interleaved rounds, dev-georgios, load 70-110, nanocode af6c01493): 38.74, 39.27, 39.50M instructions per turn at "s", 32.10, 32.29, 33.45M with the two crates at 3 (-17%); the module 13,138,027 to 13,158,664 bytes (+20.6 KB). Also measured: adding nanocode-turnlog, nanocode-durability-core and nanocode-durability at 3 gave 30.42-30.46M against 31.06M (-2%) for 13,647,597 bytes (+490 KB), and serde_json at 3 another -0.6% for +100 KB; neither kept, for the module size Workers compile on every cold start. Workers (workerd through wrangler dev, ~/djs2/r3-durab/wd drive.mjs, 4 rounds of 100 turns per cell) completes every cell with both modules, CPU per turn within noise (open+1 median 33.8 to 29.8 ms, tool 7.6 to 7.1 ms, the rest within 0.5 ms); bench/overhead.ts --n=20 completes every cell on Node.
- bench/sla's `rejected-claude` lane and `rejected-nc-*` lanes pass the parent's `HTTP_PROXY`, `HTTPS_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY` (either case), `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` and `SSL_CERT_FILE` to the client (2026-11-14). These lanes start the client from `base`, which drops every other variable, and the hosted Hand now reaches api.anthropic.com only through its egress proxy (a direct `curl --noproxy '*'` times out). Without the variables, nanocode and Claude Code both waited 60 s with no token on every `--live --rejected --key` turn (3 of 3 nanocode turns in the first session). With them, live claude-haiku-5-5 turns draw their first token in 590 to 1,990 ms. Mock lanes keep `base` alone, so the local fixture is never reached through a proxy.

- bench/sla/dashboard.ts serves a continuous live TTFT dashboard, and bench/sla/run.ts matches terminal text with whitespace removed (2026-11-14). The dashboard runs as a systemd user unit on dev-georgios (`sla-dashboard.service`, linger on) and listens on its Tailscale address, port 8830. Every 900 s after a cycle ends, it starts `flock -n -E 75 ~/sla/live.lock node bench/sla/dashboard.ts --cycle`, so a cycle never waits for another live benchmark and is recorded as skipped when the lock is held. A cycle runs one interleaved run.ts sample of 3 turns per native lane, then one new Worker session of 3 turns per provider (a port of the gak-9 `p99.mjs` probe: POST to response headers and to the first `assistant.delta`, with `server-timing` and the result timing). Rows go to `~/sla/dashboard/samples.jsonl` and cycles to `cycles.jsonl`. A lane that never readies counts as an error in each turn. `GET /` renders p50 and p99 per lane and turn over a selectable window, one time-series chart per turn, the latest run and the cycle log, and `GET /data.json` returns the same data. A cycle with `live-nc-codex@now,live-codex` (gpt-6-luna) and both Worker providers took 54 s (36 s native, 18 s Worker), so the 900 s interval holds the live lock for about 6% of the time. The unit leaves out the Claude pair: on dev-georgios the Claude Code login is expired, and nanocode reports `Claude Code login expired and has no refresh token`, so both Claude lanes wait for the timeout. After a `claude` login, `--lanes=live-nc-codex@now,live-codex,live-nc-claude@now,live-claude` adds them (`--claude-model` defaults to claude-haiku-5-5). The run.ts change: under systemd, without the `NO_COLOR` that the Pyramid shell exports, the nanocode TUI draws literal spaces, so the ready text `newsession` never matched and the lane reported `not ready` after 30 s in 2 of 2 cycles. With whitespace removed from both the watched text and the screen text, the lane readied in 65 ms and produced 3 of 3 turns. Checked: `tsc --strict --types node` on both files, and `/` and `/data.json` fetched from gak-9 over Tailscale with 12 rows from the cycle at 04:18 UTC.
- The Cloudflare app's `fetch` sends the provider prime (`egress.internal/prime`) for a `POST /sessions/<s>/turns` from the routing Worker, in parallel with the session object's start, at most once per provider per isolate in `MODEL_PRIME_IDLE_MS`, and skips plan, wallet and Workers AI turns as the session's `prime` does (2026-11-14). A new session object starts in a new isolate, and that start (about 600 to 900 ms on the demo) delayed the session's own prime until after it, so the first model request of a cold session met an unprimed AI Gateway path. Measured from gak-9 against two copies of the demo Worker that differ only in this change (`nanocode-coldtest-b` and `-c`, both without `python.wasm`), lanes interleaved per round, `?live=1`, a new session per round, 3 turns: POST to first delta of the cold turn, p50/p99, Claude Haiku 5.5 2,823/4,110 to 2,657/3,120 ms (n=12) and 2,583/3,733 to 2,502/4,379 ms (n=16), Responses gpt-6.1-sol 2,867/4,151 to 2,739/3,613 ms (n=10) and 2,630/6,412 to 2,518/3,080 ms (n=16); warm turns unchanged. `npm run check:types` passes.

- The Vercel harness takes `mcp.store` and passes it to `AgentRuntime.open` as `mcp.store`, and `nanocode-ts/vercel` exports `Listings.store(cache)`, which turns a cache with `get(key)` and `set(key, value, { ttl })` (seconds), such as the Vercel Runtime Cache that `getCache()` from `@vercel/functions` returns, into a `Contracts.McpStore` (2026-11-14, Georgios: cut the cold Function latency of the Vercel demo). Before this change a Vercel Function instance kept MCP listings only in the module memory of `Mcp.defer`, so the first session on each new instance sent `initialize` and then `tools/list` to every MCP server before its first model request, and the SDK open waits for every server. The Cloudflare host already shares listings through its `McpListings` Durable Object. nanocodexyz/durable-vercel passes two servers (mercator.sh and gateway.tempo-dev.workers.dev). From the hosted Hand, curl measured their server time (first byte minus TLS) in 5 runs each at 0.05 to 1.40 s for `initialize` and 0.04 to 1.72 s for `tools/list`. With durable-vercel 819fc9f, `next start` and a scripted model on the hosted Hand (5 cores, shared), 6 interleaved rounds of a new process and its first prompt (`output/cold/run.mjs` in that repository, ignored), with Egress sent through the Hand's proxy so the listings reach their origins: without a store the cold open took 1239, 3202, 2196, 2182, 2764 and 1995 ms and the first text came at 2607, 4244, 3430, 3393, 3703 and 3034 ms. With `Listings.store` over a file that the first run filled, the cold open took 99, 98, 119, 124, 140 and 130 ms and the first text came at 1094, 1169, 1538, 1132, 1477 and 1500 ms. With MCP off, the cold open took 115 to 225 ms and the first text came at 1076 to 1974 ms (median 1457). Warm turns read the listings from memory in all variants (first text 35 to 402 ms). Checked: `npm run check:types`, `vp check` on the changed files (one existing warning in App.ts).

- The Cloudflare session object adds `object_wait`, `object_requests` and `isolate_objects` to its `session` server timing, and a new session starts its MCP listing reads at admission (2026-11-14, Georgios: cut the live cold start of an evicted session). `object_wait` is `Date.now()` from the object's construction to the request, `object_requests` counts the requests the object served before this one and `isolate_objects` counts the session objects this isolate constructed, so 1 marks a new isolate. With them a deployment shows whether a slow cold response met a new isolate or a resident one. `Mcp.prefetch` starts the store read of each configured server's listing when a turn is admitted to a session without a resident runtime, and `Mcp.defer` takes that read instead of starting its own when the opening runtime asks for the listing. Live on build-0624213 (durable-cloudflare `scripts/measure-cold-evicted.mjs --split`, 8 new sessions, 990 s idle, all evicted): `GET /hands` on an evicted object took 833 ms of `session_response` at the median (702 to 1,297) and 86 ms again, a read of a missing turn after it 108 ms and the cold turn after both 247 ms, while a cold turn on an untouched evicted object took 829 ms (772 to 1,316) and a warm turn 133 ms. A second run (6 sessions, no split) gave 1,195 ms cold (921 to 1,352) with `session` 69 to 76 ms on five of six, against 155 ms warm. About 700 ms of a cold turn therefore passes before the object's handler runs, and the runtime open on the admission path adds about 20 ms (108 against 86). psh.wasm and python.wasm are instantiated at a shell's first use, not at a cold open, but workerd compiles every WebAssembly module when it loads the Worker, and a dynamic `import()` does not delay that: on workerd 1.20261012 (hosted Hand, 7 starts each, time to the first answer) a Worker without modules started in 36 ms, with nanocode_wasm and interpreter in 145 ms, with python.wasm alone in 193 ms and with all four in 365 ms. The change was measured on the build-0624213 TypeScript with the same two edits (durable-cloudflare dry-run bundle, a fresh workerd on the same storage per round, interleaved): with a 60 ms KV read (6 rounds) the cold runtime open took 152 ms (119 to 244) before and 98 ms (83 to 120) after, and the first model request started at 203 against 143 ms. With no KV delay (8 rounds) accept was 46.7 against 39.2 ms, runtime open 87 against 91 ms and the first model request 171 against 169 ms, and a second run of 6 rounds gave accept 46.5 against 60.6 ms and runtime open 84 against 109 ms, so without KV latency the change is within noise. Checked: `tsc -p tsconfig.cloudflare.build.json` and `vp fmt --check` on both files at this commit, and durable-cloudflare `npm run drill` (3 of 3 scenarios) on the build-0624213 bundle with the change. A first drill run on a host at load 18 ended in the harness's restart with Miniflare ERR_DISPOSED before any scenario failed, and the rerun passed. Not checked: a drill on a bundle of this commit, which has no release to build it from.

- wasm-opt runs `-Oz` after `-O3 --one-caller-inline-max-function-size=100` for nanocode_wasm.wasm (2026-11-14, Georgios: smaller modules for cold starts). On the SDK at 7a39104 the module falls from 13,001,578 to 12,663,165 bytes (gzip 4,551,162 to 4,508,262) with no measured change in Node compile CPU or warm-turn CPU (interleaved medians on the hosted Hand), and the durable-cloudflare drill passes 3 of 3 with it. Fat LTO gives 12,991,116 bytes once wasm-opt runs, and `opt-level = "z"` gives 11,416,170 bytes but costs 7 to 22 percent more CPU per warm turn, so neither is taken. The size audit behind this: serde, serde_json and serde_core are 2.63 MB of the 13.4 MB of code before wasm-opt, djs_core 1.37 MB, core and alloc generics 2.39 MB, and the Claude and Codex backends 0.69 and 0.68 MB.

- The Vercel journal writes a `nanocode.sql` batch as `{ base91: string }` instead of `{ packed: Uint8Array }`, and the batch step returns the writer token as a bare 22-character base64url string instead of `{ writer: <UUID> }` (2026-11-14). durable-harness encodes step inputs with devalue, which writes a Uint8Array as base64, and on Vercel a payload is the devalue text in binary frames. So the packed batch cost 4/3 of its bytes. basE91, over the 91 printable ASCII characters other than `"`, `<` and `\\`, which devalue would escape, costs about 1.23. `Journal.committed`, `Journal.open` and the replica digests read all three forms (`transactions`, `packed`, `base91`), so a session written before this change continues. A digest still hashes the brotli bytes. Measured with durable-vercel 819fc9f `output/jattr.ts` (bytes of decoded event payloads, the bytes Vercel stores, 5 turns per kind, base and change interleaved, 3 runs each, identical within 15 B across runs). Payload per turn, instant model: reply 2254 to 2040, cell 4536 to 4141, timer 5689 to 5206, charge 6608 to 6088. With `WORLD_DELAY_MS=100 MODEL_MS=800`: reply 2430 to 2201, cell 4734 to 4314, timer 6577 to 5996, charge 7315 to 6719 (8 to 9.4 percent). Events and steps per turn are unchanged. A session written by the base for 4 turns and continued by the change for 4 more in a new process replays 16 batches (8 `packed`, 8 `base91`) and 20 log rows. A 20,000-case round-trip fuzz of the codec and a 24 MB buffer (326 ms encode, 93 ms decode) passed. `npm run drill` passed 8 of 8 in 3 of 3 runs.

- The Cloudflare session object skips its schema probes once a schema marker is recorded, and clears empty socket tables without `json_each` (2026-11-14, Georgios: a cold open reads only what it needs). A cold open ran three `sqlite_schema` reads of 17 rows each on every construction: the legacy `blobs` table check in `Blobs.create`, the `WITHOUT ROWID` check of `metadata` and the legacy `jobs` check, plus `PRAGMA table_info(hand_sockets)`. They migrate layouts that only objects created before those migrations hold. Now the constructor reads `metadata` key `schema` (the metadata cache loads all keys at a cold open anyway, so the marker costs 1 more row in that read) and runs `Blobs.migrate` and the other probes only when the marker is not `1`. The marker is written after the probes find no `jobs` table, or after `migrate()` drops it, so a failed migration runs again at the next open. `Blobs.create` creates `blob_parts` with `IF NOT EXISTS` only. `restoreHands` and `restoreWatchers` run `DELETE FROM hand_sockets` and `DELETE FROM watch_sockets` when no socket is live, since the `json_each` form reads 2 rows on an empty table and the plain form reads 0 (miniflare probe). With the SDK change that archives superseded snapshots and reads the last snapshot through a partial index, measured with durable-cloudflare output/turns.mjs (local workerd, `TURNS=1 LONG=5|43|200`, base and new interleaved, 3 cold opens each, rows of the cold turn plus the warm turn after it, base = the build-0624213 bundle with `snapshot_rows: Some(64)`): 8 turns 100/108/116 -> 30/34/38 rows, 46 turns 124/132/140 -> 43/47/51, 203 turns 102/110/118 -> 32/36/40. The TypeScript change alone, with the base wasm, reads 50 rows at the first cold open after 8 turns against 100. What a cold open still reads after 203 turns: the log statement 18/22/26 (k + 2 for a tail of k rows), `metadata` 5, the `nanocode_files` symlink check 4 (a scan of the scope's files, growing with the file count) and the file stat lookups 4, plus miniflare's own `__miniflare_do_name` row. Stored log bytes after 203 turns: 1,194,043 -> 563,599. Warm turns unchanged: reply 2 rows written, cell 2, timer 3, charge 3, 0 read. Cold turn ms on the shared Hand (noise dominated): base 352/365/276, 257/249/307, 384/282/260, new 317/253/271, 276/288/570, 349/359/271. Policy run with `snapshot_rows: Some(16)` and the same bundle: 203 turns 32/18/22 rows (log 17/5/8), 46 turns 23/27/32, but 728,157 stored log bytes after 203 turns (23 archives of 145,993 B, and rows after a snapshot compress worse), so 64 stays. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `tsc -p tsconfig.cloudflare.build.json`, durable-cloudflare `node scripts/drill.mjs` with a `wrangler deploy --dry-run` bundle of this tree's Cloudflare dist and the SDK wasm (3 of 3 scenarios completed, no failures).

- The Cloudflare app admits a streaming turn (`POST /sessions/<s>/turns` with `accept: text/event-stream`) with the SDK's `OneLog::submit_buffered`, and the Cloudflare transport submits the turn again when a dropped stream finds it unknown (2026-11-14, Georgios: fewer SQLite rows per warm turn on durable-cloudflare). The wasm `submit` command takes `buffered` (default false) and `AgentRuntime.submit` takes `buffered?`, and `App.ts` sets it only for a streaming request, so a 202 reply still follows a durable admission row. A buffered admission becomes durable with the turn's next commit (the External call or timer row of a cell, or the finish), so a streaming turn writes 1 row for a reply or a synchronous cell and 2 for a timer or `charge` cell. If the object resets before that commit, the turn is not in the log: `Transport.reconnect` now reads `GET turns/<request_id>` before it reconnects, and on a 404 it posts the same request body again (the SDK treats the same request_id and prompt as one turn), or, on a page reload that has no body, it notes that the turn was not saved and stops instead of waiting for a result that never comes. A UI loaded before this change still reconnects to the events stream and waits for such a lost turn until the page reloads. Vercel keeps `submit`: `Journal.place` can commit the placement before a buffered admission reaches the journal. `submit` now takes the built `Submission` and `submitted_prompt` builds the prompt, which keeps it under clippy's argument limit. Checks: `npm run check:types` passes, `npm run build` (the SDK at the integrator's tree plus `submit_buffered`) passes, `npx vp fmt` on the changed files, `cargo +1.97.1 clippy --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` passes, durable-cloudflare `npm run drill` with the dry-run bundle of this pack passes charge, waiting and deploys (3 of 3, the charge drill streams its turn and crashes during the charge, so it recovers from the admission that the charge row carried), and durable-vercel `npm run drill` with this pack passes 8 of 8. durable-vercel `output/journal.ts` (TURNS=5, build-0624213 then this pack): transactions per turn are unchanged (2, 2, 3, 3), since Vercel keeps `submit`, and batches per turn go from 1, 1, 2, 2 to 2, 2, 2, 2 with world events 10, 10, 13, 16 to 7, 7, 7, 10, which comes from the unpushed Vercel journal change already in this tree, not from this entry. Measurements: durable-cloudflare local workerd, output/turns.mjs per-turn rows, interleaved build-0624213 against this pack: warm rows written per turn, reply, synchronous cell, timer cell, charge cell, mean of 5 turns, in each of 3 runs (2 rounds of turnsby.mjs and one full turns.mjs): before 2, 2, 3, 3 in every run, after 1, 1, 2, 2 in every run, rows read 0 in both. Statement bytes per turn fall slightly (reply 868, 882, 879 to 835, 833, 823 B, charge 4,865, 4,855, 4,850 to 4,822, 4,814, 4,793 B). Turn times are within the noise of the shared machine (reply 18, 33, 24 ms against 59, 35, 21 ms, synchronous cell 29, 66, 52 against 70, 51, 39 ms, timer 93, 99, 83 against 96, 99, 91 ms, charge 3,054, 3,052, 3,046 against 3,087, 3,063, 3,038 ms with its 3 s confirmation). The long session of turns.mjs (43 synchronous cell turns, then 3 workerd restarts): a cold turn plus the next warm turn write 4 rows against 5 and read 156, 162, 168 rows against 240, 248, 256, since the log holds fewer rows, with cold turns of 231, 292, 247 ms against 231, 314, 234 ms. Files: src/wasm.rs, src/AgentRuntime.ts, src/cloudflare/App.ts, src/cloudflare/Transport.ts.
- bench/sla/fixture.ts stamps a stream's first and last token when Node hands that write to the socket (the `res.write` callback), not when `res.write` returns (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 rendered tok/s). Unpaced, `res.write` returns while Node still buffers the bytes, so `last_token_ns` came before the client could read the last token: traces of the nanocode TUI (socket-chunk, delta-publish and frame marks) showed the last chunks arriving 1 to 3.5 ms after `last_token_ns` with the client idle before them, which counted as render tail and lowered `rendered_tps`/`provider_tps` at 600k to 800k tok/s, where the whole provider window is 25 to 30 ms. Paced runs are unchanged within noise (each paced write fits the socket). Measured on dev-georgios, `run.ts --tokens=20000 --rate=0 --samples=10 --turns=2`, lanes interleaved, bench processes at SCHED_RR 5 (load 100+), nanocode 62a0936e4 (base) and bbf2b0c73 (fix), p50 / p99 tail ms and p50 rendered/provider ratio: old stamp Responses base 6.87 / 8.84 (0.848), fix 4.64 / 6.24 (0.873), Claude base 3.83 / 5.48 (0.920), fix 3.02 / 4.16 (0.944); new stamp Responses base 3.84 / 6.82 (0.916), fix 1.78 / 3.31 (0.967), Claude base 2.94 / 39.17 (0.939), fix 2.21 / 3.29 (0.967). The changed fixture served every measured cell above.
- `sdk-revision.json` moves to nanocode 3638a2c7e (2026-11-14), which brings bbf2b0c73: the Responses client publishes its held display deltas before any non-delta event, on native and on WebAssembly, so a Worker session's last text delta no longer waits for the next event. The pin also brings local compaction at 90% of the window for models without remote compaction, Claude's `InputValidationError` for malformed tool input, Codex 0.162.1 error words and custom endpoint models. `Cargo.lock` drops `futures-sink` and the `futures-util` edges the SDK removed (`cargo metadata --format-version 1`). Checked: `npm ci --ignore-scripts`, `npm run check:types` (gak-9).
- The Cloudflare app routes sessions through an optional `SESSION_POOL` directory (`Pool.SessionPool`), so a new session name binds to an object that already started instead of starting one (2026-11-14). Without the binding, `sessionObject` returns `idFromName([account, name])` as before. With it, `Pool.resolve` returns the permanent binding of the name. A name the directory knows keeps its object. A legacy name (an object id that `seal` imports from the namespace's object list), and every name before `seal`, keeps `idFromName`. Any other new name takes the oldest ready object of the pool (`newUniqueId`, started through `POST /internal/prestart`, which instantiates the runtime before any descriptor), or `idFromName` when the pool is empty. The directory fills the pool to `SESSION_POOL_SIZE`, and its alarm sends `prestart` to each ready object every `SESSION_POOL_KEEPALIVE_MS`. A session still binds its authority at its first accepted turn, and the descriptor check does not change. Bindings never change, so each isolate keeps up to `SESSION_POOL_CACHED_NAMES` of them. The routing Worker reports `session_resolve;dur` in `server-timing`, and `session_response` starts after the resolve. Interleaved scratch Workers from gak-9 on the build-2aad477 sources (nanocode-worker-pool-a as deployed, -b with the pool, size 4, keepalive 30 s, 12 new sessions of 3 turns per provider and lane, p50/p99): cold POST to headers Claude 1,163/1,825 against 788/1,811 ms, Responses 1,189/1,859 against 620/995 ms; POST to first delta Claude 2,640/3,940 against 2,058/2,893 ms, Responses 3,199/4,906 against 2,427/2,628 ms; object start (`session_response` minus `session`) 851/1,160 against 298/883 ms and 884/1,427 against 181/619 ms; warm turns to headers 156/226 against 167/262 ms (Claude) and 153/246 against 174/306 ms (Responses). A first run without the isolate cache added 15 to 22 ms of `session_resolve` to each warm turn. The resolve of a new name takes 32 to 41 ms (the commit of its binding), and the first request to a pooled object still takes about 100 ms more than a warm request.
- The routing Worker's provider prime on a turn POST (`primeTurn`, 2aad477), the session object's `session;dur` server-timing wrapper (b58fe04) and the app's appended server-timing (3a5564a) are restored after the SDK pin dddd1fb dropped them while it rebased App.ts (2026-11-14). Without them build-dddd1fb would lose 80-170 ms p50 of cold first delta measured for 2aad477 and the object-start split. The `requests` counter that dddd1fb removed stays removed. `npm run check:types` passes.

- A hand reply that a Cloudflare session recorded recovers with the nested calls its update frames carried (2026-11-14). A hand answers an `Execute` or `Wait` with a compact reply: a nested call whose completion it already sent as an update frame is listed as `{"call_id":…,"delivered":true}`, and it keeps the full reply only until the SDK's client acknowledges the compact one. `hand_requests` stored that compact reply alone, and the SDK cannot read it back without the updates (`remote reply references nested call …, which no update carried`), so a recovered turn reported a cell that had finished as `Tool execution interrupted by a host crash; outcome unknown`. build-7968b53 hides this when calls run one after the other, because it commits the session log before the next `Execute` and before the next model request; a Codex session on a model the SDK does not know sends `parallel_tool_calls: true` and runs the calls of one response together, so a cell that finished while another still ran had its result in no log row when the Worker died. `App` now keeps the update frames of a request in memory until its reply arrives, writes them to a new table `hand_updates (hand, runtime, id, seq, frame)` when the reply lists a delivered call, and `recoverHandRequest` returns the reply with those calls put back (`src/cloudflare/internal/Replies.ts`, which splices the frames' own text so numbers and strings stay byte for byte; a call the brain itself answered, `answered`, is left as it is and still recovers as unknown). Rows go when their request row is pruned. Checked with `bench/resume` (next entry) on the released module of build-7968b53, with only the TypeScript of `src/cloudflare` rebuilt (`tsc -p tsconfig.cloudflare.build.json` of 7968b53 gives the released `dist/cloudflare` byte for byte, and with this change `App.js` and `internal/Replies.js` are the only files that differ): lane `pairDone` on `gpt-right`, two cells in one response of which the first has finished and the second sleeps when the Worker is killed, passed 0 of 5 runs before and 5 of 5 after, 5 model requests in both and no command twice. A turn of six cells without a kill writes 11 rows and 3,478 bytes to `hand_updates` beside the 17 rows and 23.5 KB of its log. `npm run check:types` passes. Not built: the WebAssembly module, which this change does not touch. Not run: a deployed Worker, a cell whose nested calls are brain tools.

- `bench/resume` kills the Worker in the middle of a turn and checks what the model was asked, what ran and what was counted (2026-11-14). `node bench/resume/run.ts <lane> <shape> [runs] [port] [tag]` starts a scripted model endpoint (Messages SSE, Responses SSE and the Responses WebSocket, written without a dependency), `wrangler dev` on `bench/resume/worker.ts` (the App from `src/` with `hands: 'always'`, whose session class also records every event handed to `published`) and a real `nanocode hand`, submits one turn, sends SIGKILL to the Worker at the lane's moment, starts it again on the same state and writes `output/resume/<tag>/<lane>.<shape>/<n>/result.json`. `RESUME_WRANGLER` names the wrangler command (default `npx wrangler`) and `RESUME_HAND` the hand binary (default `nanocode`); the package's `dist/` must hold the module. A lane is rounds of scripted tool calls (`echo <mark> >> steps.log`, with a sleep where a cell must be in flight), a kill plan and the requests it expects. It fails unless the model was asked exactly those requests in order, a repeated request carries the same conversation, every mark is in `steps.log` once, no tool result reads `outcome unknown`, the turn completed, the turn's reported usage is that of the responses it kept, and the published model calls that are not marked `replayed` are those responses. Shapes: `claude`, `luna-http` and `right-http` (Codex with an API key, `gpt-6-luna` and an unknown model, HTTPS SSE), `luna-ws` (a subscription session, the only one that opens the socket; an unknown model stays on HTTPS even there). Results on the released build-7968b53 with the hand nanocode-cli-3f97ab0, wrangler 4.151.1 and no model paid for, five runs of each lane (190 runs on the release and 60 with the change above, on 5 cores, two or three lanes at a time):
