  What the lanes show beyond the fix above. A Claude session runs the tool calls of one reply one after the other, so its parallel lanes pass. After a Worker is back, the published stream holds `running`, `input.accepted` and `run.started` again, the model call of the step in flight again with `timing.replayed` (the turn's reported usage counts it once), and `tool.call` again, unmarked, for every call that was in flight. A Codex session accepts its prompt again under a new `item_id`, and on the socket it sends its whole input again. A Worker killed in the same millisecond as a model answer loses that response: the request is sent again and the lost one is in no usage count. A Worker killed after the log row of a cell and before the frame reached the hand (2 of the 15 runs of `after3`; in the other 13 the kill came before the row and the request was sent again) leaves a call the hand answers with `NOT_RECEIVED` on resume, which the SDK's Claude agent reports as an unknown outcome although nothing ran. A turn with a subagent does not go on cleanly: the SDK registers the tools that answer a cell's callbacks only when it sends the cell, so the parent's cell waiting in `wait_agent` fails with `brain callback tool \`wait_agent\` unavailable`, and a child is restarted with a recovery notice and reads an unknown outcome for the cell it had in flight, which keeps running on the hand. Those three are in the SDK and are not changed here.

  The fix and the lanes ran again on the tree of this commit (2026-11-14): nanocode 86c465b, the hand `nanocode-cli-3f97ab0`, wrangler 4.151.1, a machine with 5 cores, two lanes at a time, 5 runs per lane. `pairDone` on `right-http` passed 5 of 5 (5 asked, 5 answered). build-7968b53 failed it 5 of 5 again in the same hour. The eleven other lanes of the last column passed 5 of 5 with the counts of the table: `cell`, `model`, `wait`, `twoKills`, `pairDone` and `clean` on `claude`, `cell`, `pair`, `pairWaits` and `waitExec` on `right-http`, and `cell` on `luna-ws`. The same twelve lanes passed 60 of 60 on the code of 742198a with nanocode 3638a2c7e. From this checkout, `node bench/resume/run.ts pairDone right-http 2` and `node bench/resume/run.ts cell claude 1` pass. The hand of 3f97ab0 ignores the `shared_images` field that the SDK's requests carry since 51d7fdd18.
- A turn with subagents goes on after the Worker is killed, and a cell whose frame never left the Worker is run after the restart instead of being reported with an unknown outcome (2026-11-14). `sdk-revision.json` moves from 86c465b to nanocode 67e6a187d, which carries the SDK's side (its DECISIONS entries of the same day: a recovered remote cell answers the brain tools it calls, recovering a call waits for the hand its host awaits, a recovered call that the hand never received is dispatched); `Cargo.lock` is unchanged. Three changes here use it. (1) `Recovering` (src/wasm/remote.rs) forwards `RemoteTransport::adopt` and `delivered` to its client. Before, the callbacks of a cell that a new runtime recovered were answered `brain callback tool `<name>` unavailable`: a root's cell waiting in `tools.wait_agent` failed and the turn ended without the child's result. (2) `open` says `executors.await_replacement(true)` for a session with hands, and `WorkerInbox::place` and `Session::drive` without a request say `false`. The SDK resumes restored subagents inside `init`, before the app attaches hands and before the root's turn is placed, so a child's recovery found no hand and its model read `Tool execution interrupted by a host crash; outcome unknown` for a cell that was still running on the hand; now that recovery waits until the turn is placed on its hand and takes the cell's reply as the root's does. Background work driven without a request ends the wait, so a session that never places a turn recovers as before. (3) `WorkerLink::send` sends a new host operation, `remote_record`, with the `Execute` and `Wait` frames of a hand's batch before it commits their effect row (`OneLog::effect("hand", ...)`), and fails the send as unsent if the host refuses it; the Cloudflare app answers with `recordHandRequests` (src/cloudflare/App.ts), `Remote`'s executors answer null (src/Remote.ts), and the app still records again when it sends. Before, the `hand_requests` row was written only while the frame was sent, after the effect row was durable, so a kill between the two left a cell that the log said had started and that no record named: recovery found no row, never asked the hand, and reported the cell unknown although its command had never run. With the row written first, a log row implies a `hand_requests` row, recovery sends the hand its resume frame, the hand answers `NOT_RECEIVED`, and the SDK dispatches the call. A request that is recorded and then refused before it is sent stays pending until a hand's next resume answers `NOT_RECEIVED` for it or its deadline prunes it. Measured with the lanes of bench/resume (`subClean`, `subCell`, `subWait`, `subModel`, `after3`) under local workerd (wrangler 4.151.1) with the hand nanocode-cli-3f97ab0, which is unchanged, a scripted model endpoint and SIGKILL, on d6d0578 built against nanocode b3816dc27 (before) and with this change and the SDK's on the same two revisions (after), 5 runs per lane. A root that spawns a child and waits for it, killed while the child's cell runs, while the child's `wait` is out, and while the child's model is asked: 0, 0 and 0 of 5 before, 5, 5 and 5 of 5 after; without a kill 5 of 5 both times (9 model requests). A kill 3 ms after the model answered with the third of six cells, on Claude, gpt-6-luna over HTTPS and an unknown gpt model: before, 3 of 15 runs lost the third command with `outcome unknown`; after, 0 of 15, and the 6 runs that hit the window ran the command once after the restart. With 10 more runs before and 12 more after on each of the two gpt shapes: 9 of 35 lost the command before (the app had recorded the request in 1 of the 9), none of 39 after, of which 12 hit the window. The lanes without subagents pass on the after build, 2 runs each: `clean`, `cell`, `model`, `wait`, `twoKills`, `pair`, `pairDone`, `pairWaits`, `waitExec`, `cellDown`, `waitDown`, `twoRecovery` and `twoModel` on Claude; `cell`, `model`, `wait` and `twoKills` on gpt-6-luna over HTTPS and over the WebSocket and on the unknown gpt model, which also passes `pair`, `pairWaits` and `waitExec`. On this commit itself (fab71ba with this change, SDK 67e6a187d), 3 runs each of `subClean`, `subCell`, `subWait`, `subModel` and of `after3` on Claude and on the unknown gpt model, and 2 each of `clean`, `cell`, `model` and `wait` on Claude, `cell` on gpt-6-luna over the WebSocket, and `pairDone`, `twoKills` and `waitExec` on the unknown gpt model: 34 of 34 pass, 2 of them hits of the 3 ms window. No command ran twice in any run. Not run: a deployed Worker, a real model, remote subagents (`remoteSubagents`), a child of a child, the Vercel host, the full `npm run build` (python.wasm was not rebuilt; this change does not touch it). The main module after `wasm-opt -O3` is 13,325,032 bytes at this pin (13,227,467 at 86c465b, built the same way). `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings` and `npm run check:types` pass.

The psh pin moves to nanocode 67d612220 (2026-11-14), which adds PATH lookup through relative and empty entries (`command -V` shows the absolute path, a program found past the working directory runs as `./name`), CHLD traps (once for each child that ends, ending `wait` with 145 in POSIX mode), signal traps that nest when their signal arrives during the trap, and stopped jobs under `set -m` with `fg` and `bg` (psh-spec: brush 2967 to 2995 of 3171, oils 2418 of 2511 unchanged). Stopped jobs need a host that reports stops (ExtProc::poll_stop, default Pending), so the WebAssembly host keeps its behavior there. Since that pin, psh-pattern and psh-utils depend on nanocode's `memchr` (crates/core/memchr), so `scripts/psh.mjs` exports that directory with the others; without it the pinned source does not build. The module's ABI stays 7. Checked: `node scripts/psh.mjs` builds the pin with `--locked` (gak-9, 1 min 9 s); through the glue with that module, a CHLD trap runs for a subshell and a command substitution, `kill -USR1 $$` inside its own trap nests to n=5, `set -o posix` with a CHLD trap ends `wait` with 145, `fg` says `no job control` without `set -m` and `%3: no such job` with it, `bg` on a running job says it is already in the background, `command -V` of a program found through `PATH=d1:...` prints `/home/user/d1/ex`, `type` keeps a relative `hash -p` path, and `uname -r` still prints 6.0.0; `npm run check:types` and `npx vp check` on the changed file pass. `scripts/build.mjs` (wasm-opt and dist/) was not run, and nothing was published or deployed.
- A pooled `AgentSession` primes its provider path at `POST /internal/prestart` (2026-11-14). The prestart, which `SessionPool` sends when it fills and again on each `SESSION_POOL_KEEPALIVE_MS` alarm, now sends `egress.internal/prime` for the default Claude and Codex models under the same `MODEL_PRIME_IDLE_MS` gate as admission, and reads `nanocode-mcp:prime` from the MCP listing store. `prime` takes a list of provider and model pairs, so one gate covers both requests. A claimed object then sends its first model request through an egress path that already ran, instead of in parallel with the admission prime. With the demo values (4 objects, a 30 s keepalive, a 15 s gate), the pool sends 16 prime requests each minute. Local workerd harness (`durable-cloudflare` bundle of build-8f204d2 with this change patched into `dist`, mock provider that answers primes with 400, `MODEL_PRIME_IDLE_MS=15000`, object prestarted 500 ms before turn 1, interleaved A/B, n=20 for each cell, 5 cores), turn 1 p50/p99 ms before and after: Claude headers 76.8/112.1 to 45.4/88.5, first delta 380.7/480.2 to 340.0/467.7; Codex headers 73.7/101.1 to 48.0/90.3, first delta 357.4/433.3 to 345.0/458.7. Turns 2 and 3 do not change (first delta p50 22 to 29 ms in both). The largest remaining cold cost is `runtime_open` (p50 225 ms, `sdk.open` with `wasm.enter` 90 to 177 ms), which the prestart does not cover because the SDK `init` needs the session descriptor. The production effect on the first AI binding request is not measured: it needs a release and a deploy.

The psh pin moves to nanocode 8ad5f93ad (2026-11-14), which adds `$BASH` (`/bin/bash` in an embedding without a program of its own), an ignored SIGQUIT, untrappable signals ignored at start, PIPE traps for builtins' writes, job signal reports without the duplicate `Terminated  wait $pid`, stops of an in-process shell, forked subshells for `$BASHPID` where the host forks (the standalone shell on Linux, not this module), a CHLD count kept only while a CHLD trap is set, and signal traps bounded by the nesting limit (psh-spec: brush 2995 to 3005 of 3171, oils 2418 unchanged). Since nanocode 8978566a7, nanocode-shell depends on nanocode's `bytes` (crates/core/bytes), so `scripts/psh.mjs` exports that directory with the others; nanocode f9c492c13 moved psh-wasm to it and fixed the psh workspace's Cargo.lock, without which the pinned source does not build with `--locked`. The module's ABI stays 7; it is 6,923,739 bytes before wasm-opt (6,914,899 at 67d612220). Checked: `node scripts/psh.mjs` builds the pin with `--locked` (gak-9, 1 min 7 s); through the glue with that module, `$BASH` is `/bin/bash`, a CHLD trap runs for a subshell, a PIPE trap that writes to its own closed pipe returns where the module used to stop with `Maximum call stack size exceeded`, `kill -USR1 $$` nests its trap to n=20, `kill -QUIT $$` leaves the shell running, `fg` says `no job control`, `command -V` prints an absolute path and `uname -r` still prints 6.0.0; a 20,000-iteration builtin loop takes 29.1 ms per exec through the glue (28.6 ms at f9c492c13, the same within noise). `npm run check:types` and `npx vp check` on the changed file pass.
- A session can run Codex with direct tools instead of Code Mode: `codex_orchestration` (`code_mode`, the default, or `direct`) in `AgentConfiguration`, and the same field in a Cloudflare turn body, which the session binds at its first accepted turn like its model (a later turn naming another answers 409) and keeps in the descriptor its runtime opens with, so sessions of one `App` differ (2026-11-14, Georgios: one nanoeval run carries a code-mode arm and a direct arm). The WebAssembly host gives `CodexHarness::direct_tools_for(|_| true)` for `direct`: the model gets the hand's or workspace's tools (`exec_command`, `apply_patch`, ...) itself, without `exec`/`wait` or the Code Mode developer block. `sdk-revision.json` moves to nanocode master d2473f9dd (landed on nanocode-ts master on 2026-11-14 from branch commit f3aa94f, which pinned 0e428f673 on `feat/codex-direct-orchestration`): it carries cbc3e78f6, master's commit of 0e428f673 that makes `direct_tools_for` hold with a hand, 7f4cef6be and b55bdf602, so the wasm brain sends upstream Codex's permissions message (danger-full-access, network enabled, approval never) and `<filesystem>` element instead of `<host_execution_context>`, and a Codex `exec` cell without `yield_time_ms` first yields after 30 s as codex-cli's does, with `wait` at 10 s and an explicit `cell_yield_ms` still winning. It also carries 96314dec0 (Codex Code Mode hides a hand's Claude-shaped tools; Claude Read shows BMP and PNM images), 1c6dfe3ed (a Claude response the safety classifier stops is asked again once per turn) and d8bbf7921 (with a Code Mode host a Claude environment's working directory is the hand's). Cargo.lock follows the SDK. Checks: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `cargo clippy --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown -- -D warnings`, `npm run check:types`.
- The WebAssembly crate takes `HeaderMap`, `HeaderName`, `HeaderValue` and `StatusCode` from nanocode-http instead of the `http` crate, and `http` leaves Cargo.toml (2026-11-14). nanocode 8978566a7 gave nanocode-http its own HTTP types, so the Codex host transport (`HostHttpResponse`, `send_http`) and `ClaudeClient::with_auth_headers` take nanocode-http's `HeaderMap`, and `node scripts/build.mjs` against nanocode d2473f9dd failed with seven E0308 errors ("`HeaderMap` and `HeaderMap` have similar names, but are actually distinct types") in src/wasm/transport.rs and src/wasm.rs. Checks: `cargo check --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm` without warnings, `node scripts/build.mjs`, `npm run check:types`.
- `SessionPool` starts a replacement object on each claim (2026-11-14). `refill` counts the objects that it is starting, and starts `SESSION_POOL_SIZE` minus the ready and starting objects at once. Before, `fill` ran in rounds: the first claim of a burst started one object, and the other objects started only after that prestart ended, so a drained pool was full again after two prestarts. A start that fails is retried by the next claim or the next keepalive alarm, not in a loop. Production on 5fdd376 (hosted Hand, bursts of 8 new sessions per host and provider, 2026-11-14 09:00 UTC) gave four to five claims an object start of 136 to 452 ms and the next three to four claims of each burst 673 to 1,445 ms (`idFromName`), and the one claim between 0.6 and 0.9 s after a burst that found a replacement had an object start of 136 to 173 ms. Local Miniflare harness (`SessionPool` with a stub `AgentSession` whose prestart takes 500 ms, `SESSION_POOL_SIZE` 4, 8 claims at once then claims at 600 to 1,000 ms, 6 interleaved rounds per version): claims between 600 and 900 ms after the burst found a pooled object 6 of 24 times before and 24 of 24 after. `npm run check:types` passes. Production needs a release and a durable-cloudflare deploy.
- A pooled `AgentSession` compiles the SDK code paths at `POST /internal/prestart` (2026-11-14). The local workerd isolate experiment (`durable-cloudflare` `output/iso.mjs`: three prestarted objects in one Miniflare isolate, one turn each in order, n=10 per cell) measured turn 1 of the first object at `runtime_open` p50/p99 130/158 ms (`sdk.open` 124/148, `wasm.enter` 97/114) and first delta 201/242 ms, and the second and third objects at `runtime_open` 6 to 7 ms and first delta 18 to 22 ms. The cold cost is therefore per isolate (lazy WebAssembly compilation, the in-memory MCP listings), not per session, and it does not need the session descriptor. In Node, `init` on a second runtime in the same process takes 4 ms against 32 to 43 ms on the first. The prestart now opens a separate `AgentRuntime` once per object with no authority: the default Claude model with the Codex endpoint, the host tool definitions, the workspace, and the static MCP servers with the listing store. A local `dispatch` answers it with 400, it asks one side question, and it is disposed. It writes no rows (checked in Node: `nanocode_log` stays empty after open and the side question) and sends no network request, and the session date is not stored. A failed compile clears the memo, so the next prestart runs it again. A throwaway turn on a separate scope removed 10 to 18 ms more but writes `nanocode_log` rows that the host must delete from an SDK table, so it is not included. Local workerd harness (bundle of build-8f204d2 with bc9a923 and this change patched into `dist`, mock provider, object prestarted 500 ms before turn 1, interleaved, n=20 for each cell, 5 cores, without the MCP servers in the warm open), turn 1 p50/p99 ms before and after: Claude first delta 191.4/227.7 to 151.4/173.6, `runtime_open` 118/151 to 88/111, headers 42.5/54.4 to 33.4/54.9; Codex first delta 194.7/216.3 to 160.9/194.9, `runtime_open` 125/145 to 97/122. Turns 2 and 3 do not change. Prestart takes 108 to 111 ms p50 instead of 48 ms. The production effect needs a release and a deploy.
- The Cloudflare routing Worker keeps a colo-level cache of `SessionPool` bindings in the Cache API (2026-11-14). `sessionObject` reads `caches.default` before the `SessionPool` RPC and writes the resolved object id after it, under `https://<request host>/internal/session-pool/<pool object id>/<account and name>` with `cache-control: max-age` from the new `SESSION_POOL_BINDING_CACHE_S` variable. The per-isolate `bound` map stays in front of it. A binding never changes after `SessionPool.resolve` writes it (`bindings` has no update or delete), so a cached id cannot become stale, and the pool object id in the key separates namespaces on one zone. Without `SESSION_POOL_BINDING_CACHE_S`, or for a request without a host (the `AgentSession` parent and child stubs, `deleteSession`), only the isolate map applies. The Cache API does not store on `*.workers.dev` hostnames, so a request to one skips the cache explicitly and always resolves through the RPC: only a custom domain such as cloudflare.nanocode.xyz gets the colo cache. The write is not awaited, as `primeTurn`, so a miss adds no latency, and a failed write rejects unhandled. Production before (2026-11-14): 83 of 96 warm turns missed the isolate map because they landed on another isolate or colo, and a miss cost `session_resolve` p50/p99 71/94 ms (headers 176/540 ms against 65/147 ms on a hit). Local Miniflare harness (durable-cloudflare `output/binding-cache/run.mjs`: the committed `sessionObject` and `Pool.ts` with a stub session, 40 names, a fresh `bound` map on each request to model an isolate miss): with `SESSION_POOL_BINDING_CACHE_S` 86400 on host cloudflare.nanocode.xyz, 40 of 40 names have a cache entry with the pool's id and `max-age=86400`, and a second resolution returns the same id; on x.workers.dev and without the variable no entry is written. Local timings are at the 1 ms timer resolution of workerd and the local RPC costs under 1 ms, so the production effect needs a release, the variable in durable-cloudflare and a deploy. `npm run check:types` passes.
- `bench/sla/run.ts` live lanes wait for the turn's mark in the reply, not in reasoning (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 latency SLA, Georgios). Every client draws its reasoning before the reply: nanocode under `∴`, Claude Code as `● Thinking…` with its text, and Codex in its status header. A live lane matched the mark anywhere on the screen, which in the live open-model runs of nanocode's SLA.md on 2026-11-14 was 0.1 to 6 s early. A lane's `reply` now names its reply bullet. For nanocode and Claude Code, the mark must lie within `--reply-window` characters (default 60) after a `●` that `Thinking` does not follow. For Codex, the mark must follow `•` after at most 12 other characters that are not letters. Up to 6 characters that are not letters may separate the letters of the mark, which a diffing renderer leaves between them. Each `tui` sample records `wall`, the wall-clock time of Enter in ms, to match proxy logs. `SLA_STREAM=<file>` appends one line for each turn: its screen chunks without escape sequences, each with its ms after Enter, and the turn's `e2e`, `index`, `lane`, `turn` and `wall`. Measured by replay with `output/sla-reply/replay.ts`, which evaluates the matcher source of run.ts, over the 288 turns that the SLA.md run recorded with `SLA_STREAM` (4 runs of 2 lanes, 12 sessions of 3 turns each). Without a window, the rule gives each recorded time, and no reply is missed. The 60-character window moves two nanocode turns 55 and 108 ms later, where reasoning under a redrawn earlier reply held the mark, and changes no p50 or p99. The old match found 92 turns more than 30 ms early and missed 5. The windowed rule on Codex matches one turn 650 ms early, so Codex keeps the strict rule. End to end with fake clients (`output/sla-reply/fake.ts`: reasoning that holds the mark at 200 ms, the reply at 900 ms, 2 sessions of 3 turns on `live-nc-claude@fake`, `live-claude` and `live-codex`), run.ts before this change measured 200 to 207 ms on every turn and after it 900 to 905 ms. With `--reply-window=100000`, nanocode turns 2 and 3 measured 201 to 202 ms, because the fake redraws the earlier reply above the reasoning. Checks: `npm run check:types` passes, `tsc --strict` on bench/sla/run.ts reports no errors before or after, and `vp lint` reports the same 13 warnings before and after. `vp check` reported formatting issues in run.ts before this change too, so the file keeps its format.
- The interleaved A/B of the prestart compile (ae7c617) on a seeded store (2026-11-14): `durable-cloudflare` `output/prime-ab-seed.mjs --bundles=output/deploy-b,output/deploy-c,output/deploy-e --rounds=20`, which runs one turn on a first object to write the MCP listings and copies that persisted state into each round's fresh Miniflare instance. Bundles: `deploy-b` is build-8f204d2 with bc9a923 patched into `dist` (prestart without the compile), `deploy-c` adds the compile without the MCP servers, and `deploy-e` adds the compile with the static MCP servers and the listing store, as ae7c617 landed. Mock provider, object prestarted 500 ms before turn 1, bundles interleaved, n=20 for each cell, hosted Hand (5 cores), no other run on `~/sla-live.lock`. Turn 1 p50/p99 ms, b to c to e. Claude: first delta 121.9/157.4 to 85.5/101.5 to 79.1/99.8, `runtime_open` 56/89 to 28/40 to 16/22, headers 34.2/41.4 to 31.0/36.6 to 31.0/46.5. Codex: first delta 123.8/182.3 to 96.5/117.1 to 96.4/112.0, `runtime_open` 62/84 to 37/52 to 35/39, headers 35.2/75.6 to 31.9/37.4 to 31.6/35.4. With the listings already in the store, `deploy-b` and `deploy-c` open the runtime 60 to 62 ms faster and draw the first delta 65 to 71 ms sooner than in the unseeded run of the ae7c617 entry. The compile removes 36 ms of the Claude first delta and 27 ms of the Codex one. The MCP servers in the compile remove 12 ms more of the Claude `runtime_open` and 6 ms of its first delta, and nothing measurable on Codex. Turns 2 and 3 do not change (first delta 8 to 15 ms p50 in all bundles). Prestart takes 40 to 41 ms p50 on `deploy-b`, 94 to 100 ms on `deploy-c` and 104 ms on `deploy-e`. Results: `output/prime-seed-bce-1794651552424.json`, with the samples in `output/ab4.err`.
- Remote children: `child_run` returns once the child object holds the request, and `child_wait` returns the turn's result (2026-11-14, dst lane). This follows the nanocode commit in which `ChildHost::run` returns the acceptance and the result as two futures, so that the parent admits a message only after the child has it. `Contracts.ChildHost.run` returns `ChildStart`: a finished `ChildTurn`, or `{ status: 'running' }` after the child's 202. The new `ChildHost.wait(session_id, request_id)` returns the result of a running turn through the new `child_wait` bridge op, and src/wasm/children.rs sends `child_wait` only for a running turn. App.ts keeps each run's promise in `childRuns` from before the `child_turn` POST until `childWait` reads it, so a `child_done` that arrives before the wait still resolves it. A network error of the `child_turn` POST now rejects `child_run`, and nanocode then repeats the run with the same request id after `completion_retry_delay`. The child answers a known request id with its running or finished turn. A non-OK answer stays a failed turn, as before. Why: in nanocode-sim, 36 of seeds 0..5000 (seed 231 first) lost a message when the parent restarted after it admitted the message and before the child received the request. Requests per child do not change: one `child_turn` POST and one `child_done` per turn, and `child_wait` stays inside the parent isolate. If the parent drops a turn's result future without a wait, its `childRuns` entry stays until the object is evicted. Not measured: scripts/hillclimb.mjs in durable-cloudflare, which needs a WebAssembly release built from the new nanocode commit. Checked: `npm run check:types`; `npx vp fmt` on the changed TypeScript files; `cargo +1.97.1 check --locked --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` against nanocode c8f04f3ba (this lane's commit) passes.
- A pooled `AgentSession` can write the provider prompt cache at `POST /internal/prestart` (2026-11-14). `PROMPT_CACHE_PRIME_MS` (optional, unset keeps the old behavior) makes the prestart run the compile runtime a second time in `waitUntil`, and this time the side question `prestart` goes through the egress as a deployment-paid Claude request instead of the refused `dispatch`. The isolate sends it at most once in that many milliseconds, across all its objects, and counts it in the `prompt_cache_primes` and `prompt_cache_prime_failures` metrics. MCP requests of that runtime are still refused, so its tools come from the listing store. The side question carries the same `tools` and the same `system` blocks with cache breakpoints as the first request of a new session: in the local workerd harness (durable-cloudflare bundle of build-683b12f with this change in `dist`, 3 prestarted objects with 2 prestarts each, `PROMPT_CACHE_PRIME_MS=240000`) the mock provider received one side question, whose `tools` and `system[0..2]` equal those of a turn's first request (only the unmarked environment block differs), and no request with the variable unset. Why: in production a new session's first Claude request wrote the whole prefix (`cache_write_input_tokens` 12,716, `cached_input_tokens` 0, demo, 2026-11-14 08:09 UTC), and the refused prime (`max_tokens: 0`) writes no cache. Measured directly against api.anthropic.com from the hosted Hand with the captured bodies (claude-haiku-5-5, a per-round nonce in the first tool description so that no lane meets an earlier cache entry, first content delta in ms, n=24 per lane): a turn 5 s after the prime read 9,681 to 9,685 cached tokens, and its first delta p50/p90 was 801/1,294 ms against 1,228/1,623 ms for a turn without the prime (paired difference p50 444 ms, primed faster in 14 of 24 rounds). An earlier run of an identical prefix 1.5 s apart measured 675/892 ms cached against 745/1,294 ms uncached. The prime costs one side question (about 11k prefix tokens written or read, and a median of 200 output tokens) per isolate per interval. The value must stay below the provider cache lifetime, which is 5 minutes for `ephemeral` breakpoints and restarts on each read. The effect on the deployed demo is not measured: it needs a release and a deploy. Codex is not primed. Checked: `npm run check:types`.
- A Cloudflare turn body can turn subagents off for one session: `subagents` (boolean), which the session binds at its first accepted turn like `codex_orchestration` (a later turn naming another value answers 409) and keeps in the descriptor its runtime opens with, so sessions of one `App` differ (2026-11-14, Georgios: nanoeval's Codex arms run without subagent tools and without web search, as codex-cli runs). `false` opens the runtime with `subagents: false` (the SDK's `without_subagents()`), and `true` keeps the `App`'s `subagents`. A body with `true` where the `App` sets `subagents: false` answers 400, and a session that started before this change binds the `App` option. A body without the field keeps the `App` option, as before. `web_search` already was a turn-body field. `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 21730bb09 to master 0bbcb64a0, which carries 7746aa975 (Codex Responses requests keep up to 500 images, the documented per-request limit, instead of the newest 8), 0bbcb64a0 (Code Mode's Bash description says `&` or `nohup` jobs are not followed), 5af8ea1e5 and 1d7cbd4bc (one `assistant.message` per Codex output item, emitted at `response.output_text.done`), 61b640ae6 (pyyaml), and 38f05b9fb and 87df9f7f4 (a remote child's turn is admitted after the child holds its input). Checked on dev-georgios: `node scripts/build.mjs` from source with `Cargo.lock` unchanged under `--locked`, `npm run prepare` and `npm run check:types` pass.
- The opening key of a Cloudflare session runtime holds the effective `codex_orchestration` (`code_mode` when the descriptor has none) and the effective `subagents` (the descriptor's, else the `App` option). Before this change, the first turn of a new session opened its runtime with `codex_orchestration` absent (`null` in the key), and every later turn derived `code_mode` from the initialized session. The second turn therefore found a different key, disposed the resident runtime and reopened it inside the pump, and the turn stayed `queued` with keepalives until the Durable Object reset. On the demo, from the build-683b12f deploy (2026-11-14 09:42 UTC) until the rollback, every new session lost its second turn: the stream emitted `queued` and no `running` within 60 s, and the third turn completed on a fresh object (its event seq moved up by 16384). 6039207 introduced the mismatch. Bisect with the durable-cloudflare local harness (`node scripts/measure-sla.mjs --rounds=1 --turns=3`): build-8f204d2 passes, build-8e141d5, build-683b12f and build-7a5d073 time out on turn 2. With this key, build-7a5d073's dist and master's (b8158fd) compiled TypeScript over the build-7a5d073 tarball pass 3 sessions x 3 turns for claude and codex, and master without it times out on turn 2 (2026-11-14). Checked: `npm run check:types`.
- A buffered streaming admission survives a runtime reopen (2026-11-14). `AgentSession` keeps each `buffered: true` submission it accepted in `buffered`, a `WeakMap` from the accepting `AgentRuntime` to its admissions, until the pump finishes that turn. If `runtime()` disposes a resident runtime because its opening key, hands mode or subscription changed, it submits each held admission again, durably, into the fresh instance before that instance opens. A submission that answers neither `accepted` nor `existing` throws a `HostFailureError`. A buffered admission lives only in the turn log's write buffer until the log's next commit, and `dispose` drops that buffer. Before this change, the reopened runtime's log had no such turn: `drive` answered `idle`, the pump marked the queue empty, and the stream sent `queued` and then only keepalives until the object reset. A turn whose `web_search` differs from the previous turn's reopens the runtime, so this case reached master after bd02b0c too. The re-submission admits only turns that no model call has dispatched, because a turn's first call commits the log, and a turn the log already holds answers `existing`. Measured in the durable-cloudflare local workerd harness (`scripts/measure-sla.mjs`, mock provider, nanocode-ts b8158fd module with master TypeScript), 3 sessions x 3 turns for claude and codex: with turn 2 sending `web_search: true`, master timed out on turn 2 of the first session and this change completed 18 of 18 turns (turn 2 dispatch p50 42 ms claude, 44 ms codex, which includes the reopen). With the key forced to change (`codex_orchestration ?? null`), the results were the same: a timeout before, 18 of 18 after. Sessions without a change keep turn 2 dispatch at 11 to 18 ms. Checked: `npm run check:types`.
- Pin nanocode 2ccfcecd (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: restart lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 0bbcb64a0 to 2ccfcecd, whose `OneLog::journals` parses each tool catalog once and restores every cell journal in one `CellJournals` update instead of one copy-on-write update per session. On a 10,000-child in-object Durable Object restarted from a saved kill-point log (interleaved, 3 runs each), the first model call after restart went from 7,389, 6,235 and 7,163 ms to 4,662, 4,025 and 4,300 ms, `sdk.open` from 3.3 to 3.8 s to 1.2 to 1.4 s, and parent WebAssembly memory from 1,605 MB to 957 MB. `Cargo.lock` follows the dependencies that the SDK removed. Checks: `cargo check --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`, `npm run check:types`.
- Pin nanocode a2662527d (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: root lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 2ccfcecd to a2662527d, whose root receives subagent completions in one batched prompt per wake. In scripts/hillclimb.mjs (durable-cloudflare) with 10,000 in-object children, `--batch 100 --rate 200`, interleaved master/change twice, the root model request bytes per child went from 62,359 and 62,512 to 142 and 148, root calls from 309 to 15, parent peak WebAssembly memory from 1,696 and 1,672 MB to 1,123 and 1,125 MB, and wall time rose from 559.7 and 572.0 s to 595.9 and 608.8 s. The 2,000-child crash gate passes. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`, `npm run check:types`.
- `/metrics` reports `requests` (requests the session object served) and `child_requests` (requests it sent to remote children) again (2026-11-14). 8ff3d5f added both counters and dddd1fb dropped them with its App.ts rewrite, so scripts/hillclimb.mjs in durable-cloudflare read 0 for `parent_requests_per_child` and `parent_outbound_per_child` and priced every run without its requests. The counters return at the same two sites: `fetch` and `childPost`. With them, `node scripts/hillclimb.mjs --agents 1000 --batch 100 --rate 200 --scenarios score` on a remote-children bundle (`remoteSubagents: true`, `maxConcurrentSubagents: 64`) of 177b460 against nanocode 22f8297d9 reads 7,089 parent requests and 1,000 child requests for 1,000 children, in each of 2 runs. `npm run check:types` passes.
- A remote child takes the subagent record for its first `childSubagent` call of a turn from `child_turn` instead of a `child_subagent` request to its parent (2026-11-14, hill-climb writes lane). `childRun` reads the parent's record for the child (lineage and budgets, the value `child_subagent` returns) and sends it with `child_turn`. The child keeps it for one call and sends `child_subagent` to the parent for later calls of the turn and after a restart. A child's first model request reads its record, so each finished child cost the parent one served request for it. Measured with durable-cloudflare's `node scripts/hillclimb.mjs --agents 1000 --batch 100 --rate 200 --scenarios score` on local workerd against the rate-limited mock, remote children (`remoteSubagents: true`, `maxConcurrentSubagents: 64`), on 177b460 with the request counters against nanocode 22f8297d9, without the change (2 runs at 08:46 and 09:46 UTC, built with an unlanded one log change at a zero window that skips empty commits) and with it (1 run at 12:10 UTC, not interleaved, since the shared bench lock was held by other lanes): parent requests per child 7.09 and 7.10 against 6.12, parent rows written per child 7.34 and 7.35 against 7.34 (unchanged by this change), wall 234.4 and 324.8 s against 266.4 s (other lanes' builds and benchmarks shared the 5-core Hand, so wall does not separate the builds). The crash gate (`--agents 300 --gate-agents 300 --scenarios spawn,turn,done,twice`) passes: 0 lost, 0 repeated after done, 0 repeated outside the calls in flight at each kill. `npm run check:types` passes. A group commit of the parent's durable settles within `EVENT_COALESCE_MS`, measured in the same runs at 0.24 rows per child, is not part of this change: at a 100 ms window, 61 of 300 children stayed unfinished in the parent's listing after a kill at spawn, so it does not pass the crash gate.


- `SqlFileSystem` creates the partial index `nanocode_files_links ON nanocode_files (scope) WHERE kind = 'link'` with its schema, so the symlink probe that `real` and `writeMany` run once per file system instance reads the index instead of every file row of the scope (2026-11-14, Georgios: a cold open reads a constant number of rows). `SELECT 1 FROM nanocode_files WHERE scope = ? AND kind = 'link' LIMIT 1` had no index to use, so a scope with no symlink read all its file rows at the first file operation of every cold open: 201 rows with 200 files, 0 with the index (miniflare probe, output/probe/links.mjs in durable-cloudflare). Writes of plain files stay at 3 rows each, a symlink write costs 4 instead of 3, and creating the index on an existing table reads the scope's file rows once. The schema transaction runs 5 statements instead of 4 (0 rows read). With nanocode's change that extends the tail row of the turn log, measured with durable-cloudflare output/cold.mjs (local workerd, the build-8f204d2 dry-run bundle with this statement added to its schema transaction and nanocode_wasm.wasm built from this repository at e94cf80 against the patched SDK, 2 interleaved rounds, 3 cold opens each): the cold turn reads 15 rows after 43 turns (58/61/64 before) and 17 after 200 turns (22/25/28 before) in 25 statements (24 before). What it still reads: `metadata` 5, the cold turn's own 2 log commits 2 each, the file stat lookups 4 (6 statements), the log load 1 to 3, and miniflare's `__miniflare_do_name` row. The harness session holds 4 file rows, so the probe read 4 of the 58 to 64 before. Checks: `npm run check:types`; durable-cloudflare `node scripts/drill.mjs` with that bundle and wasm (charge, waiting and deploys completed, no failures) and `node scripts/measure-faults.mjs --seed 1` (1,340 checks, no findings, with the fault harness change described in durable-cloudflare); durable-vercel `npm run drill` with the wasm (8 of 8).
- Pin nanocode 37f0aeb00 (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: restart lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 0dff6b832 to 37f0aeb00, whose `TurnLog::open` sets the writer's `state` from the last snapshot row, so the first commit after a restart no longer encodes the whole fold only to measure it. On a 10,000-child Durable Object restarted from a saved kill-point log, the first model call went from 4,700, 4,217 and 4,550 ms to 3,874 ms (one run), `sdk.open` from 1.1 to 1.3 s to 738 ms and peak WebAssembly memory from 957 MB to 738 MB, and natively the first commit after open went from 398 to 536 ms to 0.1 to 0.2 ms over 5 interleaved rounds. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`, `npm run check:types`.

- bench/sla/dashboard.ts shows the 1.0 latency gate per cell (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 latency SLA, Georgios). Before, the Worker probe sent no `model`, so its sessions ran the Worker defaults (`gpt-6.1-sol`, `CLAUDE_MODEL` or `claude-opus-5-5`) against native lanes on `gpt-6-luna` and `claude-haiku-5-5`, and its rows sat in their own `worker` pair with no verdict. Now `--workers=provider:model,...` (default the two native models and `codex:glm-5.3`, which Workers AI serves without a provider key) names each probe's model, and its lane is `worker-<provider>@<model>`. Each row and summary cell carries `model`. Each Worker cell carries `splits`: p50 and p99 of every `server-timing` entry plus the result's `model_start` and `model_headers`. `--cold-gate` (500) and `--warm-gate` (50) come from the gate. Turn 1 passes when POST to response headers is below the cold gate. A later turn passes when Worker TTFT minus native nanocode TTFT on the same model and turn is at most the warm gate. The page colors each native cell against its pair's best rival and prints the difference. It adds a Worker gate table, a turn 1 headers chart and per-cycle warm-difference charts with dashed gate lines, and a line for each pair without a native sample in the window (its configured lanes and last error, or the unit's `--lanes`). One local cycle on the hosted Hand (`--lanes=`, prod Worker): claude-haiku-5-5 headers 1,050 ms on turn 1 (`worker` 138, `model_start` 61), gpt-6-luna 961, glm-5.3 818, all above the 500 ms gate (n = 1 each). Checks: `tsc --strict --types node` on the file, and `/` rendered in Chromium with no page errors.
- The public route forwards a request through the `SessionPool` object when the routing isolate has no binding for the session (`Pool.forward`, `SessionPool.forward`): the pool resolves the binding and calls the session object in the same request, and returns the object ID in `x-nanocode-session-object`, which the route caches and removes. Before, a binding miss cost one round trip to the pool and then one to the session object. On the demo (build-6cb0e8d/491fe83, hosted Hand, 24 sessions of 3 turns per provider and host, 2026-11-14) a warm turn's `session_resolve` was 73/86 ms p50/p99 on workers.dev, where the Cache API is not available, and 8 to 12/92 to 100 ms on cloudflare.nanocode.xyz, and a cold turn's was 55 to 98 ms p50. A cached binding still dispatches to the object directly, and an upgrade request keeps `Pool.resolve`. `Pool.warm` refills the pool and returns its ready count, for a host's cron trigger. The route reports `session_route;desc=pool|object` in `server-timing`. Checked: `npm run check:types`, and durable-cloudflare's local workerd 3-turn sessions with a bound, sealed `SESSION_POOL` (claude and codex, 3 rounds each, turn 2 from a new isolate, with and without `SESSION_POOL_BINDING_CACHE_S`): 30 of 30 turns finished.
- The SDK moves to nanocode 1d4463636 (hedged first-byte model requests), and `wasm.rs` gives both harnesses one `Hedging` per isolate, kept in a `thread_local` and replaced when the policy changes, so the rolling first-byte p90 and the hedge cap span the isolate's sessions. `AgentConfiguration.hedge` (`cap_percent`, `floor_ms`, `window`) sets the policy, and each omitted field takes `HedgePolicy::DEFAULT` (1.5 s floor, p90, 10% cap, 100 requests), so a host that sends no `hedge` hedges with the CLI's defaults, and `cap_percent: 0` sends no duplicate. A `cap_percent` over 100 rejects the configuration. A hedge is reported on `model.call.completed` as `hedge` and counted in the call's cost (see nanocode DECISIONS, 2026-11-14). Checked: `cargo check --target wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `cargo clippy --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` with no findings in `src/wasm.rs`, and `npm run check:types`. Not yet measured on the Worker.
- Pin nanocode 8467d6aa0 (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: memory lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 1d4463636 to 8467d6aa0, whose turn log packs snapshots as a stream, decodes one logical row at a time at open, and drops events from the fold once their row is committed. In scripts/hillclimb.mjs (durable-cloudflare, in-object, `--batch 100 --rate 200`, one run each), the parent peak WebAssembly memory at 10,000 children went from 1,244 MB (124,420 B per child) to 585 MB (58,537 B per child), and at 2,000 children from 187,728 to 61,669 B per child. The 2,000-child crash gate and the 10,000-child turn scenario pass with 0 lost and 0 repeated. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`, `npm run check:types`.
- Session object and pool timings in `server-timing` (2026-11-14, worker cold p99 lane). The pooled object start (Worker to the session object's first byte, less `session;dur`) is 677 to 1,108 ms at p99 on the demo, and the existing timings cannot tell an evicted pooled object from a slow route or a shared isolate. Each `AgentSession` response now appends `object_age;dur=<ms since the constructor>;desc=<requests served>`, `object_isolate;dur=<ms since the first object in the isolate>;desc=<objects constructed in the isolate>`, `object_prestart;dur=<ms since this object's last prestart>` when the object took one, and `object_isolate_prestart;dur=<ms since any prestart in the isolate>`. `SessionPool.forward` appends `pool_claim;dur=<claim ms>;desc=bound|named|pooled`, `pool_object;dur=<pool to session headers ms>`, and for a pooled claim `pool_ready;dur=<ms since the id entered ready>` and `pool_ping;dur=<ms since its last answered prestart>` when this pool instance pinged it. A claimed object without `object_prestart` was constructed again after its prestart (evicted). `Pool.resolve` keeps its contract and shares `claim` with `forward`. Checked: `npm run check:types`, and the durable-cloudflare local pool harness (output/pool-sla.mjs, 3 rounds of 3 turns per provider on miniflare): 18 of 18 turns completed with the timings present.
- A prestarted pooled session object holds a timer until its next keepalive (2026-11-14, worker cold p99 lane). The demo timings from build-d867d11 (24 single-turn claims from the hosted Hand, 15:55 UTC) show that a ready object stays in memory only while its last prestart is recent: 14 claimed objects whose prestart was 0.8 to 12.6 s old kept `object_prestart`, and 10 whose prestart was 10.3 to 18.9 s old (or whose pool instance had no ping on record) were constructed again at the claim. Cloudflare removes an idle Durable Object that it can hibernate after about 10 s, and the prestart left no pending work, so the 30 s `SESSION_POOL_KEEPALIVE_MS` ping restarted objects that were already gone. A reconstructed object in a live isolate started in 155 to 324 ms, and in a new isolate in 626 to 762 ms (Worker to session headers, less `session;dur`), against 122 to 190 ms for an object still in memory. `SessionPool` now sends `x-nanocode-prestart-hold-ms` with the keepalive interval on each prestart, and the session object keeps a timer of that length, so it is not hibernatable between pings. The first request that is not a prestart clears the timer. An object that the pool stops pinging becomes hibernatable when the timer ends. An invalid header value rejects the prestart. Checked: `npm run check:types`, and the durable-cloudflare local pool harness on miniflare (3 rounds of 3 turns per provider): 18 of 18 turns completed, with the pool refilled by prestarts that carry the header.
- Per-colo ready pools (`SessionShard`, 2026-11-14, regional pool lane). The demo build-e1ed915 measured the Worker to session object round trip at 66/110/301 ms (p50/p90/p99), 47 ms p50 from CMH and 100/128/725 ms from SJC, because every pooled object starts near the one `SessionPool` object (`idFromName('pool')`, no location hint). When the host binds `SESSION_SHARDS`, the public route forwards a binding miss to the `SessionShard` named by the request's `cf.colo` (`Pool.forwardShard`). The shard is created from that colo, so it and the objects it starts with `newUniqueId` run near the colo. The shard takes its oldest ready object and offers it to `SessionPool.bind`, which stays the one binding directory: it returns an existing binding, a named ID before `seal` or for a legacy name, or binds the offered ID. A shard returns an unused offer to its ready set. The ready set (prestart, hold header, keepalive alarm, refill) moved into `readySet`, which both classes use, and `SessionPool` keeps its own ready set for `Pool.resolve` callers without a request colo. `server-timing` adds `pool_bind` and `session_route;desc=shard`. Without `SESSION_SHARDS` or `cf.colo`, the route is `SessionPool.forward` as before. Validation: `npm run check:types`, and 6 local Miniflare 3-turn sessions through the durable-cloudflare harness with a `SESSION_SHARDS` binding (all done, `pool_claim;desc=pooled` after `seal`, `pool_bind` 1 to 12 ms).
- A streamed turn starts the pump after its response takes the first event chunk (2026-11-14, cold open lane). The demo probe after build-e1ed915 (96 cold turns from the hosted Hand) found 11 turns over 400 ms to edge headers, and 10 of them had a `runtime_open_ms` of 300 to 630 ms: the object returned the event stream at admission, and the pump then opened the turn's runtime in the same isolate before the response left it. `EventHub.stream` now takes `options.consumed`, which it calls once at the first `pull` after the first chunk (the consumer read it), at `cancel` or at a stream error. An accepted streamed turn starts `pump(true)` from that callback instead of at admission, so the runtime open runs after the response head is released. The turn is durable before the response returns, and the admission alarm (`RECOVERY_DELAY_MS`) starts the pump if the stream is never read. Turn timings add `respond_ms` (admission to the returned response), `consumed_ms` (admission to the first chunk read) and `commit_wait_ms` (the `storage.sync` before the runtime open), which separate a held output gate from runtime open CPU. Validated with `npm run check:types` and the local miniflare harness (`output/pool-sla.mjs --rounds=3`, 18 of 18 turns on claude and codex, cold dispatch 111 to 145 ms against 114 to 152 ms before).
- Per-colo ready pools removed (2026-11-14, regional pool lane). durable-cloudflare deployed build-d2294f1 with `SESSION_SHARDS` (version a3ffe492). Warm claude-haiku-5-5 turns from gak-9 (96 sessions, 288 turns, 0 errors) show that an object started near SJC cuts the Worker to object round trip from 100 to 35 ms at p50. The same object commits admission in 88/227/409 ms (p50/p90/p99, against 17/26/114 near the pool), and its provider first byte is 732 ms p50 against 674. The server first delta from SJC is 833/1235/1802 ms, against 761/1017/1305 before. A session from the hosted Hand also routes through SEA, IAD and EWR, so a colo key does not keep one session in one region. Object placement near the colo costs more in storage commit and provider distance than it saves in the round trip. This commit reverts `SessionShard`, `SessionPool.bind`, `readySet` and `Pool.forwardShard`, and durable-cloudflare 2b1ba6f deletes the class with migration v11.
- Turn timings add `log_commit_ms` and `alarm_commit_ms` (2026-11-14, cold open lane): the admission time at which a `storage.sync` started before and after the admission `setAlarm` resolves. On build-ee39952 the cold turns over 400 ms to edge headers show `runtime_open_ms` near 0 and `consumed_ms` of 414 to 1,457 ms, so the response waits in the session object after it is returned and before its first chunk is read, while the runtime open no longer runs. The two values tell whether the turn log commit or the alarm write holds the output gate.
- A prestart commits the `prestarted` metadata row (2026-11-14, cold commit lane). On build-02309cc the cold turns over 500 ms to edge headers wait on the output gate for the first admission commit of a claimed pooled object: 64/130/876 ms (p50/p90/p99, n = 92, gak-9) against 35 to 39/47 to 50/111 to 116 ms on warm turns. Each prestart and pool keepalive now writes `prestarted` with the prestart time and returns 204 after `storage.sync` resolves, so the object commits once every keepalive before a claim. Responses report the last prestart commit as `object_prestart_commit;dur=<ms from the prestart request>`. Validated with `npm run check:types`, `npm run build:ts` and a local pooled 3-turn run (12 of 12 turns done).
- A prestart of an unbound session object sets its alarm to the prestart time plus `x-nanocode-prestart-hold-ms` (2026-11-14, cold commit lane). With build-c511607 the prestart commit takes 18 ms p50, but the first admission commit of a claimed pooled object is still 76/601/613 ms (p50/p90/p99, n = 16), and that commit is the first one on the object with an alarm. Each keepalive moves the alarm forward, so the admission `setAlarm` changes a pending alarm. A bound object (an `authority` row) keeps its recovery alarm. Validated with `npm run check:types`, `npm run build:ts` and a local pooled 3-turn run (12 of 12 turns done).
- Pin nanocode a5d401e0c (2026-11-14, Terminal-Bench 2.1 Codex parity, wave 3). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 8467d6aa0 to a5d401e0c, which brings Codex sessions to codex-cli 0.166.0's request: no `reasoning.summary` for gpt-6 catalog models and a 10000 ms `exec_command` yield in the exec description (96dfd92c5), a reprompt instead of a placeholder answer when a final message is empty (778e5ad4a), and no `<code_mode_orchestration>` developer block (a5d401e0c), along with the durability, CLI and dependency commits between the two pins. Checked with node 24.22.2: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `npm run prepare`, `npm run check:types`.
- Revert the prestart alarm of an unbound session object (2026-11-14, cold commit lane). build-024956e (durable-cloudflare 2ec7b78, version 1031a1e6) set the alarm at each prestart. It did not shorten the first admission commit of a claimed pooled object: 60/136/4,888 ms (p50/p90/p99, n = 86, gak-9) and 55/123/399 ms (n = 60, hosted Hand), against 64/78/173 and 65/88/229 ms with build-c511607 alone. Cold turns over 500 ms to edge headers rose from 0 of 192 to 5 of 86 (gak-9) and 1 of 60 (hosted Hand). The prestart commit of build-c511607 stays.
- `SessionPool` hedges the first turn of a claimed pooled session object (2026-11-14, cold commit lane). With `SESSION_POOL_HEDGE_MS` set, the pool sends a `POST /internal/turns` on a pooled claim with `x-nanocode-hedge` (the session key). If the response headers have not returned after `SESSION_POOL_HEDGE_MS`, the pool takes a second ready object and sends it the same request, and the first response wins. The binding moves to the winner before the response returns. A held object admits the turn and sets the recovery alarm in the admission commit, but it does not start the pump. `POST /internal/hedge` with `won` starts the pump, and `lost` destroys the object after its response headers arrive. If a verdict does not arrive, the recovery alarm reads `SessionPool.bound` and runs or destroys the turn. One turn therefore runs on one object only. The cause is the admission commit tail of a newly claimed object: build-c511607 cold edge headers were 204/582/1,557 ms (p50/p90/p99, n = 48, gak-9, 19:00 UTC) with a claim commit of 70/420/1,290 ms, and 201/268/635 (n = 22, gak-9) and 172/863/2,152 (n = 19, hosted Hand) at 19:25 UTC. The public handler removes `x-nanocode-hedge` from client requests. Without `SESSION_POOL_HEDGE_MS` the pool forwards as before.
- `App.create` takes `hedge`, the host's hedge policy for every session object it opens (2026-11-14, Georgios's TB2.1 Codex gate: hedged requests in eval sessions). Since c1fe48c an omitted `AgentConfiguration.hedge` hedges with `HedgePolicy::DEFAULT`, and `App` sent none, so every Cloudflare host hedged with no way to choose otherwise. nanoeval's sessions did: the `model.call.completed` events of nanoeval run 16e15f9a (build-281c7dd, gpt-6-luna, 439 calls) carry 47 dispatched hedges (43 won by the primary, 4 by the hedge) and 49 capped ones, and each dispatched hedge resent the whole input on a second WebSocket, 1,205,127 input tokens on top of the 11,954,285 the calls used (10.1%), which the run's cost counts and the ChatGPT plan pays; run 0d6c29c9 (104 calls) has 10 hedges and 7.4%. An evaluator compares against CLIs that send one request per call, so it needs `hedge: { cap_percent: 0 }`. `options.hedge` goes into the configuration of every session, child and prompt-cache compile alike (the compile shares the isolate's `Hedging`, which `wasm.rs` replaces when the policy changes); omitted, the configuration is as before. Checked: `npm run check:types`; the end-to-end check is nanoeval's slow-first-byte lane (see nanoeval DECISIONS, 2026-11-14).
- `psh-revision.json` pins nanocode f24d9b2a9, and `scripts/psh.mjs` exports `crates/core/compression`, `crates/core/digest` and `crates/core/windows-sys` (2026-11-14, psh conformance). The pinned psh takes bash's `--verbose`, `--debug`, `--rcfile` and trailing `-o`/`-O` listings at invocation (5712a5ce2), and matches bash 5.3 in declaration expansion, `local -`, `set +`, hash, trap PIPESTATUS, `printf -v` subscripts, nameref depth and `${!ref:=word}` (f24d9b2a9). Upstream psh now depends on nanocode-compression, nanocode-digest and nanocode-windows-sys, so the export at 8ad5f93ad's path list fails to load the workspace. `psh_wasm_core.wasm` goes from 6,923,739 to 7,007,610 bytes. Checked: `node scripts/psh.mjs`, a `Bash.create` smoke run of `local -`, `${!z:=foo}`, quoted `declare`, `printf -v 'a[x[1]]'`, `set + -Z` and a utils pipeline, and `npm run check:types`.
- `scripts/build.mjs`, `psh.mjs` and `python.mjs` run `cargo fetch --locked` in each Cargo workspace before they build it (root, `interpreter/`, the psh source and `python/`), and every build passes `--offline` (2026-11-14, Georgios's TB2.1 Codex gate: release path). Builds here run with `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true` (the release lane's tsbuild.sh sets it, and the pins above check with `--offline`), so after `~/.cargo/registry` was removed on dev-georgios each workspace needed a hand-run `cargo fetch`. `wasm.mjs`'s `fetchCrates` runs the fetch with `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE` removed from its environment, so it downloads only what the cache lacks and needs no network when the cache is complete; the builds after it never reach the network. The scripts are inputs of the WASM fingerprint, so the next release builds and uploads a new WASM asset. Before and after on dev-georgios at 6fb41b5 (nanocode a5d401e0c, psh f24d9b2a9), each with its own empty `CARGO_HOME`, `CARGO_NET_OFFLINE=true`, `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=8`: `node scripts/build.mjs` exits 101 with `error: no matching package named `blake3` found`; with this change it fetches 201 MB and builds all four modules (nanocode_wasm 13,127,177 bytes, interpreter 1,213,960, psh 6,393,277, python 28,636,970) in 8 min 3 s.
- Revert the `SessionPool` hedge of the first turn of a pooled session object (2026-11-14, cold commit lane). build-ddca280 with `SESSION_POOL_HEDGE_MS` 183 (durable-cloudflare 23fa1f7, version 7fbbed6c) did not hold the cold gate. `probe.ts --sessions=12` (48 sessions, 144 turns, 0 errors per client) gave cold edge headers of 187/294/709 ms (p50/p90/p99) with 1 of 48 over 500 ms from gak-9, against 181/294/939 ms with 3 of 67 on build-c511607 in the same hour, and 146/227/1,327 ms with 3 of 48 from the hosted Hand, against 155/248/2,152 ms with 2 of 48. The hedge fired on 3 and 1 cold turns. The slowest turns spent 585 to 632 ms in the first object's `session` time before the admission commit, and a second object did not answer sooner, so the tail is not specific to one claimed object.
- The first live `assistant.delta` of a turn leaves `EventHub.stream` in the same millisecond as its `created_at` (`streamed_at` minus `created_at` is 0 to 1 ms over 40 warm haiku-5-5 turns on the demo, 2026-11-14), and its client receipt trails `created_at` by as much as the `queued` frame of the same turn (59 against 65 ms p50). A long stream does not receive its first delta sooner than a short reply, so no output gate or coalescing window holds the first delta. The remaining delay is the network path from the session object to the client, and durable-cloudflare's DECISIONS.md holds the measurement.
- Pin nanocode 2dff921f7 (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: memory lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode eb678f3bb to 2dff921f7, whose turn log keeps one deflate window, folds records as it decodes them, collects unreachable records through chunk manifests at open (50,434 records, 29.2 MB at 10,000 children, where it collected 0 before), moves SQL row bodies instead of cloning them, and moves the written row body into its stored rows. On a 10,000-child in-object parent (scripts/hillclimb.mjs `turn` scenario), peak WebAssembly memory went from 566,624,256 to 385,155,072 B, restart to ready from 1,506 to 1,034 ms and to the first model call from 5,104 to 4,158 ms, with 0 lost. Natively, memory held after `TurnLog::open` on the 10,000-child log went from 257.9 MB to 95.6 MB. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`, `npm run check:types`.
- Codex sessions on a hand see only the hand tools codex-cli has (2026-11-14, Terminal-Bench 2.1 Codex parity, wave 4). The pin stays at nanocode 2dff921f7, which contains 2922b399b, and every session now gets a hand tool selection: `remote::ToolSelections::new` adds a `codex` entry that excludes `nanocode::claude::HAND_TOOLS` (Bash, Edit, Read, TaskOutput, TaskStop, Write) when the host's `hand_tools` names none, and an explicit `hand_tools.codex` (such as `{}`) replaces it. A Claude session keeps every hand tool, and with 2922b399b a selection that keeps every tool sends none, so Claude sessions and hands that cannot select tools behave as before. Before, a codex cell's `ALL_TOOLS` on nanocode-cli-0bbcb64 was apply_patch, exec_command, view_image, write_stdin, Read, Edit, Write, Bash, TaskStop, hands (nanoeval gateway e2e lane on build-67dea25, and a gpt-6-luna TB2.1 trace called `tools.Bash` 13 times); after, apply_patch, exec_command, view_image, write_stdin, hands. Checked with node 24.22.2: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `npm run prepare`, `npm run check:types`; nanoeval's gateway e2e lane (`NANOEVAL_E2E_BACKEND=dir NANOEVAL_E2E_GATEWAY=1`, 3 tasks), which asserts a codex cell's `ALL_TOOLS`, fails on build-67dea25 and passes on this tree, and its Claude lane passes.
- A streaming turn POST (`accept: text/event-stream`) returns its `text/event-stream` headers from the Worker before the session object admits the turn, and `relayTurn` relays the session object stream after them (2026-11-14, cold POST to headers p99 lane, 3c7077e). 77286b6 split the cold header time first: `SessionPool.forward` reports `pool_in`, `pool_commit`, `pool_total` and `object_out`, the Worker reports `pool_out`, and the session object reports `object_in` and one `object_<step>` duration per admission step. On build-77286b6 (durable-cloudflare f2a4cd5, version f334eff9, `probe.ts --sessions=12`, 48 three-turn sessions per client, 0 errors), the pooled cold turns from gak-9 gave `pool_in` 26/44/51 ms (p50/p90/p99), `pool_commit` 18/20/22, `object_in` 28/37/46, `session` 16/27/44 (all of it `object_subscribe`; `object_instance`, `object_admit`, `object_submit`, `object_alarm` and `object_events` 0), `object_out` 74/100/459 and `pool_out` 10/51/66. The hosted Hand gave `object_out` 77/100/383 and `pool_commit` 18/20/23. The two turns over 500 ms (529 and 514 ms) spent 459 and 383 ms in `object_out`, and the first had `log_commit_ms` 464: the output gate of the session object holds its response until the first admission commit. The pool binding commit and the wasm instance are not on the tail, so they stay unchanged. A refused or failed admission arrives in the stream as `event: error` with `{ body, status, retry_after? }`, and a 2xx JSON answer as `event: response`. `Transport` notes the error as it noted an error status (and reconnects on 408, 429 and 5xx while it reconnects), and `Cloud.follow` throws it as `failure` did. The downstream server-timing moves into a leading `: server-timing` comment of the stream. After the change (build-3c7077e, durable-cloudflare 8e39e29, version 9b2b7d2d), cold edge headers were 0/0/0 ms with 0 of 96 over 500 ms from gak-9 and the hosted Hand (before: 175/240/529 with 1 of 48, and 189/240/514 with 1 of 48). The cold client first delta did not change: 951/2,102 ms (claude) and 1,223/2,355 ms (codex) from gak-9 against 969/2,192 and 1,340/2,109 before. A POST with an empty prompt returns 200 headers and `event: error` with status 400 and the session object body.
- A warm streamed turn sends its first model request before the session object writes the admission alarm (2026-11-14, warm TTFT lane, 581ed28). A Durable Object output gate holds every outgoing message, provider fetches included, until each write made before it commits. Before, the admission `setAlarm` committed in 34 ms p50, and the pump waited for the response's first chunk, which waited for that commit, so the provider request started 33 to 36 ms after admission. Now `AgentSession` takes a direct path when the turn is streamed and accepted, the pump is idle, the scope is the owner's, the turn names no hand and the resident runtime matches the turn's opening key (`resident`, with the key in `opening`). On that path it starts the pump and waits until the runtime's `dispatch`, `socket.open` or `socket.admit` calls `dispatching`, or until the pump ends, and only then writes the alarm and returns the stream. The fetch therefore leaves before the write, and the output gate still holds the stream until the alarm and turn-log writes commit, so no event of an unadmitted turn reaches a client. If the alarm write rejects, `ctx.abort` resets the object, which cancels the provider request, and the client reads a failed stream. A commit that fails after it is issued breaks the output gate, which also resets the object. The SDK writes no row before a warm turn's first model request: a streamed admission is buffered (`submit_buffered`), so this change is host plumbing only and the SDK is unchanged. Measured on the demo from gak-9 (`gk2/inter3.sh`, `wprobe2.mjs`, claude-haiku-5-5, 20 interleaved native and Worker sessions of 3 turns each, 0 errors). The before run was on version f334eff9 (build-77286b6). The after run was on 9b2b7d2d (build-3c7077e, which also returns the turn's stream headers from the Worker before admission). Provider request start after admission, p50: turn 3 went from 33 to 13 ms (9 to 27 ms over all 20 sessions), and turn 2 stayed at 36 ms, because on turn 2 the runtime's `commit_wait` `storage.sync` waits 15 to 31 ms for a write made at admission. Worker minus native first delta, p50: turn 2 went from +214 to +208 ms and turn 3 from +247 to +223 ms. The gap remains far above the +50 ms gate, and the round trip between the edge and the session object is most of what remains.
- The second streamed turn of a session writes no row before its first model request (2026-11-14, warm TTFT lane, a190f20). Before a190f20, the first admission left `codex_orchestration:<scope>` unset, so the second admission inserted the `code_mode` default, and the `storage.sync` at the start of the pump (`commit_wait_ms`) waited for that commit before the runtime dispatched. A local workerd trace of every non-SELECT statement, alarm write and `storage.sync` per turn (durable-cloudflare `scripts/local-runtime.mjs` with the statement log added, a mock Anthropic stream, two sessions of three turns per build) shows one `INSERT INTO metadata` before the pump's `storage.sync` on turn 2 with build-3c7077e and none on turn 3. With build-a190f20 turn 2 has none, the same as turn 3. In both builds the SDK writes its first turn-log row only on turn 1 and its turn-log update only after dispatch, so the SDK floor commit is not on the warm path and the SDK stays unchanged. On the demo with build-a190f20 (durable-cloudflare bd375e4), 20 interleaved haiku-5-5 sessions of three turns from the hosted Hand (`wa.mjs`, 0 errors) gave turn 2 `commit_wait_ms` 0 ms p50 (1 ms max) and the provider request 13 ms p50 (17 ms p90) after admission start, against 14 ms (27 ms p90) on turn 3. One turn 2 on build-3c7077e from dev-georgios before the deploy had `commit_wait_ms` 18 and the provider request at 26 ms.
- `psh-revision.json` pins nanocode 93ae28965 (2026-11-14, psh conformance). The 15 psh commits since f24d9b2a9 (a06d169c5 to 93ae28965) close conformance gaps across declarations and namerefs, traps and signals, program paths and invocation (`-r`, `-i`, `-D`), word expansion, globbing and here-documents, parse errors, xtrace and messages. psh-spec in-process on Linux goes from brush 3033 to 3133 of 3171, oils 2419 to 2498 of 2511, with agent 727 and utils 999 of 1001 unchanged and no new failures. `scripts/psh.mjs` needs no new paths. `psh_wasm_core.wasm` goes from 7,007,610 to 7,188,289 bytes. Checked: `node scripts/psh.mjs`, a `Bash.create` smoke run (nameref element assignment, ERR traps of subshells, pipelines and functions, brace padding and extglob, a here-document with arithmetic, `return 1 2`, `set -r` refusing cd, a utils pipeline), and `npm run check:types`.
- Codex sessions on a hand get no hand placement block in their AGENTS.md instructions (2026-11-14, Terminal-Bench 2.1 Codex parity, wave 5). A session with hands or `remote` appended nanocode's `PLACEMENT_INSTRUCTIONS` (Code Mode scripts run on one machine, `hand: "<name>"` routes a call, "query pwd through the shell when an absolute path is needed") to `environment.project_instructions`, which the codex backend renders as an `# AGENTS.md instructions` user message. codex-cli sends no such block: its model learns the working directory only from `<environment_context>`, and the `hands` tool's description already says how to route a call. `open` now resolves the codex environment from the host's `environment` before the block is appended (a default one when the host gives none and the session places cells, as before, so `<environment_context>` keeps its cwd and date), and only the Claude environment gets the block, as before. The pin stays at nanocode 3bab4efb3, which contains 0bf3b0c0a: its codex turn reports the hand's shell in `<environment_context>` (`<shell>bash</shell>` where it said `javascript`) and its native hand reports bash when `SHELL` is unset. Checked with node 24.22.2: `node scripts/build.mjs`, `npm run prepare`, `npm run check:types`; nanoeval's gateway e2e lane (`NANOEVAL_E2E_BACKEND=dir NANOEVAL_E2E_GATEWAY=1`, 3 tasks), which now asserts that no codex request carries "query pwd" and that each carries `<shell>bash</shell>`, fails on build-a7fdac2 with nanocode-cli-0bbcb64 (the placement block) and passes on this tree.
- Pin nanocode 291f0dc7e (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: finished-child memory). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 3bab4efb3 to 291f0dc7e, where `LocalCells::begin_turn` forgets a session's finished cell journals and the turn log collects unreachable subagent journal records before each snapshot instead of only at open. In scripts/hillclimb.mjs (durable-cloudflare, 2,000 in-object children, `--batch 100 --rate 200`, two runs each), parent peak WebAssembly memory went from 99.8 and 96.7 MB (49,906 and 48,333 B per child) to 65.0 and 65.3 MB (32,506 and 32,637 B per child), with 2.18 rows per child on all runs, and the 2,000-child crash gate passes in all four scenarios with 0 lost and 0 repeated. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`, `npm run check:types`.
- bench(hands): `bench/hands.ts` takes `--rtt-ms=N`, which delays every frame between the WASM brain and the hand by N/2 ms each way, and `--tools-only=1`, which starts the native hand with `--tools-only` so the brain's `LocalCells` runs the cell and sends each nested call to the hand; cells `exec-hand` and `exec-hand-seq-20` name `hand: 'bench'` so both placements reach the same hand (2026-11-14, Terminal-Bench 2.1 bet B5 "Code Mode v2": cells in the sandbox instead of the brain). What it establishes: Code Mode v2 is already how the eval runs. nanoeval's slot starts `nanocode hand <url> --name <n> -C <workdir> --own-processes` (sandbox/slot.sh), without `--tools-only`, so the hand advertises cells (`RemoteCodeModeHost::runs_cells`), and a `hands: 'always'` session places its cells on the hand that sent the prompt (`CodeModeExecutors::resolve`; `LocalCells` is only the fallback). Only `exec`/`wait` and their results cross the Worker to hand wire; nested `exec_command`, `apply_patch` and `write_stdin` run in the sandbox. Measured on the hosted Hand (5 cores), nanocode-ts build-d5b21f1's `nanocode_wasm.wasm` (`NANOCODE_WASM_PATH`) against `nanocode hand` from nanocode-cli-0bf3b0c, `--provider=codex --n=10`, modes interleaved, two repetitions, cell medians in ms (cells on the hand / tools-only): one nested call per cell at RTT 0: 38, 26 / 24, 35; RTT 50: 71, 113 / 95, 98; RTT 100: 136, 143 / 138, 141. Twenty sequential nested calls per cell at RTT 0: 127, 117 / 127, 207; RTT 50: 116, 172 / 1467, 1543; RTT 100: 201, 214 / 2599, 2461. So a hand-placed cell costs one wire round trip whatever it calls, and a brain-placed cell one round trip per nested call (about 2.4 s extra for 20 calls at 100 ms). Frames per 20-call cell: hand-placed 43 in and 5 out (the hand's live tool events, never awaited), tools-only 22 in and 42 out. The same comparison with the native CLI as brain (`nanocode exec --runner-connect` through a TCP relay adding N/2 ms each way, mock Responses server, 21 calls per cell, 3 repetitions) gave loop medians 110/85/85 ms (cells) against 280/1413/2408 ms (tools-only) at RTT 0/50/100, and six one-command cells per session cost 30, 75 and 130 ms each at RTT 0/50/100 after a first cell of 90 to 330 ms (session open). Nothing ships to the eval from this: there is no remaining per-tool-call round trip to remove. Files: bench/handbench.ts, bench/hands.ts.
- `App.progress()` reads the newest turn log sequence through the `nanocode_log` primary key (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: root lane). `SELECT coalesce(max(seq), 0) FROM nanocode_log` scanned every row, because `seq` is the second column of the `(scope, seq)` primary key, and a background pump calls `progress()` for its resets tripwire and on each first dispatch. At 10,000 children on nanocode-ts 4dbb05e it read 118,990 of the parent's 159,461 SQL rows (363 calls of 328 rows). Now a recursive CTE steps through the distinct scopes with `min(scope)` above the previous one and takes `max(seq)` per scope, so a call reads about two rows per scope instead of the whole log (the demo's parent object has one scope, `owner`, and a call reads 6 rows). The value does not change: Node 24.22.3 `node:sqlite` (SQLite 3.54.1) returns the same value and type for both statements on a parent object's log copied from a 10,000-child run and on 500 random tables with 0 to 50 scopes and deleted rows. In durable-cloudflare 9b251e0 scripts/hillclimb.mjs (in-object, `--agents 2000 --batch 100 --rate 200 --scenarios score`, bundles from nanocode-ts e1ed915 on nanocode 1ea12e58c with nanocode f824cf638, two interleaved pairs under the bench lock): progress() 49 calls reading 3,165 rows to 53 calls reading 318, sql.rows_read 11,131 to 8,288; and 61 calls reading 3,995 rows to 50 calls reading 348, sql.rows_read 11,970 to 8,318. Workerd CPU (41.0 to 42.5 and 50.0 to 43.9 ms per child) and wall time stay within the run-to-run spread of the shared host. The 2,000-child crash gate (`--gate-agents 2000 --scenarios spawn,turn,done,twice`) reports 0 lost in every scenario with and without this change, but both bundles fail it on `repeated_outside_in_flight` (1 or 2 children with a third model call; with the change turn 1, done 2, twice 2; without it turn 2, done 0, twice 1), so the failure comes from that base and not from this statement. Checks: `npm run check:types`.

The native Claude client already sends the marker to api.anthropic.com. Measured with `node scripts/hillclimb.mjs --agents 500 --gate-agents 500 --batch 100 --rate 200 --latency 1700 --scenarios done` in durable-cloudflare e3f4949, with the mock latency above the 1.5 s hedge floor, on one bundle (nanocode 2dff921f7, nanocode-ts 0a14153) before and after this change in `index.js`: before, 1,017 child calls, 0 hedges, 17 children repeated, gate failed. After, 1,000 child calls, 16 hedges, 0 repeated, gate passed. `src/machines/cloudflare/Egress.ts` and `src/machines/vercel/Wallets.ts` filter with the Claude list too and are unchanged. `npm run check:types` passes.
