- `src/machines/cloudflare/Egress.ts` and `src/machines/vercel/Wallets.ts` forward `x-nanocode-hedge` to the provider (2026-11-14, nanocode 1.0 durability track: hedgemark lane). Both egresses filter a model dispatch's headers with one list that admitted only `accept`, `content-type`, `anthropic-version` and `anthropic-beta`, so the marker that nanocode d23a902c6 and 75aec9576 put on a hedged request did not reach the Tempo gateway, the same defect that 22d02ad fixed in `Gateway.create`. A hedge from a machines session then reached the provider as an unmarked second request at the primary's conversation position. Both lists now admit `x-nanocode-hedge`. The other egress paths in this package keep the marker: `src/vercel/Gateway.ts` copies every dispatch header, and `Account.forward` copies every request header. `npm run check:types` passes.
- Machine payment deposits are host policy, access keys are reused, and idle channels close at logout (2026-11-15). `Payment.Config.deposit` (`{ maximum, opening, topUp }`, in MACH) replaces the constants in `Payments.ts` (0.05 opening and top-up, 0.5 cap) and `internal/Core.ts` (0.2 opening and top-up, 2 cap). `Payment.resolve` returns it as `Policy.deposit`, `Machines.create`, `Wallets.create` (Cloudflare and Vercel) and `Account.create` pass it to the payer, and `Payments.create` takes it as `options.deposit`. Without it the payer sends no `depositRaw`, `maxDeposit` or `topUpAmount`, so mppx 0.16.0 opens with the server's `suggestedDeposit` or the request amount and tops up by the server's suggestion or the exact shortfall, bounded only by the access key's spending limit. `Wallet.connect` returns the stored key while `restore` accepts it and `Wallet.reusable` finds it not yet on Tempo, or on Tempo, not revoked and with MACH limit left; `approve` in `react/Machines.tsx` uses the same check. Before, a connect after a spent or revoked key reused it and failed with `SpendingLimitExceeded`, and every `Wallet.connect` made a new key and so new channels per payee. Both payers record the paid URL of each session channel from its receipt (`url:<channelId>` in the Worker store, `<prefix>url.<channelId>` in localStorage), and `close()` sends a cooperative close credential (`action: 'close'` at the channel's cumulative amount) to that URL for each open channel whose deposit exceeds its cumulative amount, returning `Payment.Closing[]`. The Worker payer keeps a channel that passes `maximum` under `idle:` instead of dropping it, so close reaches it too, and `DELETE /v1/keys` closes before it forgets the key and answers `closed`. MCP channels and channels without a recorded URL are reported as failed, not closed. An analysis of one mainnet payer from March to November 2026 found 558 session channels, 316 of them settled under one cent in 712 transactions, 855 top-ups, and about $255 left in unclosed channels. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `npm run build:ts` (the WebAssembly module is unchanged).
- Pin nanocode d55bd904b (2026-11-15, nanocode 1.0 durability track: restart). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 30221fc5f to d55bd904b, whose turn log garbage walk indexes first holders in a hash map and removes unreached records in one retain pass. On the 10,000-child parent log (128.3 MB plain snapshot), `TurnLog::open` pinned to one core went from 539-614 ms to 419-490 ms over 3 interleaved rounds, with the same 50,434 records collected, the same snapshot digest and the same history. Checks: `cargo check --locked --lib --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --profile wasm`, `npm run check:types`.
- Plan-payer sessions skip the per-request `signedIn` RPC, and the egress sends with the remembered plan credential (2026-11-15, nanocode 1.0 latency gate: same-credential warm lane). On a warm plan turn, two `PlanVault` RPCs were on the path to the provider request. `AgentSession.planned` called `subscription` (`PlanVault.signedIn`) before every request of a session with a bound provider, which was the 17 to 25 ms p50 of `admission_ms` and `m_start` on warm turns. `Egress.send` then called `inject`, 26 to 40 ms p50 and 81 to 109 ms p90. Now `planned` sets `subscription` (or `codex_subscription`) without the RPC when `App.create` has `plans` and the session's bound payer is `plan`, because the plan egress checks the plan on every model request. A first turn still checks `signedIn` before admission, and other payers still call `subscription` on each request. The egress moves to nanocode-egress build-654d587, whose `send` dispatches with the last checked credential while `inject` checks the plan in parallel and aborts the request when the check throws. A plan that signs out mid-session therefore fails its next turn with the egress's 401 (`sign in to your Claude plan`) instead of a 403 at admission. On a scratch copy of nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare 519d210 with both changes behind a per-account switch (gak-9, 20 three-turn sessions per arm and model, interleaved with native nanocode, 0 errors), warm `admission_ms` and `m_start` fell from 17 to 22 ms to 1 ms p50 in all four cells, and the Worker minus native client first delta, p50, moved from +30/+157 to -6/-99 ms (sonnet-5-5, turns 2 and 3) and from +1/+12 to +113/-44 ms (haiku-5-5). The haiku turn 2 cell is provider variance: its overhead above the provider first byte stayed at 77 to 79 ms, and `m_first` was 548 against 469 ms. nanocodexyz/durable-cloudflare's DECISIONS.md holds the gate run on the released build.

- App option `remoteCoordinators` (2026-11-14, Nanocode 1.0 durability track, big-bet lane b: the sharded parent). With `remoteSubagents`, a remote child opens with `subagents: false` and takes every subagent tool from its parent through `child_tool`, so its own children land in the root object's registry and the root holds O(N) state. With `remoteCoordinators` the child object keeps its own subagents (in-object, under `maxConcurrentSubagents`) and receives only the parent's `submit_result`, renamed `parent__submit_result` because the child's own runtime registers `submit_result`; `childTool` maps the name back and refuses it from any session but the coordinator's own. The root then holds one remote child per coordinator, and each coordinator reports its children upward in one `child_turn` result. Default off, so no existing deployment changes. The native counterpart is measured with a harness in nanocode's ignored output/ (bet-shard lane NOTES.md). Checks: `npm run check:types` rc 0.
- `remoteCoordinators` measured (2026-11-15, Nanocode 1.0 durability track, bet-shard lane round 2). Workerd through `durable-cloudflare` `scripts/hillclimb.mjs --shard K` on a bundle of this commit's TypeScript over the build-e994fcf WebAssembly module, with `maxConcurrentSubagents` 64, `remoteSubagents` and `remoteCoordinators`, mock provider at 200 requests/s, one m4-metal-large box. 2,000 leaves under 40 coordinators (K 50): 40 of 40 coordinators done, 2,000 leaves, lost 0, root peak WebAssembly memory 11.6 MB (5.8 KB per leaf), 5 root calls, 78 root request bytes per leaf, saturation 0.728. 10,000 leaves under 100 coordinators (K 100): 100 of 100 done, lost 0, root peak 15.8 MB (1.6 KB per leaf), 8 root calls, 26 root request bytes per leaf, 0.08 root rows written per leaf, saturation 0.853, 14.7 ms workerd CPU per leaf. The root memory grows by 4.2 MB for five times the leaves, against 32.6 to 58.5 KB per child for in-object children. The 2,000-leaf crash gate (K 50, `--limit 240`) passes `done` (kill after all leaves: restart ready 812 ms, listing 8 ms, 0 calls after the restart, lost 0, repeated 0) and fails `spawn` and `turn` with `children did not all finish`: after a kill during spawn (ready 774 ms, listing 5,237 ms, first call 5,959 ms) all 40 coordinators finish but 3,934 of 4,000 leaf calls arrive, and after a kill during the turn (ready 813 ms, listing 3,238 ms) 43 coordinator completions arrive for 40 coordinators and 3,896 leaf calls. The native harness (bet-shard2 lane NOTES.md) shows the cause on the SDK side: a restored remote child is respawned under a new session id, so its object and its log are orphaned and its subtree runs again or is lost. Checks: `npm run check:types` rc 0, `npm run build:ts` rc 0.
- Re-send the root's in-flight model request when a session object wakes (2026-11-15, nanocode 1.0 durability track: restart to the first model call). The first model call after a restart waited on the whole open: TurnLog::open of the 10,000-child fold, restore_inner and the resume of about 5,000 interrupted children. `App.ts` now stores each root primary model dispatch (`subagent` null, no `x-nanocode-hedge`) in table `model_heads` (scope, id, egress dispatch header, request body) before the egress fetch. It deletes the row when the response stream ends, fails, is cancelled or its signal aborts, so a head exists only for a response that the turn log has not committed. The constructor sends every stored head through egress before any runtime opens. The restored root's dispatch with the same endpoint, headers, origin, provider and byte-identical body takes that response instead of a new request. A root dispatch that matches no head, or a finished root turn, aborts the scope's unclaimed heads and deletes their rows. A body with its header over the SQL value limit is not stored and counts `model_head_skips`. `/metrics` reports `model_head_writes`, `model_head_prefetches`, `model_head_claims`, `model_head_aborts` and `model_head_skips`. In-object children dispatch with a non-null `subagent`, so they write no head. Cost: one insert and one delete per root model call (rows written per wake 139 to 147 base, 144 to 150 head). A deploy between the crash and the wake changes the request bytes, so the head aborts and costs one provider call. Measured with durable-cloudflare `output/snap-prof3.mjs` on the restart3 10K snapshot re-saved with the root call in flight, mock provider, base build-e994fcf against the same tarball with this `App.js`, 4 interleaved pairs at host load 2.5 to 7.7 (2026-11-15, lane snapfirst3). The first root provider call after the session object wakes went from 3,180, 3,563, 4,837 and 6,977 ms to 55, 60, 66 and 264 ms (the 264 ms run started at load 5.4 and rose to 7.7). Each head run claimed 1 head with 0 aborts, and the head body reached the provider once. The first child call after the restart was 4.1 to 7.6 s base and 4.7 to 5.4 s head, except one head run at 16.8 s whose workerd start took 8.2 s at the same load rise. In two pairs the harness disposed workerd right after the first root provider call (`KILL_AFTER_PREFETCH=1`, no `/metrics` read) and restarted on the same state. The second wake claimed the head again, and no non-hedge root body reached the provider twice in one wake in any of the 8 runs. 2,000-child crash gate (`scripts/hillclimb.mjs --agents 2000 --gate-agents 2000 --batch 100 --rate 200`, scenarios spawn, turn, done and twice) passed with lost 0, repeated_after_done 0, repeated_outside_in_flight 0 and no duplicate root body. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `git diff --check`.

- Codex sessions take six per-session options that the nanocode CLI has as flags (2026-11-16). A turn POST may carry `compact_instructions`, `lean_tool_arguments` and `lenient_patches` (booleans) and `context_window`, `max_output_tokens` and `min_wait_ms` (positive whole numbers), validated like the finish levers and kept in the session descriptor and its opening key, so a change opens a new runtime. `context_window` becomes `AgentConfiguration.auto_compact_window_tokens` (the codex client's context window, auto-compacting at 90%; default 272,000), and the others travel as `AgentConfiguration.codex_options` (`Contracts.CodexOptions`). `wasm.rs` applies them as `nanocode --compact-instructions`, `--max-output-tokens`, `--lean-tool-arguments`, `--lenient-patches` and `--min-wait-ms` do: `compact_instructions` and `max_output_tokens` only for a model nanocode does not know (`Model::Custom`, such as a Workers AI `@cf/` model, whose chat request then carries `max_completion_tokens`), `compact_instructions` only without host `instructions`, and `lenient_patches` and `min_wait_ms` as the codex tools' `ToolSettings` (nanocode 87e2bb8ff), which ride on the remote tool context, so `apply_patch` and `exec_command` honor them where they run: on the hand for a hand-placed cell, which needs a `nanocode hand` built from 87e2bb8ff or later (an older hand ignores them), or in the Worker. Claude sessions get default tool settings. Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run check:types`; nanocode `cargo check --locked --lib` native and `--target wasm32-unknown-unknown` and `cargo clippy` for nanocode-tool-contracts, nanocode-tool-host, nanocode-code-mode and nanocode-backend-codex.
- feat(vercel): forward mcp.failureMs from Harness.create (2026-11-16, tsopt2 lane, 2b7a848). `Harness.create` passed only `mcp.store` to the runtime, so a Vercel host could not set the `RuntimeOptions.mcp.failureMs` policy that d7b95d5 added. It now forwards `failureMs` with `store`. In a durable-vercel production build (Node 24, mock gateway, two MCP servers whose `initialize` fails with `fetch failed` in 650 to 940 ms), replaying the failure took the warm first event from 786 to 807 ms to 77 to 81 ms (one run of three turns, chunk patched to the d7b95d5 logic). Check: `npm run check:types`.
- Pin nanocode d5c9560b3 (2026-11-16, mmaproot2). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 95291a918 to d5c9560b3, which adds `ClaudeClient::with_root_region` and `prefetch_root_region` (default off). A host that stores the root request region and prefetches it before the turn log opens reaches the first root call after a 100K-child restart in 93.9 ms median (p95 123.0 ms, N=5) instead of 8.97 s. Hosts that set no region are unchanged. Checks: `cargo +1.97.1 metadata`, `npm run check:types`.
- Pin nanocode 4ed9a56b2 (2026-11-16, exactly-once on restart). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode d5c9560b3 to 4ed9a56b2, in which a restored remote child keeps its session id instead of a respawn under a new one, and `Registry::persist_durably` flushes the subagent store before a receipt answers durable. Measured with a mock provider: a 100K-child flat run killed at 25 and 50 percent reruns 0 of 100,000 tasks, runs no task twice and calls no acknowledged child again (50,004 acknowledged, all present). The 2K workerd crash gate with `remoteSubagents` passes spawn, turn and twice with lost 0 and 0 repeats, and done ends with lost 0 and 0 child calls after the restart. With `remoteCoordinators`, a restart still repeats leaves that were done before the kill. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `cargo +1.97.1 metadata`.
- Pin nanocode 92c9c22fa (2026-11-16, image lane). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 4ed9a56b2 to 92c9c22fa, which splits `ClaudeClient::prefetch_root_region` into `arm_root_region` (checks the region magic, length and checksum, decodes it and builds the auth headers) and `ArmedRoot::fire` (polls the post once inline, so the request is written before the host opens the turn log). `root_region_checksum` reads the region checksum from its header. The root request clears its region before it records the completion, so a crash between the two leaves no region to prefetch. Hosts that set no region are unchanged. Checks: `cargo +1.97.1 metadata`, `npm run check:types`.
- Pin nanocode 8d6382902 (2026-11-16, nanocode 1.0 durability track: standby). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 92c9c22fa to 8d6382902. That range adds the coordinator_leaf_rerun sim invariant and makes `Follower::advance` warm each owner that a deferred `Record::Forget` names, so a standby claim reads no cold owner state. Native, standby on one core: claim to writable log is 35.9 ms (95% CI 26.5 to 45.3, n=3) at 100K agents and 2.4 ms (1.3 to 41.1, n=4) at 10K. Checks: `cargo +1.97.1 metadata`, `npm run check:types`.
- Pin nanocode 7a46f58d1 (2026-11-16, nanocode 1.0 durability track: restart). `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 8d6382902 to 7a46f58d1, which adds `nanocode_turnlog::FileLog`, an opt-in turn log host with a fixed two-slot header, a seq index and a load that reads only the records from the newest snapshot onward. No host here uses it yet, so behavior does not change. Native, one core, on a converted 100K-agent root log of 397 MB: the turn log open reads 28.9 MB in 24 reads instead of the whole file, and the log open before it reads only the 1.8 MB root region. The first root call fires from the armed root region 4.6 to 10.8 ms after main at 10K and 100K. Checks: `cargo +1.97.1 metadata`, `npm run check:types`.
- Cloudflare chat shows images that `image()` cells return (2026-11-16). Durable Objects keep large tool-result images in the session's blob store, and the SDK sends them to the page as `nanocode-blob:<type>;b3:<hash>` sources. `imageSource` and the tool-result image scan only took http, data and workspace sources, so on cloudflare.nanocode.xyz `image(await tools.Read({ file_path: '/workspace/sine_wave.png' }))` completed while the chat showed nothing (reproduced 2026-11-16 with the homepage sine wave prompt on build-fef436d: the `exec` result carried a `source.type: url` block with `nanocode-blob:image/png;b3:e67fa32d…`). The Cloudflare app now answers `GET /sessions/<id>/blobs/<key>` from that store with the same session authority as `files`, `Transport.Files` gains an optional `blob(url)` the Cloudflare transport implements, and `Image` reads a `nanocode-blob:image/*` source through it the way it reads a workspace file. A transport without `blob` shows the image's label as a failed image.

## 2026-11-16: a session may name omit_media_on_empty_cut

A session field `omit_media_on_empty_cut: true` (one more of `finishLevers`) passes to `ClaudeBuilder::omit_media_on_empty_cut` (nanocode PR 1195): on a reply cut at its output limit with at most 1 output token, PDF documents are replaced with text as images already were, and a cut that removed media does not spend the 3-continuation budget. Two Haiku 5.5 eval attempts (financial-document-processor, deployed run 0f032a59 a00009 and a00036) failed with "output token limit exhausted after 3 continuations" while a read PDF stayed in every retry. Off by default: a session without the field sends the same bytes. `sdk-revision.json` moves from nanocode 8ec3b215e to 55b597835, the merge of PR 1195 on master, which keeps `stall_nudge`.

## 2026-11-16: bench/sla/threeway.ts drives nanocode, Claude Code and Codex through one fixture

`bench/sla/threeway.ts` runs `bench/sla/run.ts` once per round with `--samples=1` and rotates the lane order every round (ABC, BCA, CAB...), so each round is one fresh fixture process, one TUI session per CLI, three turns each, at a fixed provider TTFT (300 ms) and 200 tokens. The fixture serves Messages (nanocode `nc-claude`, Claude Code) and Responses (Codex) with the same timing code, so the client is the only variable. It appends one JSONL line per sample (round, arm, session, turn, metric, value, wall start, per-arm build string and one matched-config string) for clustered paired bootstrap CIs. Smoke run on a shared host at load 160-196 (two rounds, `--lanes=nc-claude,claude,codex,nc`, nanocode 0.7.0 at 1f167e0b, Claude Code 2.1.325, Codex 0.169.0): both nanocode lanes completed all turns; Claude Code missed its first turn's token within the 20 s window; Codex 0.169.0 never matched the `codex` lane's ready marker `bench/model` (its idle screen shows `› Ask Codex to do anything`). Its timings are not results. Command: `NC_BIN=... CLAUDE_BIN=... CODEX_BIN=... node bench/sla/threeway.ts --rounds=30 --lanes=nc-claude,claude,codex --out=output/bench/sla/threeway`.
- RustPython's on-demand filesystem prefetches a directory tree once the script walks it (2026-11-16, perf(python), prefetch lane). Without a synchronous filesystem (`SqlFileSystem`, Cloudflare's workspace), `python3` runs RustPython against `RustPython.layered`, a cache that answers from what it holds and stops the run at the first path it lacks, then fetches that path and reruns the script. `os.walk` over a tree therefore reran once per directory and `open()` in a loop once per file, each rerun walking everything again. `layered` (moved from `python.ts` into `rustpython.ts`) now widens each fetch: a listing miss whose parent the run already listed prefetches that directory's whole tree of listings and stats (the parent's tree once a sibling was listed too), level by level with the lists and stats of a level issued together; a second read miss in one directory reads every file there, and after two such directories the rest of the prefetched tree; a rename whose source subtree is missing prefetches it and reads its files together, and a rename onto a new path no longer stats every destination path (it was one rerun per copied file). Memory is the host's `Limits.maxMemoryBytes`: the cache counts the bytes of the paths, names and contents it holds; a speculative prefetch that would pass the budget is skipped (the run continues fetching on demand), and needed data past it ends the run with `the N paths and M files this Python run used needs about X MB, which exceeds this host's memory budget of Y MB` (exit 126). Measured 2026-11-16 in Node 24 with `output/walk.ts` (a wevm/viem clone, 3,266 files, 273 directories, 66 MB, written into a `SqlFileSystem` over `node:sqlite` `:memory:`; python.wasm from build-aa3daa2; before = this commit's parent and after, interleaved twice on the shared Hand, so times are noisy and runs exact): `os.walk` + count 1,286-2,653 ms and 84 runs -> 128-203 ms and 4 runs; `pathlib.rglob('*.ts')` 3,333-4,531 ms / 107 -> 177-240 ms / 4; `glob('**/*.ts', recursive=True)` 3,302-5,527 ms / 133 -> 117-136 ms / 4; recursive `os.scandir` 3,714-4,953 ms / 275 -> 100-129 ms / 5; walk and read 375 files of `src/utils` (3.2 MB) 4,265-5,017 ms / 382 -> 85-198 ms / 9; makedirs + write + rename `src/actions` + remove + walk 9,380-10,171 ms / 384 runs / 102,991 SQL transactions -> 325-432 ms / 10 / 4,119; walk and read all 3,266 files 264-276 s / 3,336 runs -> 501-765 ms / 9 runs. Every output matches. Listing SQL transactions stay one per directory (275 for the walk). `os.listdir` of one directory and `print(1)` are unchanged (3 runs and 1 run). With `maxMemoryBytes` at 57 MB, reading all 66 MB ends with the budget message above after 472 runs. The interpreter (nanocode `crates/core/python`) needs no change: it already stops at the host's miss and the host decides what to fetch.
- A collapsed tool cell shows its images (2026-11-16). `ToolCell` drew a cell's images only while its body was shown, so in a `collapsed` transcript (the Claude clone's tool rows) the chart an `image(await tools.Read(...))` cell returned stayed hidden until the visitor opened the row, while the model, which saw the image, answered as though it was on screen ("shown above"). A top-level cell now draws its images whether or not its body is open; a nested cell still draws them only when open. The model-facing guidance is upstream nanocode's `exec` description ("To show an image file, such as a chart a script saved, pass what reading it returns: `image(await tools.Read({ file_path }))`"), which both demos send unchanged, so no host instruction is needed. Measured on 2026-11-16 with Claude Haiku 5.5 and the prompt "Plot a sine wave with matplotlib and save it as a PNG", three turns each against cloudflare.nanocode.xyz and nanocode-vercel-durable.vercel.app, with the logged events replayed through `Transcript.create` and `renderToStaticMarkup` in `output/`: 2 of 6 turns called `image()` (one per host; the Cloudflare one carries a `nanocode-blob:image/png` source, the Vercel one a `data:image/png` source), and their `<ToolCell collapsed>` render held 0 `data-nc="image"` elements before and 1 after; the expanded render held 1 both times. Check: `npm run check:types`.
- Pin nanocode 9b94e982e for python.wasm (2026-11-16, perf(python), embedded Python round 3). `python-revision.json` moves from nanocode 68b61cc19 to 9b94e982e, which takes kiwisolver._cext as a built-in Rust module (db0bbe034), imports from `sys.prefix/lib/python3.14/site-packages` (36eca719c), matplotlib._c_internal_utils as a built-in Rust module (4c53666de), extension sources and include directories listed in per-extension `sources` and `includes` files compiled in parallel (e2671fbba, c471ca8d5), the standard C library declared in the wasm libc headers (6b5e02278) and list slice assignment, attribute deletion, in-place number slots, the traceback type and fatal errors in the C API (9b94e982e). `python/Cargo.lock` gains nanocode-sys's and psh-utils' djs-libm dependency (`cargo update --workspace`, no versions changed). Measured on an Apple M-series Mac with Homebrew LLVM: `scripts/python.mjs` builds python.wasm in 2 min 49 s; `npm run build` gives dist/python.wasm 29,098,510 bytes. Through `Bash.create({ python: { module } })` in Node 24: `kiwisolver.Solver` solves `x >= 3` to 3.0 (1,610 ms for the first run, which includes compiling the module), markupsafe, regex, msgpack, ujson and yaml with `__with_libyaml__` True import (131 ms), and `sys.path` ends with `/usr/local/lib/python3.14/site-packages`.
- bench/python/score.ts checks numpy (2026-11-16, feat(python), Georgios: numpy works in python.wasm through nanocode's C API). The scoreboard prints `numpy`, the count of 14 numpy expressions whose str() in python.wasm equals what CPython 3.12 with numpy 2.5.5 prints: linspace, sin, an axis reduction, broadcasting, float-to-int and string-to-float casts, linalg.solve/det/inv/eig, random.default_rng(0).normal, fft.fft, unique and np.char.upper. The expected strings were taken from native CPython. Against python.wasm built through scripts/python.mjs from nanocode 07aef375c (numpy compiled in and its Python package frozen; release wasm before wasm-opt 31,291,617 bytes), `CAPI=<dir with an empty x/> node --experimental-strip-types bench/python/score.ts <that wasm>` gives numpy 14/14; the pinned nanocode 9b94e982e has no numpy, so it scores 0/14 until the pin moves.
- wasm-opt runs with `--enable-exception-handling` (2026-11-16, fix(build), embedded Python round 3). nanocode 07aef375c compiles C++ extensions into python.wasm with `-fwasm-exceptions` (nanocode be6232f88), so python.wasm carries `try`, `throw` and a tag, and `node scripts/build.mjs` stopped at `[wasm-validator error] try requires exception-handling` / `Fatal: error validating input`. Enabling the feature for every module lets wasm-opt read them; modules without exception instructions build unchanged. Measured on an Apple M-series Mac: `node scripts/build.mjs` with python-revision 07aef375c finishes in 1 min 29 s and writes dist/python.wasm at 37,304,929 bytes (29,097,809 at 9b94e982e, so +8,207,120 with numpy and the C++ runtime); through `Bash.create({ python: { module } })` in Node 24, `import numpy` is 2.5.5 from python.wasm (137 ms first run, 1 ms after), and kiwisolver, yaml with libyaml, regex, msgpack, ujson and markupsafe still import.
- Pin nanocode 82db76563 for python.wasm (2026-11-16, feat(python), embedded Python round 3). `python-revision.json` moves from nanocode 9b94e982e to 82db76563, which compiles C++ extensions with libc++ and wasm exceptions (be6232f88, 314d30d14), adds a musl libc subset for wasm32 extensions (793361c89, 80412a7), puts numpy 2.5.5 in python3 with its pure-Python package frozen (07aef375c, 369d23c3d) and ends a run whose import check stopped instead of panicking (82db76563). Measured on an Apple M-series Mac with Homebrew LLVM: `node scripts/build.mjs` takes 4 min 9 s; dist/python.wasm is 37,271,217 bytes (15,022,941 with gzip -9), against 29,097,809 at 9b94e982e. Through `Bash.create({ python: { module } })` in Node 24: `import numpy` gives 2.5.5 in 106 ms on the first run and 1 ms after, with no warnings; linspace/sin, linalg solve and det, `default_rng(0).normal`, fft and reductions print what CPython numpy 2.5.5 prints; kiwisolver, yaml with libyaml, regex, msgpack, ujson and markupsafe still import; `import matplotlib` and `import scipy` exit 0 through the Pyodide fallback.
- RustPython on an async filesystem answers absent paths from listings it holds, and loads python.wasm under Turbopack (2026-11-17, fix(python), Georgios: numpy on durable-cloudflare and RustPython on durable-vercel). Two fixes, measured with the new `bench/python/asyncfs.ts` (Node 24: seven probes, each in a fresh `Bash` over a MemoryFileSystem behind a Proxy that hides `sync` and counts calls, then again in one warm shell, then the same over the sync MemoryFileSystem; exit 1 on any mismatch; `node bench/python/asyncfs.ts <python.wasm>`). (1) `RustPython.layered` stopped the run at every path it had not stat'ed, so each module probe (`/workspace/numpy`, `/workspace/ctypes/__init__.py`, site-packages) was a rerun, and the rerun of an import cut partway broke numpy (`AttributeError: partially initialized module 'numpy' has no attribute 'isfinite'`, `default_rng` NameError, linalg RecursionError; nanocode f22e4901c fixes the interpreter side). A stat now answers "absent" without a rerun when the nearest ancestor the cache listed does not name the next step, or an ancestor is a file; each stat fetch also lists the missed paths' parents; and `executeRust` lists cwd and `/usr/local/lib/python3.14/site-packages` (the interpreter's `sys.path`) before the first attempt, which is one stat and one list each and skipped past the memory budget like any prefetch. (2) On Vercel every `python3` ran Pyodide: Turbopack rewrites `import.meta` to an object with only `url`, so `rustModule` found no `import.meta.resolve` and returned no module. It now falls back to `new URL('../../../python.wasm', import.meta.url)` (dist/python.wasm from dist/bash/internal/commands), which Turbopack emits as a traced server asset; a runtime with neither `resolve` nor `url` still gets no module. Cloudflare passes its module, so it is unchanged. Results on the async filesystem (cold ms, host stats): with build-67bdee0's python.wasm and cache, numpy array, default_rng and linalg fail (266, 186 and 323 ms; 25 to 32 stats); with this cache and nanocode f22e4901c all 7 pass: array 232 ms, default_rng 209 ms, linalg 108 ms, 9 stats and 1 list each, `import nosuchmodule_xyz` 36 ms, and matplotlib and scipy, which go through the Pyodide fallback, pass too. The cache change alone (old python.wasm) still fails numpy, and nanocode f22e4901c alone (old cache) passes 7 of 7 with 13 stats. Sync filesystem results are unchanged. durable-vercel built with `next build` (16.4.6) and served by `next start`, with a probe route making `Bash.create({ fs: <async>, python: { mirror } })`: `sys.implementation.name` is cpython before and rustpython after, and numpy prints arrays, `default_rng(0).integers` and `linalg.det` (213 ms first call). `python-revision.json` moves from nanocode 82db76563 to f22e4901c. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `vp fmt` on the changed files.
- bench/python/score.ts checks matplotlib, Pillow, contourpy and kiwisolver (2026-11-17, feat(python), Georgios: matplotlib in python.wasm without Pyodide). The scoreboard prints `plots`, per package the count of 15 expressions whose str() in python.wasm equals what CPython 3.13 prints with the same wheels (matplotlib 3.11.2, Pillow 13.0.0, contourpy 1.4.0, kiwisolver 1.5.0, numpy 2.5.5): matplotlib 6 (Path.contains_point, get_extents, an Affine2D rotation, a Triangulation, and sha256 of the Agg RGBA buffer of a sine plot and of a contourf plus imshow), Pillow 6 (a pixel, GaussianBlur, ImageMath.lambda_eval, ImageMorph erosion, sha256 of a PNG Pillow writes, and reading it back), contourpy 2 (lines, filled) and kiwisolver 1 (a two-constraint solve). Matplotlib needs its mpl-data and its pure-Python requirements, so the check copies the four wheels' non-compiled files and `$CAPI/deps` (cycler, dateutil, fontTools, packaging, pyparsing, six) into /site, and the import row for matplotlib now gets the same files. Against python.wasm built through scripts/python.mjs from nanocode 1bdf9f1cb (release wasm before wasm-opt 35,758,261 bytes), `CAPI=<dir> node bench/python/score.ts <that wasm>` gives plots matplotlib 6/6, pillow 6/6, contourpy 2/2, kiwisolver 1/1, numpy 14/14, limits 4/4 and packages 7/18 (about 3 minutes): matplotlib imports 7 of its 8 extensions (backends._tkagg is not built) and Pillow 3 of 8 (_imagingft, _imagingcms, _webp, _avif and _imagingtk are not built), so neither counts as a package yet; contourpy and kiwisolver count. The PNG hash equals native CPython's, so Pillow's zlib-only PNG encoder writes the same bytes in wasm. The pinned nanocode f22e4901c has no matplotlib, so it scores 0/6 matplotlib until the pin moves.
- A Vercel drive holds an open `never` effect while it drives a turn (2026-11-17, fix(vercel), preempt lane). durable-harness decides whether an arriving drive may take a session over from the open effects in the log: an open record whose anchor is live is `covered`, and the arrival logs `skip: open work is held elsewhere` and leaves. A nanocode turn has no open effect while the model streams or a cell's nested call runs outside the log (the effects are the session open and the `nanocode.sql` writes), so a second drive (a second request or a warm signal) recovered, fenced the epoch, and the first drive stopped with `yield: superseded` with its cell's nested call in flight; the drive that took over replayed the cell and answered that call with "This nested call was running when its cell moved to another host; its outcome is unknown". `Harness.create` now opens one `nanocode.hold:<uuid>` effect (`replay: 'never'`) before it drives the first turn or waits for an arrival, and resolves it when the drive ends or its signal aborts, so an arrival during the turn finds covered open work and the running drive takes the new message through `changed`. Repro: two processes on one local world (`WORKFLOW_LOCAL_DATA_DIR`), a mock gateway whose first reply is one `exec` cell running three `git clone --depth 1` through `tools.Bash`, process a starting the turn and process b arriving with a warm signal 4 s later (`scripts/moved2.ts a|b` in a durable-vercel checkout on nanocode-ts 241889c, durable-harness from npm), interleaved base/hold over 8 rounds by swapping `dist/vercel/Harness.js` for the `tsc -p tsconfig.build.json` output: base 7 of 8 rounds return moved cells (10 in all) and the first drive is superseded in 5; hold 0 of 8 and 0, with b's later drive logging `skip: open work is held elsewhere` on the hold key in all 8. b's own reply waits for a's turn instead of replacing it: 13.4 to 16.5 s against 5.7 to 14.9 s. A b drive that arms before a's hold is written still loses at the in-band fence (expected 3, seqInBand 4) without stopping a. Checks: `npm run check:types`, `vp fmt` and `vp lint` on the changed file.
- fix(rustpython): read python.wasm's request and reply buffers at unsigned offsets (2026-11-17, mpltest lane). python_input() and python_output() return wasm i32 pointers, which JS reads as negative once the interpreter's memory passes 2 GiB, so the run failed with "Start offset -1946475904 is outside the bounds of the buffer" and the interpreter restarted. Both now go through \`>>> 0\`, as the file-read path already did. matplotlib's test_axes (925 tests) in python.wasm at nanocode c928a0c69 went from that failure to 865 passed, 2 failed, 58 skipped (CPython 3.13 with the matplotlib 3.11.2 wheel: 890 passed, 35 skipped; both failures are the embedded fontTools lacking fontTools.subset for PDF output); \`npx tsc --noEmit\` passes.

## 2026-11-16: python3 runs only on nanocode-python; pip installs pure-Python wheels

Georgios (2026-11-16): python3 in nanocode runs only on our RustPython-based interpreter, with no Pyodide fallback anywhere. This removes Pyodide: `src/bash/internal/commands/pylazy.ts`, `pyserver.ts`, `pysnapshot.ts`, `pyworker.ts`, `src/cloudflare/python.ts` (the `nanocode-ts/cloudflare/python` Durable Object export) with `src/cloudflare/internal/Pyodide.ts`, `scripts/pyodide.mjs` (the memory-snapshot build), the `./pyodide-modules` export, the `pyodide` peer and dev dependency, `Python.Remote`/`Python.Reply` and the Pyodide fields of `Python.Options` (`remote`, `mirror`, `url`, `packages`, `preload`), `workspace.python` in `App.create` and the fallback in `python.ts` that handed a run to Pyodide when RustPython could not import a package. 3,935 lines go. Release build-aa3daa2 carried `dist/pyodide` at 120,050,775 bytes unpacked (92,139,653 of them the snapshot) of its 173,717,391-byte `dist`, and its tarball is 58,279,275 bytes; the `pyodide` npm package was 13,879,282 bytes in `node_modules`.

A module that is not in python.wasm now fails with one line, such as `python3: No module named 'scipy' (nanocode's Python ships contourpy, kiwisolver, markupsafe, matplotlib, msgpack, numpy, PIL, yaml, regex, ujson; pure-Python packages: pip install scipy)`, exit 127. A host that cannot load python.wasm gets `python3 needs nanocode-ts's python.wasm`, exit 126.

pip: `pip`, `pip3` and `python3 -m pip` run `src/bash/internal/commands/pip.ts`, an installer for pure-Python wheels only. It reads PyPI's JSON API (`https://pypi.org/pypi/<name>/json`), picks the newest non-yanked release that meets the specifiers (stable unless `--pre`), takes its `py3-none-any` wheel whose `Requires-Python` admits 3.14, unzips it with the shell's zip reader into `/usr/local/lib/python3.14/site-packages` (nanocode 36eca71 puts that directory on `sys.path`), writes `INSTALLER` and `REQUESTED` beside the wheel's `RECORD`, and follows `Requires-Dist` with environment markers evaluated for RustPython on wasm32 (`sys_platform` linux, `platform_machine` wasm32). Packages that ship with python.wasm count as satisfied. A release with only an sdist or compiled wheels, or a wheel holding `.so`/`.pyd` files, is refused with the tags it has and the compiled modules python.wasm ships; there is no source build, because python.wasm cannot load compiled code that is not linked into it. `uninstall` removes the files in `RECORD`; `list`, `freeze` and `show` read `.dist-info/METADATA`. Alternatives: keeping micropip needs Pyodide; installing sdists would need a build backend running in RustPython and still cannot build C.

Measured on the hosted Hand with release build-aa3daa2's python.wasm in this branch's `dist`: `pip install six attrs idna packaging` installs four wheels in about 1 s and they import; `pip install numpy` reports it ships; `pip install pandas==2.2.3` is refused with its compiled wheel tags; `pip install nosuchpkg-xyz-123` fails `not on PyPI`; `pip uninstall -y six` then `import six` gives the line above. `import requests` still fails on `socket` and `importlib.metadata` on `email`, which python.wasm does not carry. Checked with `npm run check:types` and `npm run build:ts`; durable-cloudflare's branch passes `npm run check` and `wrangler deploy --dry-run`, and durable-vercel's `npm run check`.

pip also takes a CPython 3 x86_64 Linux wheel (newest `cp3N` up to 3.14, `abi3` included) when a release has no pure wheel: it lists python3's `sys.builtin_module_names` and `_imp._frozen_module_names()` once per install, installs the wheel's Python and data files and leaves out its `.so` files and `<name>.libs/` when every compiled module in it (`matplotlib/_path.cpython-314-x86_64-linux-gnu.so` is `matplotlib._path`) is built into python.wasm, and otherwise refuses with the modules python.wasm lacks. A requirement whose name is a module python.wasm carries itself (msgpack in build-aa3daa2) is satisfied without a download. With build-aa3daa2's python.wasm: `pip install pyyaml markupsafe ujson regex` installs their cp314 manylinux wheels without the `.so` files and `import yaml, regex, ujson` then work through the built-in C modules (`yaml.__with_libyaml__` True); `pip install kiwisolver` is refused for `kiwisolver._cext`, and `pip install pandas` lists its 44 `pandas._libs` modules. This is how matplotlib's Python, mpl-data, pyparsing, dateutil, cycler, fonttools and pillow's Python parts can install once python.wasm carries their C modules.
